「那我的银器不再给你们添堵了。」我气派十足地说。「我本打算它若安然无恙就留在贵行，可听了你这一番话，我断然不留了。叫你的人——一个也罢、几个也罢——立刻把箱子抬上来。谅他们也是『一下午都在跟旁人掰扯』来着，不过我会让这一趟不白辛苦。」

===Original===
I did not mind driving through the streets with the thing this time. My present relief was too overwhelming as yet to admit of pangs and fears for the immediate future. No summer sun had ever shone more brightly than that rather watery one of early April. There was a green-and-gold dust of buds and shoots on the trees as we passed the park. I felt greater things sprouting in my heart. Hansoms passed with schoolboys just home for the Easter holidays, four-wheelers outward bound, with bicycles and perambulators atop; none that rode in them were half so happy as I, with the great load on my cab, but the greater one off my heart.
===Chinese===
这一回，我倒不在乎载着那玩意儿穿街过巷了。眼下的如释重负太过于汹涌，还容不下对眼面前前途的刺痛与忧惧。四月初那轮多少带着水汽的太阳，比哪个夏日的太阳照得都亮。经过公园时，树梢蒙着一层新芽嫩枝织就的金绿粉尘。我觉得更了不起的东西正在我心里抽芽。双轮马车载着刚回家过复活节假期的学童驶过，出城的四轮马车顶着自行车和婴儿车驶过；车中诸君，没有一个有我一半快活——尽管我的车顶压着千钧重载，我心中卸下的却更重。

===Original===
At Mount Street it just went into the lift; that was a stroke of luck; and the lift-man and I between us carried it into my flat. It seemed a featherweight to me now. I felt a Samson in the exaltation of that hour. And I will not say what my first act was when I found myself alone with my white elephant in the middle of the room; enough that the siphon was still doing its work when the glass slipped through my fingers to the floor.
===Chinese===
到了芒特街（Mount Street），它恰好塞得进电梯——算是一桩走运；电梯工和我两人合力，把它抬进了我的公寓。此刻它在我手里轻若鸿毛。在那时刻的高扬里，我自觉是一员参孙（Samson）。至于独自与我的「白象」同处一室、站在屋子正中时，我的头一件事是什么，我就不说了；只说一句：玻璃杯从我指间滑落、摔在地板上的时候，虹吸苏水瓶还在履行它的职责。

===Original===
“Bunny!”

It was Raffles. Yet for a moment I looked about me quite in vain. He was not at the window; he was not at the open door. And yet Raffles it had been, or at all events his voice, and that bubbling over with fun and satisfaction, be his body where it might. In the end I dropped my eyes, and there was his living face in the middle of the lid of the chest, like that of the saint upon its charger.
===Chinese===
「邦尼！」

是拉夫尔斯。可一时间我环顾四周，竟遍寻不见。他不在窗前，也不在敞开的门口。然而那确凿是拉夫尔斯——至少是他的声音，而且那声音欢畅得意得直往外冒泡，不管他的身子在何处。末了我垂下眼睛：箱盖正中央，赫然现出他一张活生生的脸，恰似圣徒的头颅映在盘中。

===Original===
But Raffles was alive, Raffles was laughing as though his vocal cords would snap﻿—there was neither tragedy nor illusion in the apparition of Raffles. A life-size jack-in-the-box, he had thrust his head through a lid within the lid, cut by himself between the two iron bands that ran round the chest like the straps of a portmanteau. He must have been busy at it when I found him pretending to pack, if not far into that night, for it was a very perfect piece of work; and even as I stared without a word, and he crouched laughing in my face, an arm came squeezing out, keys in hand; one was turned in either of the two great padlocks, the whole lid lifted, and out stepped Raffles like the conjurer he was.
===Chinese===
可拉夫尔斯活着，拉夫尔斯笑着，笑得声带仿佛都要绷断——拉夫尔斯的这次显形，既无惨剧，亦无幻象。原来是一具真人大小的玩偶匣（jack-in-the-box）：环绕箱身的两道铁箍，恰似行李箱上的束带，他便在两箍之间亲手锯开了一扇盖中之盖，把自己的脑袋从里头钻了出来。我撞见他假装收拾行装那会儿，他八成正埋头赶这桩活计，若不是一直干到深夜，也差相仿佛——那活计做得着实漂亮；就在我瞪目结舌、他蹲在里头冲着我的脸大笑的当儿，一条胳膊已挤了出来，手里攥着钥匙；两把大挂锁各转了一转，整块箱盖掀起，拉夫尔斯一迈步跨了出来，活脱脱是他本行的戏法大师。

===Original===
“So you were the burglar!” I exclaimed at last. “Well, I am just as glad I didn’t know.”

He had wrung my hand already, but at this he fairly mangled it in his.

“You dear little brick,” he cried, “that’s the one thing of all things I longed to hear you say! How could you have behaved as you’ve done if you had known? How could any living man? How could you have acted, as the polar star of all the stages could not have acted in your place? Remember that I have heard a lot, and as good as seen as much as I’ve heard. Bunny, I don’t know where you were greatest: at the Albany, here, or at your bank!”
===Chinese===
「原来窃贼就是你！」我终于喊出声来。「唉，幸亏我先前不知道。」

他早已紧握过我的手；听到这一句，简直把我的手在他掌中揉了个稀烂。

「你这可爱的小好汉，」他嚷道，「万千句话里，我顶想听的正是这一句！你若早知道了，还能有你这一路的做派吗？哪个活人能？你这一举一动，便是全舞台的北极星亲自来替你，也演不出来！别忘了，我耳闻了许多，耳闻的又几乎等于眼见。邦尼，我说不清你哪一段最见本事：在奥尔巴尼，在这儿，还是在你的银行！」

===Original===
“I don’t know where I was most miserable,” I rejoined, beginning to see the matter in a less perfervid light. “I know you don’t credit me with much finesse, but I would undertake to be in the secret and to do quite as well; the only difference would be in my own peace of mind, which, of course, doesn’t count.”

But Raffles wagged away with his most charming and disarming smile; he was in old clothes, rather tattered and torn, and more than a little grimy as to the face and hands, but, on the surface, wonderfully little the worse for his experience. And, as I say, his smile was the smile of the Raffles I loved best.
===Chinese===
「我也说不清哪一段最叫我受罪。」我应道，开始以不那么滚烫的眼光看待这桩事。「我知道你不信我有多少心机；可我敢打保票，让我参与秘密，我照样办得一样漂亮；分别只在自个儿心里的太平——那自然是不值一提的。」

可拉夫尔斯只是摇着头，满面那最迷人、最卸人武装的笑；他一身旧衣，颇有些褴褛，脸上手上也带着不止一点污垢，但就表面而论，这场经历几乎没在他身上留下什么损伤。而且，如我所说，他脸上的笑，正是我最爱的那个拉夫尔斯的笑。

===Original===
“You would have done your damnedest, Bunny! There is no limit to your heroism; but you forget the human equation in the pluckiest of the plucky. I couldn’t afford to forget it, Bunny; I couldn’t afford to give a point away. Don’t talk as though I hadn’t trusted you! I trusted my very life to your loyal tenacity. What do you suppose would have happened to me if you had let me rip in that strongroom? Do you think I would ever have crept out and given myself up? Yes, I’ll have a peg for once; the beauty of all laws is in the breaking, even of the kind we make unto ourselves.”
===Chinese===
「你会拼命到死，邦尼！你的英勇没有极限；可你忘了，再剽悍的人里头也有个『人情常数』。我却忘不起，邦尼；我一分底也泄露不得。别说得好像我不曾信任你似的！我把我这条性命，都信托给了你的忠贞不渝。想想看，你在那间保险库里若由着我自生自灭，我会落得什么下场？你以为我还会爬出去自首吗？——对了，这回我要破例来上一杯；一切法律之美，尽在违犯——连我们给自己立的那一种，也不例外。」

===Original===
I had a Sullivan for him, too; and in another minute he was spread out on my sofa, stretching his cramped limbs with infinite gusto, a cigarette between his fingers, a yellow bumper at hand on the chest of his triumph and my tribulation.

“Never mind when it occurred to me, Bunny; as a matter of fact, it was only the other day, when I had decided to go away for the real reasons I have already given you. I may have made more of them to you than I do in my own mind, but at all events they exist. And I really did want the telephone and the electric light.”
===Chinese===
我也给他备下一支沙利文（Sullivan）；不出一分钟，他已摊手摊脚躺上我的沙发，无限受用地伸展着蜷麻了的四肢，指间夹着香烟，手边——他那胜利与我的磨难之箱上——搁着一杯满斟的黄澄澄的美酒。

「几时起的念头就不必细究了，邦尼；实话说，也就是前几天，等我出于已经告诉过你的那些真正缘由、定下出门之计以后。这些缘由，我对你说得兴许比我自己心里掂量的还重些，但无论如何，它们是存在的。再说，电话和电灯，我也确乎是真心想要的。」

===Original===
“But where did you stow the silver before you went?”

“Nowhere; it was my luggage﻿—a portmanteau, cricket-bag, and suitcase full of very little else﻿—and by the same token I left the lot at Euston, and one of us must fetch them this evening.”
===Chinese===
「可你走之前，把那些银器藏到哪儿去了？」

「哪儿也没藏；那就是我的行李——一只旅行提箱、一条板球袋、一只手提箱，里头装的多半不是别的正是它们——而且顺带一说，那一整堆我都撂在尤斯顿了，今晚咱们总得有一个人去取回来。」

===Original===
“I can do that,” said I. “But did you really go all the way to Crewe?”

“Didn’t you get my note? I went all the way to Crewe to post you those few lines, my dear Bunny! It’s no use taking trouble if you don’t take trouble enough; I wanted you to show the proper set of faces at the bank and elsewhere, and I know you did. Besides, there was an up-train four minutes after mine got in. I simply posted my letter in Crewe station, and changed from one train to the other.”
===Chinese===
「我去就行。」我说。「不过你当真坐到了克鲁？」

「没收到我的条子吗？我一路坐到克鲁，就为给你寄那几行字，亲爱的邦尼！功夫不下到十分，便等于白下；我要的，正是你在银行等地摆出恰如其分的各副面孔，而我知道你摆出来了。再说，我那班车进站四分钟之后，就有一班上行的车。我干脆在克鲁站里寄了信，从那一班车换到了这一班。」

===Original===
“At two in the morning!”

“Nearer three, Bunny. It was after seven when I slung in with the *Daily Mail*. The milk had beaten me by a short can. But even so I had two very good hours before you were due.”

“And to think,” I murmured, “how you deceived me there!”

“With your own assistance,” said Raffles laughing. “If you had looked it up you would have seen there was no such train in the morning, and I never said there was. But I meant you to be deceived, Bunny, and I won’t say I didn’t﻿—it was all for the sake of the side! Well, when you carted me away with such laudable despatch, I had rather an uncomfortable half-hour, but that was all just then. I had my candle, I had matches, and lots to read. It was quite nice in that strongroom until a very unpleasant incident occurred.”
===Chinese===
「凌晨两点！」

「快三点了吧，邦尼。我拎着《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）溜回来的工夫，都过七点了。送奶的比我早到了一小罐。饶是如此，离你该到的钟点，我还有足足两个好钟头。」

「想想看，」我喃喃道，「在那件事上，你把我骗得那般苦！」

「也有你自己一份力。」拉夫尔斯笑道。「你若查一查，就会查到早上根本没那一班车，而我也从没说过有。不过我的确存心要你受骗，邦尼，这我不否认——全是为了咱们这一队的体面！后来你以那般可嘉的干练把我运走，我着实别扭了半个来钟头，但当时也就仅此而已。我有蜡烛，有火柴，还有的是可读的书。在那间保险库里本也颇为惬意，直到出了一桩很不愉快的事。」

===Original===
“Do tell me, my dear fellow!”

“I must have another Sullivan﻿—thank you﻿—and a match. The unpleasant incident was steps outside and a key in the lock! I was disporting myself on the lid of the trunk at the time. I had barely time to knock out my light and slip down behind it. Luckily it was only another box of sorts; a jewel-case, to be more precise; you shall see the contents in a moment. The Easter exodus has done me even better than I dared to hope.”
===Chinese===
「快讲给我听，亲爱的伙计！」

「得再来一支沙利文——谢谢——再添根火柴。那桩不愉快的事，就是外头响起了脚步声，钥匙插进了锁眼！当时我正在那箱子的盖上舒展筋骨呢。我勉强来得及捻灭烛火，溜到箱子背后蹲下。幸而来的不过是另一只箱子之类的东西；说得确切些，是一只首饰匣；里头是什么，回头你便见着。这趟复活节大迁徙，给我的好处比我敢指望的还大。」

===Original===
His words reminded me of the *Pall Mall Gazette*, which I had brought in my pocket from the Turkish bath. I fished it out, all wrinkled and bloated by the heat of the hottest room, and handed it to Raffles with my thumb upon the leaded paragraphs.

“Delightful!” said he when he had read them. “More thieves than one, and the coal-cellar of all places as a way in! I certainly tried to give it that appearance. I left enough candle-grease there to make those coals burn bravely. But it looked up into a blind backyard, Bunny, and a boy of eight couldn’t have squeezed through the trap. Long may that theory keep them happy at Scotland Yard!”
===Chinese===
他这话提醒了我那份《帕尔摩报》，正是我从土耳其浴场揣在口袋里带出来的。我把它掏了出来，皱皱巴巴，被最热那间房的热气胀得鼓鼓的，拇指按在那几段黑体导语上，递给拉夫尔斯。

他读完，说道：「妙极了！贼倒不止一个，进门的路偏偏选了煤窖！这副模样确实是我着力妆扮出来的。我留下的蜡烛油，够那些煤烧得旺旺的。可惜那地方抬头望上去是堵死的天井，邦尼，八岁的孩子也钻不过那扇活门。但愿这套理论能让苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）多开心些时日！」

===Original===
“But what about the fellow you knocked out?” I asked. “That was not like you, Raffles.”

Raffles blew pensive rings as he lay back on my sofa, his black hair tumbled on the cushion, his pale profile as clear and sharp against the light as though slashed out with the scissors.
===Chinese===
「可那个被你打倒的人怎么说？」我问。「那可不像你的为人，拉夫尔斯。」

拉夫尔斯仰在我的沙发上，吐着一圈圈悠悠的烟环，乌黑的头发散乱在靠垫上，苍白的侧影衬着亮处，轮廓利落分明，仿佛是用剪刀铰出来的一般。

===Original===
“I know it wasn’t, Bunny,” he said regretfully. “But things like that, as the poet will tell you, are really inseparable from victories like mine. It had taken me a couple of hours to break out of that strongroom; I was devoting a third to the harmless task of simulating the appearance of having broken in; and it was then I heard the fellow’s stealthy step. Some might have stood their ground and killed him; more would have bolted into a worse corner than they were in already. I left my candle where it was, crept to meet the poor devil, flattened myself against the wall, and let him have it as he passed. I acknowledge the foul blow, but here’s evidence that it was mercifully struck. The victim has already told his tale.”
===Chinese===
「我知道不像，邦尼。」他不无懊悔地说。「可是这一类事，正如诗人会告诉你的，同我这样的凯旋本是拆散不开的。我花了个把钟头才从那间保险库里破壁而出；第三个钟头，我正用于一桩无害的功课——布置出『破窗而入』的种种迹象；恰恰在那时，我听见了那家伙蹑手蹑脚的脚步。换作旁人，有的会据守原地，结果了他；更多的会一头扎进比原先更糟的死角。我却把蜡烛留在原地，爬着迎上那可怜虫，把身子贴平在墙上，等他走过时，结结实实给了他一下。这记暗棍我认账；可这里有证据，证明它打得慈悲。受害人不已经把他的故事讲出来了么。」

===Original===
As he drained his glass, but shook his head when I wished to replenish it, Raffles showed me the flask which he had carried in his pocket: it was still nearly full; and I found that he had otherwise provisioned himself over the holidays. On either Easter Day or Bank Holiday, had I failed him, it had been his intention to make the best escape he could. But the risk must have been enormous, and it filled my glowing skin to think that he had not relied on me in vain.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯饮尽杯中酒，却在我要为他续斟时摇了摇头；他给我看那只随身揣在口袋里的扁瓶：还几乎是满的；我还发现，节日期间的口粮他也另有备办。倘若复活节正日或银行假日（Bank Holiday）那天我误了他的事，他本打算尽力独自脱身。可那风险必定大得吓人；想到他终究没有信错我，我那还在发烫的皮肤不由得涨满了感慨。

===Original===
As for his gleanings from such jewel-cases as were spending the Easter recess in the strongroom of my bank, without going into rhapsodies or even particulars on the point, I may mention that they realized enough for me to join Raffles on his deferred holiday in Scotland, besides enabling him to play more regularly for Middlesex in the ensuing summer than had been the case for several seasons. In fine, this particular exploit entirely justified itself in my eyes, in spite of the superfluous (but invariable) secretiveness which I could seldom help resenting in my heart. I never thought less of it than in the present instance; and my one mild reproach was on the subject of the phantom Crawshay.

“You let me think he was in the air again,” I said. “But it wouldn’t surprise me to find that you had never heard of him since the day of his escape through your window.”

“I never even thought of him, Bunny, until you came to see me the day before yesterday, and put him into my head with your first words. The whole point was to make you as genuinely anxious about the plate as you must have seemed all along the line.”
===Chinese===
至于他从那些在我银行保险库里消磨复活节假期的首饰匣中拾掇来的收获，不必绘声绘色，更不必开列细目，只说一句：那笔进项，足够我赶到苏格兰，与拉夫尔斯同享他推迟了的假期；此外还让他得以在接下来的夏天里，比此前几个赛季都更经常地为米德尔塞克斯郡（Middlesex）出赛。总而言之，这一桩妙计，在我眼里全然自己证明了自己——尽管那多余（却从不缺席）的讳莫如深，仍叫我心底难免生出怨望。从没有哪一回像这回一样，我把它看得这样轻；我仅有的一点温和的责备，落在那个凭空捏造的克劳肖身上。

「你由着我以为他又在空气里游荡。」我说。「可若查出打他那天从你窗子里逃走之后，你就再没听到过他半点音讯，我也一点不奇怪。」

「我压根儿没想到过他，邦尼，直到前天你来见我，头一句话就把他塞进了我的脑子。整出戏的关窍，就是要你对那批银器生出真真切切的焦虑——横竖这一路之上，你也都得显得焦虑万分。」

===Original===
“Of course I see your point,” I rejoined; “but mine is that you labored it. You needn’t have written me a downright lie about the fellow.”

“Nor did I, Bunny.”

“Not about the ‘prince of professors’ being ‘in the offing’ when you left?”

“My dear Bunny, but so he was!” cried Raffles. “Time was when I was none too pure an amateur. But after this I take leave to consider myself a professor of the professors. And I should like to see one more capable of skippering their side!”

===Chinese===
「你的关窍我自然懂。」我应道。「可我的意思是，你用力过猛了。你犯不着给我就那家伙写下一句彻头彻尾的谎话。」

「我也没写呀，邦尼。」

「还没呢？你不是说那位『教授中的亲王』在你走时『就在左近』么？」

「亲爱的邦尼，他那时可不就在左近吗！」拉夫尔斯喊道。「从前嘛，我也不算个太纯粹的业余选手。可经过这一遭，恕我冒昧，我要自封为教授们的教授了。我倒要看看，还有哪一位更有本事，替他们那一队执掌队长！」

## The Rest Cure / 休养疗法

===Original===
I had not seen Raffles for a month or more, and I was sadly in need of his advice. My life was being made a burden to me by a wretch who had obtained a bill of sale over the furniture in Mount Street, and it was only by living elsewhere that I could keep the vulpine villain from my door. This cost ready money, and my balance at the bank was sorely in need of another lift from Raffles. Yet, had he been in my shoes, he could not have vanished more effectually than he had done, both from the face of the town and from the ken of all who knew him.

It was late in August; he never played first-class cricket after July, when, a scholastic understudy took his place in the Middlesex eleven. And in vain did I scour my *Field* and my *Sportsman* for the country-house matches with which he wilfully preferred to wind up the season; the matches were there, but never the magic name of A. J. Raffles. Nothing was known of him at the Albany; he had left no instructions about his letters, either there or at the club. I began to fear that some evil had overtaken him. I scanned the features of captured criminals in the illustrated Sunday papers; on each occasion I breathed again; nor was anything worthy of Raffles going on. I will not deny that I was less anxious on his account than on my own. But it was a double relief to me when he gave a first characteristic sign of life.

===Chinese===
我已有一个多月没见着拉夫尔斯（Raffles）了，而我又何等迫切地需要他的指点。一个恶棍弄到了我芒特街（Mount Street）寓所家具的动产抵押契据，从此把我的日子折腾成了苦役；唯有搬到别处去住，我才能把这只狐狸心肠的坏蛋挡在门外。这自然要现钱，而我在银行里的存款，正亟需拉夫尔斯再拉一把。然而，哪怕换作他处在我的境地，也决计消失不到他那个地步——他不仅从这座城的地面上消失了，也从所有认识他的人的耳目里消失了。

时值八月下旬；七月一过他便不再打一级板球，到时自有一位学界替补，顶上他在米德尔塞克斯郡（Middlesex）队里的位置。我把《田野》（Field）和《体育人》（Sportsman）翻了个遍，想找那些乡村别墅赛事的消息——他偏偏喜欢拿这类比赛给赛季收尾——却是徒劳；赛事都在，却再不见 A. J. 拉夫尔斯那个有魔力的名字。奥尔巴尼公寓（Albany）里无人知道他的去向；无论在那里还是在俱乐部，他都没留下关于信件的半句吩咐。我开始担心他遭了什么不测。我把星期天画报上落网罪犯的面容一一端详过；每一回都长出一口气；也没有什么配得上拉夫尔斯手笔的事情在发生。我不否认，我为自己的悬心，实在比为他的更甚。因此，当他头一回发出独具其风的声息时，我感到的是双重的宽慰。

===Original===
I had called at the Albany for the fiftieth time, and returned to Piccadilly in my usual despair, when a street sloucher sidled up to me in furtive fashion and inquired if my name was what it is.

“ ’Cause this ’ere’s for you,” he rejoined to my affirmative, and with that I felt a crumpled note in my palm.

===Chinese===
那天我第五十次登门奥尔巴尼公寓，照例怀着绝望折回皮卡迪利（Piccadilly），一个街头闲汉却蹑手蹑脚蹭上前来，探问我是不是叫这个名字。

「那这个就是给你的了，」他听我应了是，接口便说；说话间，我已觉出掌心里多了一张揉皱的纸条。

===Original===
It was from Raffles. I smoothed out the twisted scrap of paper, and on it were just a couple of lines in pencil:

“Meet me in Holland Walk at dark tonight. Walk up and down till I come.

A. J. R.”

===Chinese===
是拉夫尔斯来的。我把那扭作一团的纸片抚平，上面只有铅笔写的寥寥两行：

今夜天黑后在荷兰步道（Holland Walk）见我。来回走动，等我来。

A. J. R.

===Original===
That was all! Not another syllable after all these weeks, and the few words scribbled in a wild caricature of his scholarly and dainty hand! I was no longer to be alarmed by this sort of thing; it was all so like the Raffles I loved least; and to add to my indignation, when at length I looked up from the mysterious missive, the equally mysterious messenger had disappeared in a manner worthy of the whole affair. He was, however, the first creature I espied under the tattered trees of Holland Walk that evening.

===Chinese===
就这么几个字！阔别这么多星期，再无一个音节，仅有的几个字还是潦草涂成，对他那手学者的雅洁字迹，简直是一场恶作剧式的戏仿！这类事情已不再能叫我惊慌；这一切都太像我最不喜欢的那个拉夫尔斯；更添我恼意的是，待我终于从这封神秘函件上抬起头来，那位同样神秘的信使也已无影无踪，那失踪的架势，同整桩事情相得益彰。然而当天傍晚，在荷兰步道那些枝叶凋残的树下，我头一眼认出的仍是他。

===Original===
“Seen ’im yet?” he inquired confidentially, blowing a vile cloud from his horrid pipe.

“No, I haven’t; and I want to know where you’ve seen him,” I replied sternly. “Why did you run away like that the moment you had given me his note?”

===Chinese===
「见着他了吗？」他知心地探问，一面从他那只可憎的烟斗里喷出一团恶臭的烟。

「没呢，我没见着；我倒想知道，你是在哪儿见着他的，」我板起脸答道。「递完他的字条，你怎么撒腿就跑？」

===Original===
“Orders, orders,” was the reply. “I ain’t such a juggins as to go agen a toff as makes it worf while to do as I’m bid an’ ’old me tongue.”

“And who may you be?” I asked jealously. “And what are you to Mr. Raffles?”

===Chinese===
「吩咐，上头有吩咐，」对方答道。「俺还没傻到那个地步，去违拗一位肯出大价钱、只求俺听话照办闭上嘴巴的老爷。」

「那么你究竟是何许人？」我酸溜溜地问。「你同拉夫尔斯先生又是什么关系？」

===Original===
“You silly ass, Bunny, don’t tell all Kensington that I’m in town!” replied my tatterdemalion, shooting up and smoothing out into a merely shabby Raffles. “Here, take my arm﻿—I’m not so beastly as I look. But neither am I in town, nor in England, nor yet on the face of the earth, for all that’s known of me to a single soul but you.”

===Chinese===
「你这头蠢驴，邦尼（Bunny），可别招得整个肯辛顿（Kensington）都知道我人在城里！」我这衣衫褴褛的家伙答道，一面直起身、抖擞开，变回一个不过略显寒酸的拉夫尔斯。「来，挽上我的胳膊——我没有看上去那么邋遢。可我人既不在城里，也不在英格兰，甚至不在这个地球的面上，因为除了你，普天之下没有一个灵魂知道我的下落。」

===Original===
“Then where are you,” I asked, “between ourselves?”

===Chinese===
「那你眼下人在哪里，」我问，「咱们私底下讲？」

===Original===
“I’ve taken a house near here for the holidays, where I’m going in for a Rest Cure of my own description. Why? Oh, for lots of reasons, my dear Bunny; among others, I have long had a wish to grow my own beard; under the next lamppost you will agree that it’s training on very nicely. Then, you mayn’t know it, but there’s a canny man at Scotland Yard who has had a quiet eye on me longer than I like. I thought it about time to have an eye on him, and I stared him in the face outside the Albany this very morning. That was when I saw you go in, and scribbled a line to give you when you came out. If he had caught us talking he would have spotted me at once.”

===Chinese===
「我在左近弄了一幢房子过假期，打算照我自拟的方子，实行一套休养疗法（Rest Cure）。为什么？哦，缘由多着呢，亲爱的邦尼；其中一条，我早就想蓄一把自己的胡子了；走到下盏路灯底下，你自会承认它养得挺不错。再者，你未必知道，苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）有位精明人，暗地里盯我，盯得比我乐意的时日久多了。我想也该轮到我去盯他一盯，就在今天早上，我在奥尔巴尼公寓外头同他打了个照面，直勾勾盯住他的脸瞧。也就是那会儿，我看见你进去，便涂了一行字，预备你出来时交给你。倘若叫他撞见你我二人说话，他当场就能把我认出来。」

===Original===
“So you are lying low out here!”

===Chinese===
「原来你是在这一带匿迹潜藏！」

===Original===
“I prefer to call it my Rest Cure,” returned Raffles, “and it’s really nothing else. I’ve got a furnished house at a time when no one else would have dreamed of taking one in town; and my very neighbors don’t know I’m there, though I’m bound to say there are hardly any of them at home. I don’t keep a servant, and do everything for myself. It’s the next best fun to a desert island. Not that I make much work, for I’m really resting, but I haven’t done so much solid reading for years. Rather a joke, Bunny: the man whose house I’ve taken is one of her Majesty’s inspectors of prisons, and his study’s a storehouse of criminology. It has been quite amusing to lie on one’s back and have a good look at one’s self as others fondly imagine they see one.”

===Chinese===
「我更愿意管它叫我的休养疗法，」拉夫尔斯答道，「而它也确实名实相副。我在一个谁也做梦想不到在城里租房的时节，弄到一幢带家具的宅子；连我的近邻都不知道我在里头，不过说句公道话，他们也几乎没一家在家。我不雇仆人，样样亲手操持。这乐趣仅次于荒岛。倒也说不上有多少活计，因为我确乎在休息，只是多年不曾读这么多扎实的书了。颇为可笑的一桩事，邦尼：我占的这宅子的主人，是女王陛下委任的监狱督察官之一，他的书房便是一座犯罪学的宝库。仰面朝天躺下来，把旁人自以为看见的那个自己，好生端详一番，实在有趣得很。」

===Original===
“But surely you get some exercise?” I asked; for he was leading me at a good rate through the leafy byways of Campden Hill; and his step was as springy and as light as ever.

“The best exercise I ever had in my life,” said Raffles; “and you would never live to guess what it is. It’s one of the reasons why I went in for this seedy kit. I follow cabs. Yes, Bunny, I turn out about dusk and meet the expresses at Euston or King’s Cross; that is, of course, I loaf outside and pick my cab, and often run my three or four miles for a bob or less. And it not only keeps you in the very pink: if you’re good they let you carry the trunks upstairs; and I’ve taken notes from the inside of more than one commodious residence which will come in useful in the autumn. In fact, Bunny, what with these new Rowton houses, my beard, and my otherwise well-spent holiday, I hope to have quite a good autumn season before the erratic Raffles turns up in town.”

===Chinese===
「可你总归有些锻炼吧？」我问；他正领着我，脚程不慢地穿过坎普登山（Campden Hill）绿荫夹道的小径；他的步子依旧轻盈而有弹性，一如往昔。

「我平生做过的最好的锻炼，」拉夫尔斯说，「你猜到死也猜不着是什么。这也是我置办这身褴褛行头的缘由之一。我跟马车。不错，邦尼，我大约黄昏时分出动，到尤斯顿（Euston）或国王十字（King's Cross）去接快车；也就是说，我在外头闲荡，物色我的马车，常为了一先令上下的赏钱，跑上三四英里。这不止叫人保持绝佳的竞技状态：你若乖巧，人家还许你把衣箱扛上楼去；我已从不止一幢宽敞宅邸的内部记下了门道，到秋天自有用处。说实在的，邦尼，有这些新开的罗顿公寓（Rowton houses），有我的胡子，加上我这度过得处处充实的假期，我指望趁那位行踪无常的拉夫尔斯在城里露面之前，好好经营一个秋季。」

===Original===
I felt it high time to wedge in a word about my own far less satisfactory affairs. But it was not necessary for me to recount half my troubles. Raffles could be as full of himself as many a worse man, and I did not like his society the less for these human outpourings. They had rather the effect of putting me on better terms with myself, through bringing him down to my level for the time being. But his egoism was not even skin-deep; it was rather a cloak, which Raffles could cast off quicker than any man I ever knew, as he did not fail to show me now.

===Chinese===
我感到，此其时也，该硬插进一句，提提我自己那摊远不如意的营生了。可我的苦处，连一半也不必细数。拉夫尔斯同许多更不堪的人一样，也会满口是自己，而这些凡人心声的倾吐，并不叫我减损对他的喜欢。它们倒起了另一种作用：把他一时拉低到我的水平，使我心安了几分。但他的自我连一副皮相的深度都没有；那与其说是本性，不如说是一件外氅，而拉夫尔斯脱下它来，比我所识得的任何人都快——此刻，他就不曾错过向我露这一手的机会。

===Original===
“Why, Bunny, this is the very thing!” he cried. “You must come and stay with me, and we’ll lie low side by side. Only remember it really is a Rest Cure. I want to keep literally as quiet as I was without you. What do you say to forming ourselves at once into a practically Silent Order? You agree? Very well, then, here’s the street and that’s the house.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，邦尼，这可正中下怀！」他叫道。「你务必来同我住下，咱们并肩蛰伏。只是记牢，这的的确确是一场休养疗法。我要保持的清静，须同没有你时一字不差。你说，咱们即刻自组一个近乎缄口的修会，如何？你同意？很好，那么，这儿就是那条街，那就是那幢房子。」

===Original===
It was ever such a quiet little street, turning out of one of those which climb right over the pleasant hill. One side was monopolized by the garden wall of an ugly but enviable mansion standing in its own ground; opposite were a solid file of smaller but taller houses; on neither side were there many windows alight, nor a solitary soul on the pavement or in the road. Raffles led the way to one of the small tall houses. It stood immediately behind a lamppost, and I could not but notice that a love-lock of Virginia creeper was trailing almost to the step, and that the bow-window on the ground floor was closely shuttered. Raffles admitted himself with his latchkey, and I squeezed past him into a very narrow hall. I did not hear him shut the door, but we were no longer in the lamplight, and he pushed softly past me in his turn.

===Chinese===
那真是条僻静之极的小街，从一条翻越那座宜人山丘的大道上岔出来。一侧被一幢独占地皮、丑陋却令人艳羡的巨宅的花园墙占尽；对面是一列矮些却更高、结结实实的房子；两侧亮着灯的窗户寥寥无几，人行道上、街心路中，不见一个孤影。拉夫尔斯领头走向其中一幢瘦高的房子。房子正处在一根路灯柱背后，我不由得注意到，一绺垂发似的爬山虎几乎垂到了台阶，底层的凸窗百叶紧闭。拉夫尔斯用弹簧锁钥匙放自己进去，我侧身从他身边挤进一条极窄的门厅。我没听见他关门，但我们已不在灯光里，接着，他又轻轻从我身边挤了过去。

===Original===
“I’ll get a light,” he muttered as he went; but to let him pass I had leaned against some electric switches, and while his back was turned I tried one of these without thinking. In an instant hall and staircase were flooded with light; in another Raffles was upon me in a fury, and, all was dark once more. He had not said a word, but I heard him breathing through his teeth.

===Chinese===
「我去弄点亮，」他边走边咕哝；可为了让他过去，我倚在了几只电灯开关上，趁他转身，我无心中按了一下。霎时间门厅和楼梯一片通明；再一霎眼，拉夫尔斯已勃然大怒地扑到我身上，接着——四下重归漆黑。他一句话没说，我却听见他从齿缝里抽气。

===Original===
Nor was there anything to tell me now. The mere flash of electric light upon a hail of chaos and uncarpeted stairs, and on the face of Raffles as he sprang to switch it off, had been enough even for me.

===Chinese===
如今也不需要谁来告诉我什么了。电光那一闪，照亮一片狼藉和没有地毯的楼梯，也照亮拉夫尔斯扑上去关灯时的那张脸——单这一闪，连我也尽够了。

===Original===
“So this is how you have taken the house,” said I in his own undertone. “ ‘Taken’ is good; ‘taken’ is beautiful!”

“Did you think I’d done it through an agent?” he snarled. “Upon my word, Bunny, I did you the credit of supposing you saw the joke all the time!”

===Chinese===
「原来这房子是这么『到手』的，」我压着嗓子，学他的腔调说。「『到手』二字用得好；『到手』二字用得妙啊！」

「你还当我是通过中介办的手续？」他嗤笑道。「凭良心讲，邦尼，我高看你一眼，还当你自始至终都品出了这个玩笑！」

===Original===
“Why shouldn’t you take a house,” I asked, “and pay for it?”

“Why should I,” he retorted, “within three miles of the Albany? Besides, I should have had no peace; and I meant every word I said about my Rest Cure.”

===Chinese===
「你为什么不可以租下一幢房子，」我问，「照价付钱呢？」

「在奥尔巴尼公寓方圆三英里之内，我为什么要租？」他反诘道。「再说，租了我便须臾不得安宁；而关于我的休养疗法，我说出口的每个字都是当真的。」

===Original===
“You are actually staying in a house where you’ve broken in to steal?”

“Not to steal, Bunny! I haven’t stolen a thing. But staying here I certainly am, and having the most complete rest a busy man could wish.”

===Chinese===
「你居然就住在一幢你破门而入、行窃而来的房子里？」

「不是行窃，邦尼！我一件东西也没偷。但住，我是确确实实住下了，还享着一个忙人所能指望的最完备的休息。」

===Original===
“There’ll be no rest for me!”

===Chinese===
「这下我可别想安生了！」

===Original===
Raffles laughed as he struck a match. I had followed him into what would have been the back drawing-room in the ordinary little London house; the inspector of prisons had converted it into a separate study by filling the folding doors with bookshelves, which I scanned at once for the congenial works of which Raffles had spoken. I was not able to carry my examination very far. Raffles had lighted a candle, stuck (by its own grease) in the crown of an opera hat, which he opened the moment the wick caught. The light thus struck the ceiling in an oval shaft, which left the rest of the room almost as dark as it had been before.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯划火柴时笑出了声。我随他进了那间屋子——在寻常的伦敦小宅里，这本该是后客厅；那位监狱督察把折门处塞满书架，将它隔成了一间独立书房，我当即扫视书架，想找拉夫尔斯提过的那些正对脾胃的著作。我的考察未能深入多远。拉夫尔斯点起一支蜡烛，借烛油黏在一顶歌剧帽的帽膛里，烛芯一燃，他便把帽子撑了开来。烛光由此成一束椭圆，直打在天花板上，屋里其余各处，几乎还同先前一样黑。

===Original===
“Sorry, Bunny!” said Raffles, sitting on one pedestal of a desk from which the top had been removed, and setting his makeshift lantern on the other. “In broad daylight, when it can’t be spotted from the outside, you shall have as much artificial light as you like. If you want to do some writing, that’s the top of the desk on end against the mantelpiece. You’ll never have a better chance so far as interruption goes. But no midnight oil or electricity! You observe that their last care was to fix up these shutters; they appear to have taken the top off the desk to get at ’em without standing on it; but the beastly things wouldn’t go all the way up, and the strip they leave would give us away to the backs of the other houses if we lit up after dark. Mind that telephone! If you touch the receiver they will know at the exchange that the house is not empty, and I wouldn’t put it past the colonel to have told them exactly how long he was going to be away. He’s pretty particular: look at the strips of paper to keep the dust off his precious books!”

===Chinese===
「对不住，邦尼！」拉夫尔斯说着，在一张卸去桌面的书桌的一侧基座上坐下，又把那盏将就的灯安在另一侧上。「大白天里，只要外头瞧不出破绽，你要多少灯光有多少灯光。你若想写点什么，那桌面正立在壁炉架边。单论不受打搅，你再难遇上比这更好的机会。但深宵的灯油和电灯，一概免了！你瞧见了吧，他们临行前最后操心的就是装好这些百叶；他们像是卸下桌面，免得人踩着桌子去够；可那劳什子偏推不到顶，留下的那一条缝，会在入夜后把我们亮给后面几幢房子的后窗。留神那部电话！你一碰听筒，电话局便知道宅子不是空的；何况以那位上校的为人，把离家多久原原本本告诉他们，这事他完全做得出来。他讲究得很：瞧那一根根防尘的纸条，护着他那些宝贝书呢！」

===Original===
“Is he a colonel?” I asked, perceiving that Raffles referred to the absentee householder.

“Of sappers,” he replied, “and a V.C. into the bargain, confound him! Got it at Rorke’s Drift; prison governor or inspector ever since; favorite recreation, what do you think? Revolver shooting! You can read all about him in his own *Who’s Who*. A devil of a chap to tackle, Bunny, when he’s at home!”

===Chinese===
「他是位上校？」我问，听出拉夫尔斯说的是那位不在家的房主。

「工兵部队的上校，」他答道，「外带一枚维多利亚十字勋章（V.C.），叫人恨得牙根痒！在罗克渡口（Rorke's Drift）得的；打那以后，一直做监狱长或监狱督察；最钟爱的消遣，你猜是什么？手枪射击！他自己那册《名人录》（Who's Who）里，尽可以读到他的全部底细。这么一号人物在家的时候，可不好招架，邦尼！」

===Original===
“And where is he now?” I asked uneasily. “And do you know he isn’t on his way home?”

“Switzerland,” replied Raffles, chuckling; “he wrote one too many labels, and was considerate enough to leave it behind for our guidance. Well, no one ever comes back from Switzerland at the beginning of September, you know; and nobody ever thinks of coming back before the servants. When they turn up they won’t get in. I keep the latch jammed, but the servants will think it’s jammed itself, and while they’re gone for the locksmith we shall walk out like gentlemen﻿—if we haven’t done so already.”

===Chinese===
「那他此刻人在何处？」我不安地问。「你拿得准他不在回家路上？」

「瑞士，」拉夫尔斯答道，暗自发笑，「他行李标签多写了一张，又体己地把它留下给我们引路。嗯，你是知道的，九月初从来没有人从瑞士回来；也没有人会赶在仆人们前头动身回来。他们到时进不了门。我让门闩卡着，仆人们只当它自己卡住了，等他们去找锁匠的工夫，咱们便像绅士一般踱出门去——倘若那时还没走成的话。」

===Original===
“As you walked in, I suppose?”

Raffles shook his head in the dim light to which my sight was growing inured.

===Chinese===
「就像你们当初进来那样，我猜？」

拉夫尔斯在我已渐渐看惯的昏暗光线里摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No, Bunny, I regret to say I came in through the dormer window. They were painting next door but one. I never did like ladder work, but it takes less time than in picking a lock in the broad light of a street lamp.”

“So they left you a latchkey as well as everything else!”

===Chinese===
「不，邦尼，说来遗憾，我是从屋顶的老虎窗进来的。隔一户人家正在油漆外墙。我从来不爱爬梯子的活计，可比之大天白日在街灯底下撬锁，这还省些工夫。」

「这么说，他们连弹簧钥匙也一并给你留下了，别的更不用提！」

===Original===
“No, Bunny. I was just able to make that for myself. I am playing at *Robinson Crusoe*, not *The Swiss Family Robinson*. And now, my dear Friday, if you will kindly take off those boots, we can explore the island before we turn in for the night.”

===Chinese===
「不，邦尼。钥匙嘛，我恰好能自己配一把。我如今扮演的是《鲁滨逊漂流记》（Robinson Crusoe），可不是《瑞士家庭鲁滨逊》（The Swiss Family Robinson）。好了，我亲爱的星期五，劳驾脱下你那双靴子，咱们还能赶在上床之前，把这座岛勘察一遍。」

===Original===
The stairs were very steep and narrow, and they creaked alarmingly as Raffles led the way up, with the single candle in the crown of the colonel’s hat. He blew it out before we reached the half-landing, where a naked window stared upon the backs of the houses in the next road, but lit it again at the drawing-room door. I just peeped in upon a semi-grand swathed in white and a row of water colors mounted in gold. An excellent bathroom broke our journey to the second floor.

===Chinese===
楼梯又陡又窄，拉夫尔斯领路上去时，梯级响得吓人，上校帽子膛里那支孤零零的蜡烛是仅有的光。还没到半层平台他便吹熄了它——平台上一扇没遮没拦的窗，直瞪着下一条街那些房子的后背——到客厅门口他又点上。我仅仅窥了一眼，看见一台裹着白布的中型三角钢琴，和一排金框的水彩画。一间上好的浴室，打断了我们上二楼的行程。

===Original===
“I’ll have one tonight,” said I, taking heart of a luxury unknown in my last sordid sanctuary.

“You’ll do no such thing,” snapped Raffles. “Have the goodness to remember that our island is one of a group inhabited by hostile tribes. You can fill the bath quietly if you try, but it empties under the study window, and makes the very devil of a noise about it. No, Bunny, I bale out every drop and pour it away through the scullery sink, so you will kindly consult me before you turn a tap. Here’s your room; hold the light outside while I draw the curtains; it’s the old chap’s dressing-room. Now you can bring the glim. How’s that for a jolly wardrobe? And look at his coats on their cross-trees inside: dapper old dog, shouldn’t you say? Mark the boots on the shelf above, and the little brass rail for his ties! Didn’t I tell you he was particular? And wouldn’t he simply love to catch us at his kit?”

===Chinese===
「今晚我要洗个澡，」我说；这等奢侈，我上一处寒酸的避难窟里可无从消受，想到这里，胆气也壮了几分。

「你休想，」拉夫尔斯厉声道。「劳驾记牢，咱们这座岛，是敌对部族环伺的群岛中的一座。放水时若加着小心，倒也能悄悄放满一缸，可它排放的水正打书房窗下经过，闹起来惊天动地。不，邦尼，我都是一滴一滴舀干，再从洗碗间的水槽倒掉，所以你拧任何龙头之前，请先来请教我。这是你的房间；你在外头举灯，等我拉上窗帘；这是老家伙的更衣室。现在可以把灯拿进来了。这口衣柜如何，够气派吧？再瞧里头横杆上挂的外套：一个讲究的老家伙，你说是不是？细看上层架子的靴子，还有挂领带的那根小铜杆！我不是说过他讲究吗？若叫他当场逮住咱们乱翻他的行头，他岂不是要乐坏了？」

===Original===
“Let’s only hope it would give him an apoplexy,” said I shuddering.

===Chinese===
「咱们只指望那一惊能送他一场中风吧，」我打了个寒噤说。

===Original===
“I shouldn’t build on it,” replied Raffles. “That’s a big man’s trouble, and neither you nor I could get into the old chap’s clothes. But come into the best bedroom, Bunny. You won’t think me selfish if I don’t give it up to you? Look at this, my boy, look at this! It’s the only one I use in all the house.”

===Chinese===
「我可不敢指望这个，」拉夫尔斯答道。「中风是块头大的人的毛病，你我二人谁也套不进老家伙的衣裳。不过，随我到最好的卧室来，邦尼。我不把这间让给你，你总不至于当我自私吧？瞧瞧这个，我的伙计，瞧瞧这个！整幢宅子里，我只用这一间。」

===Original===
I had followed him into a good room, with ample windows closely curtained, and he had switched on the light in a hanging lamp at the bedside. The rays fell from a thick green funnel in a plateful of strong light upon a table deep in books. I noticed several volumes of the *Invasion of the Crimea*.

===Chinese===
我随他进了一间好屋，宽大的窗户帘幕紧掩，他拧亮了床侧一盏悬灯。光线从一只厚实的绿灯罩里泻下，满满一盘强光，照在一张深陷书堆里的桌上。我留意到好几卷《克里米亚战争史》（Invasion of the Crimea）。

===Original===
“That’s where I rest the body and exercise the brain,” said Raffles. “I have long wanted to read my Kinglake from A to Z, and I manage about a volume a night. There’s a style for you, Bunny! I love the punctilious thoroughness of the whole thing; one can understand its appeal to our careful colonel. His name, did you say? Crutchley, Bunny﻿—Colonel Crutchley, R.E., V.C.”

===Chinese===
「身体在这儿歇息，脑筋在这儿操练，」拉夫尔斯说。「金莱克（Kinglake）这部书，我早想从头读到尾，如今一晚对付一卷，正合式。这文笔，够你受用的，邦尼！我爱它通篇那种一丝不苟的绵密；也懂得它何以投合咱们这位精细的上校。他的名字，你方才问？克拉奇利（Crutchley），邦尼——克拉奇利上校，皇家工兵（R.E.），维多利亚十字勋章得主。」

===Original===
“We’d put his valor to the test!” said I, feeling more valiant myself after our tour of inspection.

“Not so loud on the stairs,” whispered Raffles. “There’s only one door between us and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「咱们倒要考校考校他的英勇！」我说；巡视一番下来，自觉胆气也壮了几分。

「楼梯上小点儿声，」拉夫尔斯耳语道。「咱们同——之间，只隔着一道门呢。」

===Original===
Raffles stood still at my feet, and well he might! A deafening double knock had resounded through the empty house; and to add to the utter horror of the moment, Raffles instantly blew out the light. I heard my heart pounding. Neither of us breathed. We were on our way down to the first landing, and for a moment we stood like mice; then Raffles heaved a deep sigh, and in the depths I heard the gate swing home.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯在我脚边蓦地站住，他也有他站住的理由！一阵震耳的两连叩，响彻了空宅；更叫这一刻的惊怖雪上加霜，拉夫尔斯应声便掐灭了灯。我听见自己的心口擂鼓。我们俩谁也不敢喘气。我们正下到一层平台，一时间僵立如鼠；随后拉夫尔斯长叹一声，我在宅子深处，听见院门哐啷一声合上了。

===Original===
“Only the postman, Bunny! He will come now and again, though they have obviously left instructions at the post-office. I hope the old colonel will let them have it when he gets back. I confess it gave me a turn.”

===Chinese===
「不过是邮差，邦尼！他们显然在邮局留了话，可他还是会隔三差五来一趟。我只盼老上校回来后，好好训他们一顿。我承认，方才那一惊，非同小可。」

===Original===
“Turn!” I gasped. “I must have a drink, if I die for it.”

“My dear Bunny, that’s no part of my Rest Cure.”

===Chinese===
「非同小可！」我喘着气说。「我非得喝上一口不可，为它死了也甘心。」

「亲爱的邦尼，喝酒可不在我的休养疗法之内。」

===Original===
“Then goodbye! I can’t stand it; feel my forehead; listen to my heart! Crusoe found a footprint, but he never heard a double-knock at the street door!”

“ ‘Better live in the midst of alarms,’ ” quoted Raffles, “ ‘than dwell in this horrible place.’ I must confess we get it both ways, Bunny. Yet I’ve nothing but tea in the house.”

===Chinese===
「那么再会吧！我受不了了；摸摸我的额头；听听我的心口！鲁滨逊不过发现了一个脚印，可他从不曾听见临街大门上的两连叩！」

「『宁居四面警报之中，』拉夫尔斯引经据典，『也强似住在这可怕的地方。』我得承认，邦尼，这两样咱们倒都占全了。可这宅子里，我除茶之外一无所有。」

===Original===
“And where do you make that? Aren’t you afraid of smoke?”

“There’s a gas-stove in the dining-room.”

===Chinese===
「那你在哪儿烧茶？不怕冒烟吗？」

「饭厅里有只煤气灶。」

===Original===
“But surely to goodness,” I cried, “there’s a cellar lower down!”

===Chinese===
「可是老天爷在上，」我叫道，「底下不还有个酒窖嘛！」

===Original===
“My dear, good Bunny,” said Raffles, “I’ve told you already that I didn’t come in here on business. I came in for the Cure. Not a penny will these people be the worse, except for their washing and their electric light, and I mean to leave enough to cover both items.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的、善良的邦尼，」拉夫尔斯说，「我早已告诉过你，我进这宅子，不是来办公事的。我是为疗法进来的。这家人除了洗衣用水和电灯两项，一个便士的损失也不会有，而这两项，我也打算留下足够的钱抵偿。」

===Original===
“Then,” said I, “since Brutus is such a very honorable man, we will borrow a bottle from the cellar, and replace it before we go.”

===Chinese===
「既然如此，」我说，「布鲁图斯（Brutus）既是这么一位体面的君子，咱们便向酒窖借一瓶来，临行之前原样补上。」

===Original===
Raffles slapped me softly on the back, and I knew that I had gained my point. It was often the case when I had the presence of heart and mind to stand up to him. But never was little victory of mine quite so grateful as this. Certainly it was a very small cellar, indeed a mere cupboard under the kitchen stairs, with a most ridiculous lock. Nor was this cupboard overstocked with wine. But I made out a jar of whiskey, a shelf of Zeltinger, another of claret, and a short one at the top which presented a little battery of golden-leafed necks and corks. Raffles set his hand no lower. He examined the labels while I held folded hat and naked light.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯在我背上轻轻一拍，我便知道这一仗我打赢了。每逢我提得起心气、稳得住心神，敢同他分庭抗礼，结局往往如此。但我这些小小的胜绩，从没有一回像这次这样叫人受用。诚然，那是个极小的酒窖，实在只是厨房楼梯下的一口柜子，配着一把再可笑不过的锁。柜中存酒也不充盈。但我辨出了一坛威士忌、一层泽尔廷格（Zeltinger）白葡萄酒、一层红葡萄酒，顶层还有短短一格，陈列着一小队金箔裹颈、软木塞封口的酒瓶。拉夫尔斯的手没有往更低处伸。他逐张查验酒标，我在一旁举着折拢的帽子和裸露的烛火。

===Original===
“Mumm, ’84!” he whispered. “G. H. Mumm, and AD 1884! I am no wine-bibber, Bunny, as you know, but I hope you appreciate the specifications as I do. It looks to me like the only bottle, the last of its case, and it does seem a bit of a shame; but more shame for the miser who hoards in his cellar what was meant for mankind! Come, Bunny, lead the way. This baby is worth nursing. It would break my heart if anything happened to it now!”

===Chinese===
「玛姆（Mumm），八四年！」他低声道。「G. H. 玛姆，公元一八八四！邦尼，你知道我不是贪杯之人，可我希望你同我一样赏识这几项名目。依我看，这是硕果仅存的一瓶，一箱里的最后一瓶，糟蹋了确乎有些可惜；可那守财奴更可鄙——把天赐人类的东西，窖藏在自己地窖里！来吧，邦尼，头前带路。这宝贝要捧着护着。它此刻若有个闪失，我的心也要碎了！」

===Original===
So we celebrated my first night in the furnished house; and I slept beyond belief, slept as I never was to sleep there again. But it was strange to hear the milkman in the early morning, and the postman knocking his way along the street an hour later, and to be passed over by one destroying angel after another. I had come down early enough, and watched through the drawing-room blind the cleansing of all the steps in the street but ours. Yet Raffles had evidently been up some time; the house seemed far purer than overnight as though he had managed to air it room by room; and from the one with the gas-stove there came a frizzling sound that fattened the heart.

===Chinese===
就这样，我们庆祝了我在那幢带家具宅子里的第一夜；我睡得没命地沉，此后在那里再没有那样睡过。可是清早听送奶人的动静，一小时后听邮差一路敲门敲过街去，眼看一位接一位的灭命天使打门前一一越过，滋味古怪得很。我下楼下得够早，从客厅百叶缝里，眼看全街的台阶都洗刷了一遍，独独绕开了我们家。然而拉夫尔斯分明已起了些时候；宅子比隔夜清爽得多，仿佛他设法一间一间给通过了风；从有煤气灶的那间屋里，传来一阵滋滋的煎炸声，听得人心头滋润。

===Original===
I only would I had the pen to do justice to the week I spent indoors on Campden Hill! It might make amusing reading; the reality for me was far removed from the realm of amusement. Not that I was denied many a laugh of suppressed heartiness when Raffles and I were together. But half our time we very literally saw nothing of each other. I need not say whose fault that was. He would be quiet; he was in ridiculous and offensive earnest about his egregious Cure. Kinglake he would read by the hour together, day and night, by the hanging lamp, lying upstairs on the best bed. There was daylight enough for me in the drawing-room below; and there I would sit immersed in criminous tomes weakly fascinated until I shivered and shook in my stocking soles. Often I longed to do something hysterically desperate, to rouse Raffles and bring the street about our ears; once I did bring him about mine by striking a single note on the piano, with the soft pedal down. His neglect of me seemed wanton at the time. I have long realized that he was only wise to maintain silence at the expense of perilous amenities, and as fully justified in those secret and solitary sorties which made bad blood in my veins. He was far cleverer than I at getting in and out; but even had I been his match for stealth and wariness, my company would have doubled every risk. I admit now that he treated me with quite as much sympathy as common caution would permit. But at the time I took it so badly as to plan a small revenge.

===Chinese===
但愿我有一支笔，配写尽我在坎普登山宅内度过的那一周！写出来或许有趣；于我，那段真实的日子，同趣味的疆域相去万里。这倒不是说，拉夫尔斯与我同处时，我不曾压着嗓子痛快笑过许多回。可我们有一半光阴，千真万确彼此照不上面。这该怪谁，毋庸我说。他非要清静；对他那套卓绝的疗法，他认真到荒唐、认真到咄咄逼人的地步。金莱克的书，他成个钟点地读，白天黑夜，就着那盏悬灯，仰卧在楼上最好的卧床上。楼下客厅里，天光于我尽够；我便坐在那里，泡进一堆犯罪学的大部头，软弱地任它迷住，直到穿线袜的两脚瑟瑟发抖。我常常渴望干一桩歇斯底里的鲁莽事，惊动拉夫尔斯，把整条街闹到我们头顶上来；有一回我当真把他闹到了我身边——踩下弱音踏板，在钢琴上单敲了一个音。他那样怠慢我，当时看来简直是存心的。我早已明白：他不惜舍弃种种冒险的温存而保持缄默，实为明智；他那些背着我的单独出动——它们害我血管里尽生恶气——也同样理直气壮。进出的门道，他远比我高明；可即便我潜行戒惧的功夫与他旗鼓相当，有我做伴，每一样风险都要加倍。如今我承认，他待我的体恤，已是寻常谨慎所能容许的极限。可当时我把这一切记恨在心，竟谋划起一场小小的报复。

===Original===
What with his flourishing beard and the increasing shabbiness of the only suit he had brought with him to the house, there was no denying that Raffles had now the advantage of a permanent disguise. That was another of his excuses for leaving me as he did, and it was the one I was determined to remove. On a morning, therefore, when I awoke to find him flown again, I proceeded to execute a plan which I had already matured in my mind. Colonel Crutchley was a married man; there were no signs of children in the house; on the other hand, there was much evidence that the wife was a woman of fashion. Her dresses overflowed the wardrobe and her room; large, flat, cardboard boxes were to be found in every corner of the upper floors. She was a tall woman; I was not too tall a man. Like Raffles, I had not shaved on Campden Hill. That morning, however, I did my best with a very fair razor which the colonel had left behind in my room; then I turned out the lady’s wardrobe and the cardboard boxes, and took my choice.

===Chinese===
他一把胡子长得正旺，随他来这宅子的唯一一套衣裳又日见寒酸，无可否认，拉夫尔斯如今占着一副常备化装的长处。这又是他把我撇在一边的借口之一，而我打定主意，偏要把这条借口除掉。于是，一天早晨，我醒来又发现他出去了，便着手执行一项在心里酝酿成熟的计划。克拉奇利上校是有妇之夫；宅中不见孩童的痕迹；另一面，种种迹象表明，太太是位时髦女子。她的衣裙从衣柜和她的房间里满溢出来；楼上各层的角落里，处处搁着扁平的大纸盒。她是位高个子太太；我也是个个子不算太高的男人。同拉夫尔斯一样，在坎普登山我也没刮过脸。不过那天早晨，我用上校落在我房里的一把上好剃刀，尽力收拾了一番；然后我翻出太太的衣柜和那些纸盒，细细拣选。

===Original===
I have fair hair, and at the time it was rather long. With a pair of Mrs. Crutchley’s tongs and a discarded hairnet, I was able to produce an almost immodest fringe. A big black hat with a wintry feather completed a headdress as unseasonable as my skating skirt and feather boa; of course, the good lady had all her summer frocks away with her in Switzerland. This was all the more annoying from the fact that we were having a very warm September; so I was not sorry to hear Raffles return as I was busy adding a layer of powder to my heated countenance. I listened a moment on the landing, but as he went into the study I determined to complete my toilet in every detail. My idea was first to give him the fright he deserved, and secondly to show him that I was quite as fit to move abroad as he. It was, however, I confess, a pair of the colonel’s gloves that I was buttoning as I slipped down to the study even more quietly than usual. The electric light was on, as it generally was by day, and under it stood as formidable a figure as ever I encountered in my life of crime.

===Chinese===
我生着金发，那时头发又留得颇长。借克拉奇利太太（Mrs. Crutchley）的一副烫发钳和一顶弃置的发网，我卷出了一副几乎有伤风化的刘海。一顶插着冬日翎毛的大黑帽，凑齐了一头顶戴，同我的溜冰裙和羽毛围脖一样不合时令；也难怪，那位好太太的全部夏装，都随她本人去了瑞士。我们正过着的是一个分外燠热的九月，这就越发叫人懊恼；所以我正忙着给发热的脸扑一层粉时，听见拉夫尔斯回来，倒也不觉得懊恼了。我在楼梯平台上侧耳听了片刻，听出他进了书房，便决意把梳妆进行到最后一道工序。我的主意，一是先给他吃一吓，吓他个罪有应得；二是向他表明，我出外走动的本钱，一点也不输给他。然而我得招认，当我比往常更悄无声息地溜下楼、溜向书房时，手上扣着的，正是上校的一副手套。电灯亮着——白天里它素来亮着——灯下立着的，是我犯罪生涯里所遇过的最可畏的一副身影。

===Original===
Imagine a thin but extremely wiry man, past middle age, brown and bloodless as any crabapple, but as coolly truculent and as casually alert as Raffles at his worst. It was, it could only be, the fire-eating and prison-inspecting colonel himself! He was ready for me, a revolver in his hand, taken, as I could see, from one of those locked drawers in the pedestal desk with which Raffles had refused to tamper; the drawer was open, and a bunch of keys depended from the lock. A grim smile crumpled up the parchment face, so that one eye was puckered out of sight; the other was propped open by an eyeglass, which, however, dangled on its string when I appeared.

===Chinese===
想象一个精瘦却极其韧健的男人，年过半百，像只野苹果似的干黄无血色，却同拉夫尔斯最凶的时候一样，冷酷地寻衅，闲适地警觉。这正是，也只能是，那位性如烈火的监狱督察上校本人！他早有防备，手里一柄左轮，我看得出，取自那张基座写字台带锁的抽屉——那些锁，拉夫尔斯还不屑去动；抽屉敞着，锁眼里垂下一串钥匙。一丝狞笑把那张羊皮纸似的脸揉皱，一只眼睛皱得没了踪影；另一只被单片眼镜撑着，可我一露面，那镜片便在丝带上晃荡起来。

===Original===
“A woman, begad!” the warrior exclaimed. “And where’s the man, you scarlet hussy?”

Not a word could I utter. But, in my horror and my amazement, I have no sort of doubt that I acted the part I had assumed in a manner I never should have approached in happier circumstances.

===Chinese===
「一个女人，老天作证！」这位武夫失声叫道。「那个男人呢，你这妖冶的贱货？」

我一个字也吐不出来。但是，在那样惊骇、那样错愕之中，我毫不怀疑，我把自己揽下的那个角色，演到了一种境地——境况顺遂时，我是断断企及不了的。

===Original===
“Come, come, my lass,” cried the old oak veteran, “I’m not going to put a bullet through you, you know! You tell me all about it, and it’ll do you more good than harm. There, I’ll put the nasty thing away and﻿—God bless me, if the brazen wench hasn’t squeezed into the wife’s kit!”

===Chinese===
「好了，好了，姑娘，」这位老橡木般的沙场宿将叫道，「我可不会拿子弹穿你，知道吧！你一五一十都告诉我，对你好处多过坏处。瞧，我把这讨厌的家伙收起来——上帝保佑，这厚脸皮的丫头，居然把自己塞进了太太的行头！」

===Original===
A squeeze it happened to have been, and in my emotion it felt more of one than ever; but his sudden discovery had not heightened the veteran’s animosity against me. On the contrary, I caught a glint of humor through his gleaming glass, and he proceeded to pocket his revolver like the gentleman he was.

===Chinese===
塞，倒也确有其事；情急之下，我只觉得塞得比先前更紧了；可他这突然的发现，并未抬高这位宿将对我的敌意。恰恰相反，我从他亮闪闪的镜片后捕捉到一丝笑意，他随即将左轮揣回口袋，像他本来那样的绅士。

===Original===
“Well, well, it’s lucky I looked in,” he continued. “I only came round on the off-chance of letters, but if I hadn’t you’d have had another week in clover. Begad, though, I saw your handwriting the moment I’d got my nose inside! Now just be sensible and tell me where your good man is.”

===Chinese===
「唉，唉，幸亏我进来瞧了一眼，」他接着说。「我只是抱着万一有信的指望拐进来的；可要是没来，你们还能再过一个礼拜的舒坦日子。不过老天作证，我鼻子才进门，就瞧见了你的笔迹！现在放明白些，告诉我你家男人在哪里。」

===Original===
I had no man. I was alone, had broken in alone. There was not a soul in the affair (much less the house) except myself. So much I stuttered out in tones too hoarse to betray me on the spot. But the old man of the world shook a hard old head.

===Chinese===
我没有男人。我是独自一人，独自一人闯进来的。这桩事里（更不必说这宅子里），除了我自己，连一个人影也没有。这许多话，我嗫嗫嚅嚅吐了出来，嗓音哑得当场露不出破绽。可这位老于世故的人，把一颗硬邦邦的老脑袋摇了又摇。

===Original===
“Quite right not to give away your pal,” said he. “But I’m not one of the marines, my dear, and you mustn’t expect me to swallow all that. Well, if you won’t say, you won’t, and we must just send for those who will.”

===Chinese===
「不肯出卖同伙，很对，」他说。「可是亲爱的，我不是海军陆战队里那等好哄的兵，你休想叫我一古脑全吞下去。好吧，你不说就不用说，咱们只好去请那些肯说的人。」

===Original===
In a flash I saw his fell design. The telephone directory lay open on one of the pedestals. He must have been consulting it when he heard me on the stairs; he had another look at it now; and that gave me my opportunity. With a presence of mind rare enough in me to excuse the boast, I flung myself upon the instrument in the corner and hurled it to the ground with all my might. I was myself sent spinning into the opposite corner at the same instant. But the instrument happened to be a standard of the more elaborate pattern, and I flattered myself that I had put the delicate engine out of action for the day.

===Chinese===
电光石火间，我看穿了他的毒计。电话号码簿摊开在写字台一侧的基座上。他准是正查着号码，听见我在楼梯上的动静；这会儿他又朝它望了一眼；而这一望，给了我可乘之机。我扑向墙角那部话机，抡圆了将它砸到地上——这份急智，在我身上实在罕见，罕见得容我自夸一句。同一瞬间，我自己也打着转栽进了对面的墙角。可那部话机，偏偏是较考究的那种立柱式，我颇自得地认定，这台精巧的机器，这一天是休想再运转了。

===Original===
Not that my adversary took the trouble to ascertain. He was looking at me strangely in the electric light, standing intently on his guard, his right hand in the pocket where he had dropped his revolver. And I﻿—I hardly knew it﻿—but I caught up the first thing handy for self-defence, and was brandishing the bottle which Raffles and I had emptied in honor of my arrival on this fatal scene.

===Chinese===
倒不是我的对手费神去查证了。他站在电灯光下，古怪地打量我，全神戒备，右手插在兜里——左轮正是掖进那个兜里的。而我——我自己也几乎不曾察觉——抓起了头一件顺手的防身物件，正挥舞着一只酒瓶：拉夫尔斯同我为了庆贺我到场、在这个致命的场合里喝空的那一只。

===Original===
“Be shot if I don’t believe you’re the man himself!” cried the colonel, shaking an armed fist in my face. “You young wolf in sheep’s clothing. Been at my wine, of course! Put down that bottle; down with it this instant, or I’ll drill a tunnel through your middle. I thought so! Begad, sir, you shall pay for this! Don’t you give me an excuse for potting you now, or I’ll jump at the chance! My last bottle of ’84﻿—you miserable blackguard﻿—you unutterable beast!”

===Chinese===
「让我挨枪子好了，要是我信不过——你就是那男人本人！」上校嚷道，把攥着武器的拳头直杵到我脸前。「你这披着羊皮的小狼。喝了我的酒，自然是吧！放下那瓶子；立刻放下，不然我在你肚子上钻一条隧道。我早料到了！老天作证，先生，这笔账有得你偿！这会儿你可别给我开枪打你的口实，不然我正求之不得！我最后一瓶八四年——你这下流的恶棍——你这说不出口的畜生！」

===Original===
He had browbeaten me into his own chair in his own corner; he was standing over me, empty bottle in one hand, revolver in the other, and murder itself in the purple puckers of his raging face. His language I will not even pretend to indicate: his skinny throat swelled and trembled with the monstrous volleys. He could smile at my appearance in his wife’s clothes; he would have had my blood for the last bottle of his best champagne. His eyes were not hidden now; they needed no eyeglass to prop them open; large with fury, they started from the livid mask. I watched nothing else. I could not understand why they should start out as they did. I did not try. I say I watched nothing else﻿—until I saw the face of Raffles over the unfortunate officer’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
他连唬带吓，把我逼进了他自己那个角落里他自己的椅子；他居高临下站在我跟前，一手空瓶，一手左轮，暴怒的脸上紫涨的褶皱里，写满了杀机。他那些言语，我连装作转述都办不到：他精瘦的喉头，随着一阵阵骇人的排炮鼓胀、发颤。我穿着他妻子的衣裳露面，他尚能付之一笑；为他最后一瓶上好的香槟，他却要我的血。这会儿他的双眼再不掩饰了；也用不着单片眼镜去撑；怒得滚圆，从铁青的面具上暴突出来。我别的什么都没看。我不明白那双眼何以那样外突。我也没去想。我说我别的什么都没看——直到我看见拉夫尔斯的脸，出现在那位倒霉军官的肩后。

===Original===
Raffles had crept in unheard while our altercation was at its height, had watched his opportunity, and stolen on his man unobserved by either of us. While my own attention was completely engrossed, he had seized the colonel’s pistol-hand and twisted it behind the colonel’s back until his eyes bulged out as I have endeavored to describe. But the fighting man had some fight in him still; and scarcely had I grasped the situation when he hit out venomously behind with the bottle, which was smashed to bits on Raffles’s shin. Then I threw my strength into the scale; and before many minutes we had our officer gagged and bound in his chair. But it was not one of our bloodless victories. Raffles had been cut to the bone by the broken glass; his leg bled wherever he limped; and the fierce eyes of the bound man followed the wet trail with gleams of sinister satisfaction.

===Chinese===
我们的争执正闹到最凶的当口，拉夫尔斯悄没声地摸了进来，觑准时机，向我们谁都不曾察觉地掩近了他的目标。趁我的注意力整个被钉死，他已攥住上校持枪的手，把它反拧到上校背后，直拧得他双眼暴突，一如我方才竭力描摹的那样。可这员战将身上还剩几分战意；我尚未看清局面，他已拿瓶子朝身后恶狠狠挥击出去，瓶子在拉夫尔斯的胫骨上摔得粉碎。于是我把全身力气压上天平；不出几分钟，我们便把这位军官堵住口，牢牢捆在了他自己的椅子上。可这不是我们那种不见血的胜利。拉夫尔斯被碎玻璃割到了骨头；他一瘸一拐，人走到哪里，血便滴到哪里；而那被捆的人一双凶眼，追着湿漉漉的血迹，闪出阵阵阴森的快意。

===Original===
I thought I had never seen a man better bound or better gagged. But the humanity seemed to have run out of Raffles with his blood. He tore up tablecloths, he cut down blind-cords, he brought the dust-sheets from the drawing-room, and multiplied every bond. The unfortunate man’s legs were lashed to the legs of his chair, his arms to its arms, his thighs and back fairly welded to the leather. Either end of his own ruler protruded from his bulging cheeks﻿—the middle was hidden by his moustache﻿—and the gag kept in place by remorseless lashings at the back of his head. It was a spectacle I could not bear to contemplate at length, while from the first I found myself physically unable to face the ferocious gaze of those implacable eyes. But Raffles only laughed at my squeamishness, and flung a dust-sheet over man and chair; and the stark outline drove me from the room.

===Chinese===
我自问从未见过捆得更牢、堵得更严实的人。可是拉夫尔斯的人性，似乎随着血一同流尽了。他撕桌布，他铰下百叶帘的拉绳，他把客厅的防尘布尽数搬来，把每一道绑缚加了又加。那倒霉人的双腿，捆在椅子腿上；双臂，捆在扶手上；大腿和后背，简直焊死在皮革里。他自己的那把尺子，两端从鼓起的两腮里戳出——中段掩在髭须底下——塞口的物件，则由他后脑上一圈圈毫不留情的绑扎锁牢。这幅光景，我久看不忍；而那双不肯甘休的眼睛里凶狠的逼视，我自第一眼起，便发觉自己在血肉上受不住。可拉夫尔斯只笑话我婆妈，一抖手，一张防尘布连人带椅罩了下去；那僵直的轮廓，把我逐出了房间。

===Original===
It was Raffles at his worst, Raffles as I never knew him before or after﻿—a Raffles mad with pain and rage, and desperate as any other criminal in the land. Yet he had struck no brutal blow, he had uttered no disgraceful taunt, and probably not inflicted a tithe of the pain he had himself to bear. It is true that he was flagrantly in the wrong, his victim as laudably in the right. Nevertheless, granting the original sin of the situation, and given this unforeseen development, even I failed to see how Raffles could have combined greater humanity with any regard for our joint safety; and had his barbarities ended here, I for one should not have considered them an extraordinary aggravation of an otherwise minor offence. But in the broad daylight of the bathroom, which had a ground-glass window but no blind, I saw at once the serious nature of his wound and of its effect upon the man.

===Chinese===
这是拉夫尔斯最不堪的样子，是我此前此后都未曾识得的拉夫尔斯——一个痛疯、怒疯了的拉夫尔斯，同这片国土上任何别的罪犯一样亡命。然而他没有下毒手，没有吐一句下流的嘲弄，他施加的痛楚，多半不及他自己须承受的十分之一。诚然，他错得昭彰，而他的受害者正对得可敬。尽管如此，即便认下这处境中原初的罪孽，即便算上这未曾预见的变局，连我也看不出，拉夫尔斯还能怎样把更大的仁慈，同保全你我共同的安危两全起来；倘若他的暴虐到此为止，单就我而论，还不至于把它看作一桩本属轻罪的案子里格外加重的情节。

===Original===
“It will maim me for a month,” said he; “and if the V.C. comes out alive, the wound he gave may be identified with the wound I’ve got.”

The V.C.! There, indeed, was an aggravation to one illogical mind. But to cast a moment’s doubt upon the certainty of his coming out alive!

===Chinese===
「这伤要叫我瘸上一个月，」他说，「倘若那位勋章得主活着出来，他这伤，兴许会同我这处对得上号。」

维多利亚十字勋章！在一颗不讲逻辑的头脑里，这确是个更教人窝火的节外之枝。可居然有一瞬间，怀疑起他必定活着出来的成算！

===Original===
“Of course he’ll come out,” said I. “We must make up our minds to that.”

“Did he tell you he was expecting the servants or his wife? If so, of course we must hurry up.”

===Chinese===
「他当然会出来，」我说。「咱们必须横下这条心。」

「他跟你说过没有，他在等仆人，还是等他太太？若是说过，咱们自然得赶紧。」

===Original===
“No, Raffles, I’m afraid he’s not expecting anybody. He told me, if he hadn’t looked in for letters, we should have had the place to ourselves another week. That’s the worst of it.”

===Chinese===
「没有，拉夫尔斯，恐怕他谁也没等。他告诉我，要不是为了信件拐进来看一眼，这宅子本该再归咱们独用一个礼拜。糟糕正糟糕在这里。」

===Original===
Raffles smiled as he secured a regular puttee of dust-sheeting. No blood was coming through.

“I don’t agree, Bunny,” said he. “It’s quite the best of it, if you ask me.”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯微微一笑，把防尘布缠成一副货真价实的绑腿，扎紧了。血没有渗出来。

「我不这么看，邦尼，」他说。「要我说，这恰是最好的一条。」

===Original===
“What, that he should die the death?”

“Why not?”

===Chinese===
「什么，让他一命归西才算好？」

「有何不可？」

===Original===
And Raffles stared me out with a hard and merciless light in his clear blue eyes﻿—a light that chilled the blood.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯直盯得我垂下眼去，那双清澈的蓝眼睛里，透着一种冷硬无情的凶光——一种叫血液发凉的光。

===Original===
“If it’s a choice between his life and our liberty, you’re entitled to your decision and I’m entitled to mine, and I took it before I bound him as I did,” said Raffles. “I’m only sorry I took so much trouble if you’re going to stay behind and put him in the way of releasing himself before he gives up the ghost. Perhaps you will go and think it over while I wash my bags and dry ’em at the gas stove. It will take me at least an hour, which will just give me time to finish the last volume of Kinglake.”

===Chinese===
「倘若要在他的性命同咱们的自由之间拣一样，你有权拿你的主意，我也有权拿我的，而在像方才那样捆他之前，我早拿定了，」拉夫尔斯说。「只是可惜我白费了这许多手脚，倘若你打算留下来，扶他赶在他咽气之前自行脱缚的话。也许你可以去把这事想个透，正好我去把裤子洗了，搁煤气灶上烘干。这至少要费我一个钟头，刚好够我读完金莱克的最后一卷。」

===Original===
Long before he was ready to go, however, I was waiting in the hall, clothed indeed, but not in a mind which I care to recall. Once or twice I peered into the dining-room where Raffles sat before the stove, without letting him hear me. He, too, was ready for the street at a moment’s notice; but a steam ascended from his left leg, as he sat immersed in his red volume. Into the study I never went again; but Raffles did, to restore to its proper shelf this and every other book he had taken out and so destroy that clue to the manner of man who had made himself at home in the house. On his last visit I heard him whisk off the dust-sheet; then he waited a minute; and when he came out it was to lead the way into the open air as though the accursed house belonged to him.

===Chinese===
然而，离他收拾停当还早得很，我已候在门厅里，衣裳固然穿上了，心绪却是我无意回首的一种。有一两回，我窥看饭厅——拉夫尔斯坐在煤气灶前——没让他听见。他同样随时可以上街；只是一缕蒸汽从他左腿上腾起，他坐着，整个人浸在他的红皮书里。书房我再没有进去过；拉夫尔斯却进去了，把这一本连同他取出的每一本书放回原架，好抹去那道线索——它泄露了在这宅子里反客为主的，是何等样人。最后一趟，我听见他嗖地掀去防尘布；随后他等了一分钟；待到他出来，便是领路走向户外的空气，仿佛这幢该咒的宅子，本就该归他所有。

===Original===
“We shall be seen,” I whispered at his heels. “Raffles, Raffles, there’s a policeman at the corner!”

===Chinese===
「会给瞧见的，」我贴着他脚跟低语。「拉夫尔斯，拉夫尔斯，街角上有个警察！」

===Original===
“I know him intimately,” replied Raffles, turning, however, the other way. “He accosted me on Monday, when I explained that I was an old soldier of the colonel’s regiment, who came in every few days to air the place and send on any odd letters. You see, I have always carried one or two about me, redirected to that address in Switzerland, and when I showed them to him it was all right. But after that it was no use listening at the letter-box for a clear coast, was it?”

===Chinese===
「我跟他熟极了，」拉夫尔斯答道，脚下却拐向另一头。「星期一他盘问过我，我解释说，我是上校团里的一名老兵，每隔几天进来给宅子通通风，把零星信件转寄走。你瞧，我身上素来揣着一两封改寄瑞士那个地址的信，我拿给他一看，便万事大吉。可打那以后，再伏在信箱口探听街上清净不清净，就没有用处了，对不对？」

===Original===
I did not answer; there was too much to exasperate in these prodigies of cunning which he could never trouble to tell me at the time. And I knew why he had kept his latest feats to himself: unwilling to trust me outside the house, he had systematically exaggerated the dangers of his own walks abroad; and when to these injuries he added the insult of a patronizing compliment on my late disguise, I again made no reply.

===Chinese===
我没有作声；这一件件机变的奇功，他当时从不肯费心告诉我，桩桩件件，都叫人火起。我也明白他为何把这最新的几手瞒着我：他信不过带我出门，便把他自己上街行走的风险，一概添油加醋地夸大；待这份亏待之外，他又添上一层居高临下的恭维，夸起我最近的化装，我便又一次一言不答。

===Original===
“What’s the good of your coming with me?” he asked, when I had followed him across the main stream of Notting Hill.

“We may as well sink or swim together,” I answered sullenly.

===Chinese===
「你跟着我，有什么用处？」他问；那时我已随他穿过了诺丁山（Notting Hill）的主流街道。

「要沉一块沉，要浮一块浮，咱们总归在一处，」我悻悻地答道。

===Original===
“Yes? Well, I’m going to swim into the provinces, have a shave on the way, buy a new kit piecemeal, including a cricket-bag (which I really want), and come limping back to the Albany with the same old strain in my bowling leg. I needn’t add that I have been playing country-house cricket for the last month under an alias; it’s the only decent way to do it when one’s county has need of one. That’s my itinerary, Bunny, but I really can’t see why you should come with me.”

===Chinese===
「是吗？好吧，我打算一路游到外郡去，路上先把脸刮了，零零碎碎置办一身新行头，连一只板球包也在内（那东西我是真想要），然后拖着投球腿上那条老劳损，一瘸一拐回奥尔巴尼公寓去。不必说，这一个月来，我一直顶着化名打乡村别墅的板球赛；本郡用得着你的时候，这是唯一体面的打法。这便是我的行程，邦尼，可我实在看不出，你何苦非要跟着我。」

===Original===
“We may as well swing together!” I growled.

“As you will, my dear fellow,” replied Raffles. “But I begin to dread your company on the drop!”

===Chinese===
「要上绞架，咱们也一同上！」我咬牙道。

「悉听尊便，亲爱的伙计，」拉夫尔斯答道。「不过有你同上绞台，我倒开始发怵了！」

===Original===
I shall hold my pen on that provincial tour. Not that I joined Raffles in any of the little enterprises with which he beguiled the breaks in our journey; our last deed in London was far too great a weight upon my soul. I could see that gallant officer in his chair, see him at every hour of the day and night, now with his indomitable eyes meeting mine ferociously, now a stark outline underneath a sheet. The vision darkened my day and gave me sleepless nights. I was with our victim in all his agony; my mind would only leave him for that gallows of which Raffles had said true things in jest. No, I could not face so vile a death lightly, but I could meet it, somehow, better than I could endure a guilty suspense. In the watches of the second night I made up my mind to meet it halfway, that very morning, while still there might be time to save the life that we had left in jeopardy. And I got up early to tell Raffles of my resolve.

===Chinese===
关于那趟外郡之行，我的笔且按住不表。倒不是我参与了拉夫尔斯沿途借以遣兴的任何一桩小小营生；我们在伦敦的最后那桩事，压在我灵魂上的分量，实在太重了。我看得见那位英武的军官坐在他的椅子上，昼夜每一个时辰都看得见：一时是他那双不屈的眼睛凶狠地迎着我的目光，一时是床单底下一具僵直的轮廓。这幻象白天遮暗我的天日，夜里叫我合不上眼。我们那位受害者受的每一分苦，我都陪着受；我的心神只有一处去得开他——那座绞架，拉夫尔斯曾拿它打趣，句句却是真话。不，那样不堪的一种死，我无法等闲面对；可不知怎的，我能迎着它上去，却熬不住这般负罪的悬心。守夜到第二天夜里，我拿定主意，半路便去迎它——就在当天早晨，趁还来得及，去救那条被我们撂在险地的性命。我一早起身，要把这个决意告诉拉夫尔斯。

===Original===
His room in the hotel where we were staying was littered with clothes and luggage new enough for any bridegroom; I lifted the locked cricket-bag, and found it heavier than a cricket-bag has any right to be. But in the bed Raffles was sleeping like an infant, his shaven self once more. And when I shook him he awoke with a smile.

===Chinese===
我们落脚的那家旅馆里，他的房间摊满了衣物行李，簇新得够得上任何一位新郎；我拎起那只上了锁的板球包，只觉它沉得远超一只板球包应有的分量。可是在床上，拉夫尔斯正睡得像个婴孩，脸又剃净了，恢复了本来面目。我摇了摇他，他一醒，脸上带笑。

===Original===
“Going to confess, eh, Bunny? Well, wait a bit; the local police won’t thank you for knocking them up at this hour. And I bought a late edition which you ought to see; that must be it on the floor. You have a look in the stop-press column, Bunny.”

===Chinese===
「要去自首了，嗯，邦尼？好，稍等一等；这个钟点把本地警察从被窝里敲起来，人家不会领你的情。再说，我买了一份晚版报，你该瞧一瞧；地板上那份准是。你去看看临时快讯那一栏，邦尼。」

===Original===
I found the place with a sunken heart, and this is what I read:

===Chinese===
我心一沉，寻到了那个地方，读到的是这样一段：

===Original===
West-End Outrage

Colonel Crutchley, R.E., V.C., has been the victim of a dastardly outrage at his residence, Peter Street, Campden Hill. Returning unexpectedly to the house, which had been left untenanted during the absence of the family abroad, it was found occupied by two ruffians, who overcame and secured the distinguished officer by the exercise of considerable violence. When discovered through the intelligence of the Kensington police, the gallant victim was gagged and bound hand and foot, and in an advanced stage of exhaustion.

===Chinese===
西区暴行

克拉奇利上校，皇家工兵，维多利亚十字勋章得主，在其坎普登山彼得街（Peter Street）的宅邸内，惨遭一桩卑劣暴行的毒手。上校举家旅居国外，宅邸本已无人居住；上校不期而返，发现宅内盘踞两名歹徒；二人施展相当暴力，将这位杰出军官制服捆缚。经肯辛顿警方明察寻获时，这位英武的受害者口被塞堵、手足被缚，已濒于精力竭尽之境。

===Original===
“Thanks to the Kensington police,” observed Raffles, as I read the last words aloud in my horror. “They can’t have gone when they got my letter.”

“Your letter?”

===Chinese===
「多亏了肯辛顿警方，」我惊骇地把最后几个字念出声时，拉夫尔斯评了一句。「不过他们收到我的信时，可没能立刻出动。」

「你的信？」

===Original===
“I printed them a line while we were waiting for our train at Euston. They must have got it that night, but they can’t have paid any attention to it until yesterday morning. And when they do, they take all the credit and give me no more than you did, Bunny!”

===Chinese===
「咱们在尤斯顿等车的当口，我用印刷体给他们写了一行。他们当天夜里准收到了，只是直到昨天早上，才肯拿正眼瞧它。而他们一瞧，功劳全归了他们自己，给我的谢意，却同你给我的一样少，邦尼！」

===Original===
I looked at the curly head upon the pillow, at the smiling, handsome face under the curls. And at last I understood.

“So all the time you never meant it!”

===Chinese===
我望着枕上那颗鬈发的头，望着鬈发底下那张含笑的俊脸。我终于明白了。

「原来你从头到尾，根本没那个意思！」

===Original===
“Slow murder? You should have known me better. A few hours’ enforced Rest Cure was the worst I wished him.”

===Chinese===
「慢刀子杀人？你该把我看得更透些。我盼他领受的，至多是几个钟头强制奉行的休养疗法。」

===Original===
“You might have told me, Raffles!”

“That may be, Bunny, but you ought certainly to have trusted me!”

===Chinese===
「你本可以告诉我的，拉夫尔斯！」

「这也说得过去，邦尼，可你本来，实在也该信我一信！」

## The Criminologists’ Club / 犯罪学家俱乐部

===Original===
“But who are they, Raffles, and where’s their house? There’s no such club on the list in Whitaker.”

“The Criminologists, my dear Bunny, are too few for a local habitation, and too select to tell their name in Gath. They are merely so many solemn students of contemporary crime, who meet and dine periodically at each other’s clubs or houses.”

===Chinese===
「可他们到底是些什么人，拉夫尔斯？他们的会所又在哪里？《惠特克年鉴》（Whitaker）的名单上可没有这么一家俱乐部。」

「犯罪学家俱乐部（The Criminologists' Club）的诸位，亲爱的邦尼，人数太少，用不着固定会所；格调又太高，不肯把名号在迦特（Gath）外传扬。他们不过是一批研究当代罪案的严肃学者，定期在各人的俱乐部或宅邸里轮流聚会用餐罢了。」

===Original===
“But why in the world should they ask us to dine with them?”

And I brandished the invitation which had brought me hotfoot to the Albany: it was from the Right Hon. the Earl of Thornaby, K.G.; and it requested the honor of my company at dinner, at Thornaby House, Park Lane, to meet the members of the Criminologists’ Club. That in itself was a disturbing compliment: judge then of my dismay on learning that Raffles had been invited too!

===Chinese===
「可他们究竟为什么要请咱们去赴宴呢？」

我扬了扬手里那封请柬——就是它，害我一路火急火燎赶到奥尔巴尼公寓：请柬出自嘉德勋位索恩比伯爵（the Earl of Thornaby, K.G.）阁下，邀我光临公园巷（Park Lane）索恩比府（Thornaby House）的晚宴，与犯罪学家俱乐部的诸位成员一晤。单是这一点，已经是一份令我惶恐的抬举了；后来又听说拉夫尔斯也在被邀之列，我的惊愕更可想而知！

===Original===
“They have got it into their heads,” said he, “that the gladiatorial element is the curse of most modern sport. They tremble especially for the professional gladiator. And they want to know whether my experience tallies with their theory.”

“So they say!”

===Chinese===
「他们满脑子认定，」他说，「角斗士的成分是大多数现代竞技的祸根。他们尤其为职业角斗士捏一把汗。他们还想知道，我的亲身经历是否与他们的理论相合。」

「他们嘴上这么说罢了！」

===Original===
“They quote the case of a league player, *sus. per coll.*, and any number of suicides. It really is rather in my public line.”

“In yours, if you like, but not in mine,” said I. “No, Raffles, they’ve got their eye on us both, and mean to put us under the microscope, or they never would have pitched on *me*.”

===Chinese===
「他们援引某位联赛球员『缢颈而死』（sus. per coll.）的案例，还有数不清的自杀事件。说实在的，这倒真与我公开的行当颇为相干。」

「你的行当，随你怎么说，反正与我的不相干，」我说。「不，拉夫尔斯，他们盯上了咱们两个，存心把咱们放到显微镜底下，否则万万不会挑中*我*。」

===Original===
Raffles smiled on my perturbation.

“I almost wish you were right, Bunny! It would be even better fun than I mean to make it as it is. But it may console you to hear that it was I who gave them your name. I told them you were a far keener criminologist than myself. I am delighted to hear they have taken my hint, and that we are to meet at their gruesome board.”

“If I accept,” said I, with the austerity he deserved.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯瞧着我这副慌张，微微一笑。

「我倒巴不得你说得对，邦尼！那可比我现在打算制造的乐子还要好玩得多。不过，说来或许能给你一点宽慰：你的名字，是我报给他们的。我告诉他们，你是个远比我敏锐的犯罪学家。听说他们采纳了我的举荐，咱们就要在那张阴森的餐桌上会面了，我着实高兴。」

「如果我接受的话，」我说，语气冷得正配得上他。

===Original===
“If you don’t,” rejoined Raffles, “you will miss some sport after both our hearts. Think of it, Bunny! These fellows meet to wallow in all the latest crimes; we wallow with them as though we knew more about it than themselves. Perhaps we don’t, for few criminologists have a soul above murder; and I quite expect to have the privilege of lifting the discussion into our own higher walk. They shall give their morbid minds to the fine art of burgling, for a change; and while we’re about it, Bunny, we may as well extract their opinion of our noble selves. As authors, as collaborators, we will sit with the flower of our critics, and find our own level in the expert eye. It will be a piquant experience, if not an invaluable one; if we are sailing too near the wind, we are sure to hear about it, and can trim our yards accordingly. Moreover, we shall get a very good dinner into the bargain, or our noble host will belie a European reputation.”

===Chinese===
「你要是不去，」拉夫尔斯答道，「就要错过一场正合咱们两人心意的把戏了。想想吧，邦尼！这帮家伙聚在一起，就是为了在最新罪案里打滚；咱们跟他们一起打滚，摆出一副比他们本人还内行的架势。也许咱们真不比他们内行，因为很少有几个犯罪学家的心气高过凶杀案；而我满心指望享有这份殊荣，把讨论提升到咱们自己那门更高级的行当里去。也让他们病态的脑筋换个口味，琢磨琢磨入室盗窃这门精细艺术；再者，既然到了那儿，邦尼，咱们不妨顺便套一套他们对我等高贵人物的看法。以作者的身份，以合著者的身份，咱们将同批评我们的行家里手济济一席，在行家眼中掂掂自己的斤两。这将是一次有滋有味的经历，即便算不上千金难买；倘若咱们行船太贴近风向，准会有人点破，咱们也好相应地调整帆桁。况且，附带着还能饱餐一顿上好的晚宴——不然，咱们那位高贵的东道主，可就有负他的全欧盛名了。」

===Original===
“Do you know him?” I asked.

“We have a pavilion acquaintance, when it suits my lord,” replied Raffles, chuckling. “But I know all about him. He was president one year of the M.C.C., and we never had a better. He knows the game, though I believe he never played cricket in his life. But then he knows most things, and has never done any of them. He has never even married, and never opened his lips in the House of Lords. Yet they say there is no better brain in the August assembly, and he certainly made us a wonderful speech last time the Australians were over. He has read everything and (to his credit in these days) never written a line. All round he is a whale for theory and a sprat for practice﻿—but he looks quite capable of both at crime!”

===Chinese===
「你认识他么？」我问。

「勋爵大人兴致来的时节，我们在看亭里有过几分交情，」拉夫尔斯答道，暗自窃笑。「不过他的底细，我一清二楚。他做过一届马里波恩板球俱乐部（M.C.C.）的主席，再没有比他更出色的了。他精通此道，虽然我相信他这辈子从没打过一场板球。可话又说回来，他什么都精通，却一样都没干过。他连婚都没结过，在上议院也从没开过金口。然而人们说，那庄严的议政厅里，再没有比他更好的头脑；上回澳大利亚队来访，他确实发表了一场精彩的演说。他什么都读过，却（在如今这个年代，这可是他的荣誉）一行字都没写过。总而言之，论理论他是条鲸鱼，论实践他是条小鲱鱼——可若论犯罪，他两样瞧着都干得来！」

===Original===
I now longed to behold this remarkable peer, in the flesh, and with the greater curiosity since another of the things which he evidently never did was to have his photograph published for the benefit of the vulgar. I told Raffles that I would dine with him at Lord Thornaby’s, and he nodded as though I had not hesitated for a moment. I see now how deftly he had disposed of my reluctance. No doubt he had thought it all out before: his little speeches look sufficiently premeditated as I set them down at the dictates of an excellent memory. Let it, however, be borne in mind that Raffles did not talk exactly like a Raffles book: he said the things, but he did not say them in so many consecutive breaths. They were punctuated by puffs from his eternal cigarette, and the punctuation was often in the nature of a line of asterisks, while he took a silent turn up and down his room. Nor was he ever more deliberate than when he seemed most nonchalant and spontaneous. I came to see it in the end. But these were early days, in which he was more plausible to me than I can hope to render him to another human being.

===Chinese===
如今我巴望亲眼见一见这位不同凡响的贵族了，何况他显然从不做的诸般事情中，还有一件是从不肯把照片刊布出来供庸众一睹，这更添了我的好奇。我告诉拉夫尔斯，我愿随他去索恩比勋爵府上赴宴，他点了点头，仿佛我压根儿没有犹豫过似的。如今我才看清，他是何等轻巧地打发掉了我的迟疑。毫无疑问，这一切他早就盘算停当：如今我凭着一副出色的记性把它们笔录下来，他那一段段小小的言谈，看起来也足够像是深思熟虑过的。然而请记住，拉夫尔斯说话，并不当真像「拉夫尔斯的书」里写的那样：话是那些话，却不是一口气连着说出来的。他的话被那支永不离手的香烟吐出的烟雾断开，那断句常常像是一行星号，趁着他在房间里默默踱一个来回。他也从没有比看似最潇洒随性的时候更深思熟虑过。这一点我到后来才看明白。可那时还是早期，他在我眼里何等可信，叫我再也无望把他如实转述给另一个人听。

===Original===
And I saw a good deal of Raffles just then; it was, in fact, the one period at which I can remember his coming round to see me more frequently than I went round to him. Of course he would come at his own odd hours, often just as one was dressing to go out and dine, and I can even remember finding him there when I returned, for I had long since given him a key of the flat. It was the inhospitable month of February, and I can recall more than one cosy evening when we discussed anything and everything but our own malpractices; indeed, there were none to discuss just then. Raffles, on the contrary, was showing himself with some industry in the most respectable society, and by his advice I used the club more than ever.

===Chinese===
也正是在那阵子，我同拉夫尔斯过从甚密；事实上，那是我记忆中唯一的一段时期——他来瞧我的次数，竟比我登门瞧他的还多。当然，他来总挑他自己那几个古怪时辰，常常正赶上人家更衣出门赴宴，我甚至记得有一回赴宴归来还发现他就在我屋里，因为早先我已把公寓的钥匙给了他一把。那是不宜待客的二月天，我却记得不止一个暖融融的夜晚，我们天南地北无所不谈，唯独不谈自家的不法勾当；说实话，那时节也根本没有勾当可谈。拉夫尔斯反倒颇为勤勉地现身于最体面的社交场中，而依着他的主意，我也比以往更常光顾俱乐部。

===Original===
“There is nothing like it at this time of year,” said he. “In the summer I have my cricket to provide me with decent employment in the sight of men. Keep yourself before the public from morning to night, and they’ll never think of you in the still small hours.”

===Chinese===
「一年里这个时节，没有比这更好的办法了，」他说。「到了夏天，我有板球替我在人前挣一份体面的营生。让自己从早到晚在公众眼前亮相，到了万籁俱寂的凌晨时分，就没人会想起你。」

===Original===
Our behavior, in fine, had so long been irreproachable that I rose without misgiving on the morning of Lord Thornaby’s dinner to the other Criminologists and guests. My chief anxiety was to arrive under the aegis of my brilliant friend, and I had begged him to pick me up on his way; but at five minutes to the appointed hour there was no sign of Raffles or his cab. We were bidden at a quarter to eight for eight o’clock, so after all I had to hurry off alone.

===Chinese===
总而言之，我们的行迹太久无可指摘，以致到了索恩比勋爵宴请犯罪学家同人及宾客的那天早晨，我起身时心里没有一丝不安。我主要的心事，是要托庇于我那位光彩照人的朋友一同到场，早先已恳求他顺路来接我；可到了约定时刻差五分，仍不见拉夫尔斯的人影，也不见他的车。请柬上写明七点三刻到场、八点开席，无奈之下，我只得独自匆匆赶去。

===Original===
Fortunately, Thornaby House is almost at the end of my street that was; and it seemed to me another fortunate circumstance that the house stood back, as it did and does, in its own august courtyard; for, as I was about to knock, a hansom came twinkling in behind me, and I drew back, hoping it was Raffles at the last moment. It was not, and I knew it in time to melt from the porch, and wait yet another minute in the shadows, since others were as late as I. And out jumped these others, chattering in stage whispers as they paid their cab.

===Chinese===
所幸索恩比府差不多就坐落在我当年那条街的尽头；而那宅子又坐落幽深——当年如此，如今亦然——独占一方气派的庭院，在我看来也是一件幸事；因为正当我要叩门，一辆双轮马车叮叮当当地赶到了我身后，我连忙退开，指望是拉夫尔斯在最后一刻赶到了。来的却不是他，幸好我认得及时，从门廊悄然隐去，又在暗影里再等了一分钟——原来还有别人和我一样迟。那两位跳下车来，一边付车钱，一边用夸张的耳语交谈。

===Original===
“Thornaby has a bet about it with Freddy Vereker, who can’t come, I hear. Of course, it won’t be lost or won tonight. But the dear man thinks he’s been invited as a cricketer!”

“I don’t believe he’s the other thing,” said a voice as brusque as the first was bland. “I believe it’s all bunkum. I wish I didn’t, but I do!”

“I think you’ll find it’s more than that,” rejoined the other, as the doors opened and swallowed the pair.

===Chinese===
「索恩比为这事跟弗雷迪·维里克（Freddy Vereker）打了赌，听说维里克今晚来不了。当然，这赌注今晚是输不了也赢不了的。可那位宝贝还满以为，自己是作为板球手受邀的呢！」

「我不信他还是那另一样，」另一个声音说，同前一个声音的和气恰成对照，粗暴得多。「我看那全是鬼话。但愿我不信，可我偏偏信！」

「我想你会发现，事情不止于此，」前一位答道，大门随即敞开，把这一对吞了进去。

===Original===
I flung out limp hands and smote the air. Raffles bidden to what he had well called this “gruesome board,” not as a cricketer but, clearly, as a suspected criminal! Raffles wrong all the time, and I right for once in my original apprehension! And still no Raffles in sight﻿—no Raffles to warn﻿—no Raffles, and the clocks striking eight!

===Chinese===
我瘫软地挥出双手，凭空拍击。拉夫尔斯受邀赴他自己妙称的这张「阴森的餐桌」，竟不是作为板球手，而分明是作为嫌疑罪犯！拉夫尔斯一直猜错了，而我最初的忧虑这一回竟猜对了！然而仍旧不见拉夫尔斯——无从示警的拉夫尔斯——不见拉夫尔斯，而时钟正敲八点！

===Original===
Well may I shirk the psychology of such a moment, for my belief is that the striking clocks struck out all power of thought and feeling, and that I played my poor part the better for that blessed surcease of intellectual sensation. On the other hand, I was never more alive to the purely objective impressions of any hour of my existence, and of them the memory is startling to this day. I hear my mad knock at the double doors; they fly open in the middle, and it is like some sumptuous and solemn rite. A long slice of silken-legged lackey is seen on either hand; a very prelate of a butler bows a benediction from the sanctuary steps. I breathe more freely when I reach a book-lined library where a mere handful of men do not overflow the Persian rug before the fire. One of them is Raffles, who is talking to a large man with the brow of a demigod and the eyes and jowl of a degenerate bulldog. And this is our noble host.

===Chinese===
这样一刻的心理，我自然尽可回避不写，因为我相信，那报时的钟声把一切思想与感受的能力都敲了个干净，而多亏那可称庆幸的官能麻痹，我那可怜的角色反倒扮演得更好。另一方面，我此生无论哪个时辰，都不曾对纯粹客观的印象如此清醒，那些印象至今记忆犹新得惊人。我听见自己发疯似的叩响那两扇对开门；门扇从中缝猛地荡开，恍如一场奢华而庄严的典礼。左右两侧各现出一段绸腿的侍者，俨然主教身份的管家在圣坛台阶上躬身祝祷。待我走进四壁藏书的书房，才得以自在呼吸：寥寥数人，围炉而立，还不至于站满那方波斯地毯。其中一个是拉夫尔斯，正同一位大汉交谈——那人有半神的额头，眼睛和下巴却像一条堕落的斗犬。而这位，正是我们高贵的东道主。

===Original===
Lord Thornaby stared at me with inscrutable stolidity as we shook hands, and at once handed me over to a tall, ungainly man whom he addressed as Ernest, but whose surname I never learned. Ernest in turn introduced me, with a shy and clumsy courtesy, to the two remaining guests. They were the pair who had driven up in the hansom; one turned out to be Kingsmill, Q.C.; the other I knew at a glance from his photographs as Parrington, the backwoods novelist. They were admirable foils to each other, the barrister being plump and dapper, with a Napoleonic cast of countenance, and the author one of the shaggiest dogs I have ever seen in evening-clothes. Neither took much stock of me, but both had an eye on Raffles as I exchanged a few words with each in turn. Dinner, however, was immediately announced, and the six of us had soon taken our places round a brilliant little table stranded in a great dark room.

===Chinese===
握手时，索恩比勋爵以莫测的高深木然盯着我，随即把我交给一位高瘦笨拙的汉子——他称他欧内斯特（Ernest），此人姓什么我始终不得而知。欧内斯特又带着羞怯而笨拙的客气，把我引见给余下两位宾客。正是乘双轮马车来的那一对：一位原来是御用大律师金斯米尔（Kingsmill, Q.C.）；另一位我一望便知，照片上见过——荒野小说家帕林顿（Parrington）。他们俩互为绝妙的陪衬：大律师矮胖利落，生着一张拿破仑式的脸；作家则是我在晚礼服里见过的毛发最浓密的汉子。两人都不大把我放在眼里，可当我同他们依次寒暄时，两双眼睛都盯着拉夫尔斯。不过晚宴随即开席，我们六人很快落座，围着一张光彩熠熠的小桌，像搁浅在大间黑屋子的中央。

===Original===
I had not been prepared for so small a party, and at first I felt relieved. If the worst came to the worst, I was fool enough to say in my heart, they were but two to one. But I was soon sighing for that safety which the adage associates with numbers. We were far too few for the confidential duologue with one’s neighbor in which I, at least, would have taken refuge from the perils of a general conversation. And the general conversation soon resolved itself into an attack, so subtly concerted and so artistically delivered that I could not conceive how Raffles should ever know it for an attack, and that against himself, or how to warn him of his peril. But to this day I am not convinced that I also was honored by the suspicions of the club; it may have been so, and they may have ignored me for the bigger game.

===Chinese===
我没料到宴会规模如此之小，起初倒觉宽心。万一落到最坏的地步，他们不过二对一——我心里竟痴到这样盘算。可我很快就巴望起格言里说的「人多保险」来了。我们人实在太少，少得没法同邻座密谈——至少我本可以借那种二人对谈，躲避众人齐谈的风险。而众人的齐谈很快显出了围攻的原形，布置得如此隐秘，出手得如此高明，我实在想不出拉夫尔斯怎能识破那是围攻、而且针对的是他自己，也想不出怎样向他示警。但直到今天我也不敢断定，我自己是否也荣幸地蒙俱乐部猜疑；或许如此，或许他们只盯大猎物，才把我撇在了一边。

===Original===
It was Lord Thornaby himself who fired the first shot, over the very sherry. He had Raffles on his right hand, and the backwoodsman of letters on his left. Raffles was hemmed in by the law on his right, while I sat between Parrington and Ernest, who took the foot of the table, and seemed a sort of feudatory cadet of the noble house.
