But it was the motley lot of us that my lord addressed, as he sat back blinking his baggy eyes.

===Chinese===
打响头一枪的正是索恩比勋爵本人，就在雪利酒端上来的时候。他右首坐着拉夫尔斯，左首是那位文坛荒野客。拉夫尔斯右侧被法律界的人夹住，我则坐在帕林顿和欧内斯特中间；欧内斯特占了桌尾，像是这座贵族门第的一名附庸小宗。可勋爵大人开口时，冲的却是我们这一伙形形色色的人，他仰在椅背上，眨着一双松垂的眼睛。

===Original===
“Mr. Raffles,” said he, “has been telling me about that poor fellow who suffered the extreme penalty last March. A great end, gentlemen, a great end! It is true that he had been unfortunate enough to strike a jugular vein, but his own end should take its place among the most glorious traditions of the gallows. You tell them Mr. Raffles: it will be as new to my friends as it is to me.”

“I tell the tale as I heard it last time I played at Trent Bridge; it was never in the papers, I believe,” said Raffles gravely. “You may remember the tremendous excitement over the Test Matches out in Australia at the time: it seems that the result of the crucial game was expected on the condemned man’s last day on earth, and he couldn’t rest until he knew it. We pulled it off, if you recollect, and he said it would make him swing happy.”

===Chinese===
「拉夫尔斯先生方才正给我讲，」他说，「讲那个去年三月受极刑的可怜家伙。好一个体面的收场，诸位，好一个体面的收场！不错，他是不幸捅破了一根颈静脉，可他自己的收场，也该在绞刑架最光荣的传统中占上一席。你讲给他们听，拉夫尔斯先生：这故事对我的朋友们，同对我一样新鲜。」

「我是上次在特伦特桥（Trent Bridge）打球时听来的，照原样讲给你们；我相信报纸上从没登过，」拉夫尔斯正色道。「诸位也许记得，那时候澳大利亚那边举行的对抗赛（Test Matches）正闹得沸沸扬扬：据说那位死囚在阳世最后一天，正盼着关键一局的结果，不知道了就不得安生。你们还记得吧，我们赢了，他说这能让他快快活活地上绞架。」

===Original===
“Tell ’em what else he said!” cried Lord Thornaby, rubbing his podgy hands.

“The chaplain remonstrated with him on his excitement over a game at such a time, and the convict is said to have replied: ‘Why, it’s the first thing they’ll ask me at the other end of the drop!’ ”

===Chinese===
「跟他们讲讲他还说了什么！」索恩比勋爵喊道，搓着一双胖手。

「狱中牧师见他到了这般田地还为一场球赛激动，劝诫了几句，据说那罪犯答道：『嗐，到了绞索那一头，人家头一句准得问我这个！』」

===Original===
The story was new even to me, but I had no time to appreciate its points. My concern was to watch its effect upon the other members of the party. Ernest, on my left, doubled up with laughter, and tittered and shook for several minutes. My other neighbor, more impressionable by temperament, winced first, and then worked himself into a state of enthusiasm which culminated in an assault upon his shirt-cuff with a joiner’s pencil. Kingsmill, Q.C., beaming tranquilly on Raffles, seemed the one least impressed, until he spoke.

===Chinese===
这故事连我都是头一回听说，可我没工夫品它的妙处。我的心思全在观察席上众人的反应。我左首的欧内斯特笑得直不起腰，咯咯地颤了好几分钟。我另一位邻座天性更易动感情，先是缩了一下，随即自我酝酿出一股热狂，顶点是用一支木匠铅笔对衬衫袖口发起一场猛攻。御用大律师金斯米尔安详地对着拉夫尔斯微笑，似乎是满座最不动声色的一个——直到他开口。

===Original===
“I am glad to hear that,” he remarked in a high bland voice. “I thought that man would die game.”

“Did you know anything about him, then?” inquired Lord Thornaby.

“I led for the Crown,” replied the barrister, with a twinkle. “You might almost say that I measured the poor man’s neck.”

===Chinese===
「听你这么说我很高兴，」他用一种又尖又和气的嗓音说。「我早就料到，那家伙会死得硬气。」

「这么说，你了解他的案底？」索恩比勋爵问道。

「我担任王室方面的主控，」大律师答道，眼里闪着狡黠。「你几乎可以说，那可怜人的脖子是我给量的。」

===Original===
The point must have been quite unpremeditated; it was not the less effective for that. Lord Thornaby looked askance at the callous silk. It was some moments before Ernest tittered and Parrington felt for his pencil; and in the interim I had made short work of my hock, though it was Johannisberger. As for Raffles, one had but to see his horror to feel how completely he was off his guard.

===Chinese===
这句妙语想必全然出于临场发挥，效果却丝毫未减。索恩比勋爵斜眼睨着这位冷酷的大律师。隔了好一会儿，欧内斯特才吃吃笑出声，帕林顿才摸出铅笔；趁这空当，我把杯中的莱茵白葡萄酒一饮而尽，尽管那是约翰内斯贝格（Johannisberger）的名品。至于拉夫尔斯，只消看看他脸上的骇然，便知道他全无半点戒备。

===Original===
“In itself, I have heard, it was not a sympathetic case?” was the remark with which he broke the general silence.

“Not a bit.”

“That must have been a comfort to you,” said Raffles dryly.

===Chinese===
「听说那案子本身，并不怎么引人同情？」他开口，打破了满座的沉默。

「一点也不。」

「那你想必得到了几分安慰，」拉夫尔斯干巴巴地说。

===Original===
“It would have been to me,” vowed our author, while the barrister merely smiled. “I should have been very sorry to have had a hand in hanging Peckham and Solomons the other day.”

“Why Peckham and Solomons?” inquired my lord.

“They never meant to kill that old lady.”

===Chinese===
「换了我，就是莫大的安慰，」我们的作家斩钉截铁地说，大律师只微微一笑。「前几天绞死佩卡姆（Peckham）和所罗门斯（Solomons）那案子，倘若插过一手，我心里会非常过意不去。」

「为什么单说佩卡姆和所罗门斯？」勋爵大人问道。

「他们压根没想害死那位老太太。」

===Original===
“But they strangled her in her bed with her own pillowcase!”

“I don’t care,” said the uncouth scribe. “They didn’t break in for that. They never thought of scragging her. The foolish old person would make a noise, and one of them tied too tight. I call it jolly bad luck on them.”

===Chinese===
「可他们用她自己的枕套，在她床上把她勒死了！」

「我不管，」那位粗野的文士说。「他们不是为了那个闯进去的。他们从没想到要掐死她。是那蠢老太婆要喊叫，其中一个又勒得太紧。我说，这是他们倒了大霉。」

===Original===
“On quiet, harmless, well-behaved thieves,” added Lord Thornaby, “in the unobtrusive exercise of their humble avocation.”

===Chinese===
「砸在安安静静、与世无害、循规蹈矩的窃贼头上，」索恩比勋爵补充道，「他们不过是低调地操持自家卑微的营生罢了。」

===Original===
And, as he turned to Raffles with his puffy smile, I knew that we had reached that part of the programme which had undergone rehearsal: it had been perfectly timed to arrive with the champagne, and I was not afraid to signify my appreciation of that small mercy. But Raffles laughed so quickly at his lordship’s humor, and yet with such a natural restraint, as to leave no doubt that he had taken kindly to my own old part, and was playing the innocent inimitably in his turn, by reason of his very innocence. It was a poetic judgment on old Raffles, and in my momentary enjoyment of the novel situation I was able to enjoy some of the good things of this rich man’s table. The saddle of mutton more than justified its place in the menu; but it had not spoiled me for my wing of pheasant, and I was even looking forward to a sweet, when a further remark from the literary light recalled me from the table to its talk.

===Chinese===
当他带着那张虚胖的笑脸转向拉夫尔斯时，我知道节目已经进行到排演过的那一段了：时机掐算得极准，恰好随香槟一同登场，连这一星小小的仁慈，我也毫不忌讳地表明心领了。可拉夫尔斯对勋爵大人的幽默笑得那样快，又带着那样一种自然的分寸，足见他已然欣然接过了我的老角色，正轮到他扮演无辜者——而且凭着他真正的无辜，演得无人能够企及。这是加在老拉夫尔斯头上的一桩诗意的报应；我在对这新奇处境的一时陶醉里，竟也有滋有味地尝起了这位富人家席面上的几道好菜。羊鞍肉名副其实，全然配得菜单上那一席之地；可它并没有败坏我吃野鸡翅膀的胃口，我甚至正巴望着甜品，这时那位文坛名士又开了口，把我的心从餐桌上召回席间的谈话里。

===Original===
“But, I suppose,” said he to Kingsmill, “it’s ‘many a burglar *you’ve* restored to his friends and his relations’?”

“Let us say many a poor fellow who has been charged with burglary,” replied the cheery Q.C. “It’s not quite the same thing, you know, nor is ‘many’ the most accurate word. I never touch criminal work in town.”

===Chinese===
「不过我猜，」他对金斯米尔说，「『经*你*的手完璧归赵、送还亲友的窃贼，也是数不胜数』吧？」

「咱们还是说，许多被控入室盗窃的可怜家伙吧，」这位乐呵呵的御用大律师答道。「你知道，这不完全是一回事，『许多』也不是最贴切的字眼。在伦敦城里，刑事案子我从来不沾。」

===Original===
“It’s the only kind I should care about,” said the novelist, eating jelly with a spoon.

“I quite agree with you,” our host chimed in. “And of all the criminals one might be called upon to defend, give me the enterprising burglar.”

“It must be the breeziest branch of the business,” remarked Raffles, while I held my breath.

===Chinese===
「要我说，那是我唯一愿意接的案子，」小说家说，一边用勺子吃着果冻。

「我完全同意，」我们的东道主插进来说。「在所有可能被人请去辩护的罪犯里头，要挑就挑那位有进取心的窃贼。」

「那想必是这一行里最轻快逍遥的一支了，」拉夫尔斯说，我屏住了呼吸。

===Original===
But his touch was as light as gossamer, and his artless manner a triumph of even his incomparable art. Raffles was alive to the danger at last. I saw him refuse more champagne, even as I drained my glass again. But it was not the same danger to us both. Raffles had no reason to feel surprise or alarm at such a turn in a conversation frankly devoted to criminology; it must have been as inevitable to him as it was sinister to me, with my fortuitous knowledge of the suspicions that were entertained. And there was little to put him on his guard in the touch of his adversaries, which was only less light than his own.

===Chinese===
可他的落子轻如蛛丝，那副天真的神态，即便在他那无可匹敌的绝技里也算得一大杰作。拉夫尔斯终于觉察了危险。我看见他谢绝再添香槟，恰在同时我又干了一杯。但这危险对我们两人并不相同。在一场公开以犯罪学为题的谈话里出现这样的转折，拉夫尔斯没有任何理由感到意外或警觉；在他看来这必然难免，正如在我听来它阴森可怖——因为我凑巧知道他们心怀的那些猜疑。何况对手们的下手只比他自己轻那么一点，几乎没有什么能叫他起戒心。

===Original===
“I am not very fond of Mr. Sikes,” announced the barrister, like a man who had got his cue.

“But he was prehistoric,” rejoined my lord. “A lot of blood has flowed under the razor since the days of Sweet William.”

===Chinese===
「我不大喜欢赛克斯先生（Mr. Sikes），」大律师宣布道，像个领到了台词提示的演员。

「可他是史前时代的古董了，」勋爵大人应道。「打从『甜威廉』（Sweet William）那个年头以来，剃刀底下流的血已经不少啦。」

===Original===
“True; we have had Peace,” said Parrington, and launched out into such glowing details of that criminal’s last moments that I began to hope the diversion might prove permanent. But Lord Thornaby was not to be denied.

“William and Charles are both dead monarchs,” said he. “The reigning king in their department is the fellow who gutted poor Danby’s place in Bond Street.”

===Chinese===
「不错；后来我们又有了皮斯（Peace），」帕林顿说，随即滔滔不绝地讲起那个罪犯临刑时刻的光辉细节，讲得我开始指望这岔开的话头或许能一岔到底。可索恩比勋爵岂肯善罢甘休。

「威廉和查尔斯都是已故的君王，」他说。「如今在他们那一王国里在位的，是把可怜的丹比（Danby）在邦德街的铺子洗劫一空的那一位。」

===Original===
There was a guilty silence on the part of the three conspirators﻿—for I had long since persuaded myself that Ernest was not in their secret﻿—and then my blood froze.

===Chinese===
三个合谋的人一时心虚地沉默下来——因为我早已说服自己相信，欧内斯特并不知情——随后，我的血凝住了。

===Original===
“I know him well,” said Raffles, looking up.

Lord Thornaby stared at him in consternation. The smile on the Napoleonic countenance of the barrister looked forced and frozen for the first time during the evening. Our author, who was nibbling cheese from a knife, left a bead of blood upon his beard. The futile Ernest alone met the occasion with a hearty titter.

===Chinese===
「我跟他熟得很，」拉夫尔斯说着，抬起了眼。

索恩比勋爵惊惶地直瞪着他。大律师那张拿破仑式的脸上，笑容头一回在今晚显得牵强而僵住。我们的作家正用刀尖抵着奶酪啃，胡子上留下一颗血珠。唯有百无一用的欧内斯特，闻声由衷地咯咯一笑，应付了这个场面。

===Original===
“What!” cried my lord. “*You know the thief?*”

“I wish I did,” rejoined Raffles, chuckling. “No, Lord Thornaby, I only meant the jeweller, Danby. I go to him when I want a wedding present.”

I heard three deep breaths drawn as one before I drew my own.

===Chinese===
「什么！」勋爵大人失声喊道。「*你认识那个窃贼？*」

「但愿我真认识，」拉夫尔斯应道，轻声笑着。「不，索恩比勋爵，我指的只是那位珠宝商丹比。我要买结婚礼物的时候，总去他那儿。」

我听见三个人异口同声地长出一口气，然后才轮到我自己的那口。

===Original===
“Rather a coincidence,” observed our host dryly, “for I believe you also know the Milchester people, where Lady Melrose had her necklace stolen a few months afterward.”

“I was staying there at the time,” said Raffles eagerly. No snob was ever quicker to boast of basking in the smile of the great.

===Chinese===
「这倒是个不小的巧合，」东道主干巴巴地说，「因为据我所知，米尔切斯特（Milchester）那家人你也认识——几个月后，梅尔罗斯夫人（Lady Melrose）的项链正是在他们家失窃的。」

「当时我正住在那儿做客呢，」拉夫尔斯热切地说。天下的势利之徒，没有一个不是这样急于夸耀自己沾沐过显贵的笑颜。

===Original===
“We believe it to be the same man,” said Lord Thornaby, speaking apparently for the Criminologists’ Club, and with much less severity of voice.

“I only wish I could come across him,” continued Raffles heartily. “He’s a criminal much more to my mind than your murderers who swear on the drop or talk cricket in the condemned cell!”

“He might be in the house now,” said Lord Thornaby, looking Raffles in the face. But his manner was that of an actor in an unconvincing part and a mood to play it gamely to the bitter end; and he seemed embittered, as even a rich man may be in the moment of losing a bet.

===Chinese===
「我们认定这是同一个人干的，」索恩比勋爵说，话音显然代表了犯罪学家俱乐部，语气却和缓了许多。

「我只巴望能有缘碰上他，」拉夫尔斯由衷地接着说。「比起你们那些站在绞索下发狠、或者在死囚牢里大谈板球的杀人犯，他这样的罪犯才合我的心意得多！」

「说不定他此刻就在这宅子里，」索恩比勋爵说，直视着拉夫尔斯的脸。可他那副神气，活像一位演着连自己都不信服的角色的演员，偏又打定主意硬着头皮演到底；而且他满面愠色——哪怕是输掉一注赌彩的当口，连富人也难免如此。

===Original===
“What a joke if he were!” cried the Wild West writer.

“Absit omen!” murmured Raffles, in better taste.

===Chinese===
「他要真在这儿，那才叫妙呢！」那位写西部蛮荒的作家喊道。

「但愿此言不验（Absit omen）！」拉夫尔斯低声道，比他雅了一筹。

===Original===
“Still, I think you’ll find it’s a favorite time,” argued Kingsmill, Q.C. “And it would be quite in keeping with the character of this man, so far as it is known, to pay a little visit to the president of the Criminologists’ Club, and to choose the evening on which he happens to be entertaining the other members.”

===Chinese===
「不过，我想你会发现，这本是他最爱下手的时候，」御用大律师金斯米尔争辩道。「就此人为人已知的做派而言，光顾一下犯罪学家俱乐部的会长，还偏偏挑中他款待其他会员的那个晚上，倒是完全合他的脾性。」

===Original===
There was more conviction in this sally than in that of our noble host; but this I attributed to the trained and skilled dissimulation of the bar. Lord Thornaby, however, was not to be amused by the elaboration of his own idea, and it was with some asperity that he called upon the butler, now solemnly superintending the removal of the cloth.

===Chinese===
这一记出击，比我们高贵东道主的那记更令人信服；不过我把这归功于律师界训练有素、炉火纯青的饰伪功夫。然而，自己随口的想法被人这样敷衍成章，索恩比勋爵可并不觉得好玩，他带着几分峻厉唤过了管家——那老先生正一本正经地督着人撤换桌布。

===Original===
“Leggett! Just send upstairs to see if all the doors are open and the rooms in proper order. That’s an awful idea of yours, Kingsmill, or of mine!” added my lord, recovering the courtesy of his order by an effort that I could follow. “We should look fools. I don’t know which of us it was, by the way, who seduced the rest from the main stream of blood into this burglarious backwater. Are you familiar with De Quincey’s masterpiece on *Murder as a Fine Art*, Mr. Raffles?”

“I believe I once read it,” replied Raffles doubtfully.

===Chinese===
「莱格特（Leggett）！打发个人上楼看看，各屋的门是不是都敞着，屋里是否一切如常。金斯米尔，你那个念头可真够呛——要不就是我出的！」勋爵大人又补上一句，靠一股我看得出来的努力，恢复了他们那个阶层的客气。「不然咱们就成了傻子。顺便说一句，当初不知是咱们哪一位，把大伙从血河干流诱进了这条盗窃的回水湾。拉夫尔斯先生，德·昆西（De Quincey）那篇论《谋杀作为一门艺术》（*Murder as a Fine Art*）的杰作，你熟悉么？」

「我想我从前读过，」拉夫尔斯迟疑地答道。

===Original===
“You must read it again,” pursued the earl. “It is the last word on a great subject; all we can hope to add is some baleful illustration or bloodstained footnote, not unworthy of De Quincey’s text. Well, Leggett?”

The venerable butler stood wheezing at his elbow. I had not hitherto observed that the man was an asthmatic.

===Chinese===
「那你必须再读一遍，」伯爵接着说。「那是一个伟大题目上的定论之作；我们所能指望添上的，无非几条凶恶的例证、几条带血的脚注，还要配得上德·昆西的正文才好。怎么样，莱格特？」

年迈的管家站在他手边喘着粗气。我先前竟没留意，这老儿原来有气喘病。

===Original===
“I beg your lordship’s pardon, but I think your lordship must have forgotten.”

The voice came in rude gasps, but words of reproach could scarcely have achieved a finer delicacy.

“Forgotten, Leggett! Forgotten what, may I ask?”

===Chinese===
「请老爷恕罪，不过小老儿想，老爷准是忘了。」

那嗓音一顿一顿地喘着说出来，可即便是责备的话，也很难说得比这更委婉得体了。

「忘了，莱格特！请问，忘了什么？」

===Original===
“Locking your lordship’s dressing-room door behind your lordship, my lord,” stuttered the unfortunate Leggett, in the short spurts of a winded man, a few stertorous syllables at a time. “Been up myself, my lord. Bedroom door﻿—dressing-room door﻿—both locked inside!”

===Chinese===
「老爷离开卧室时，把老爷的更衣室的门反锁上了，老爷，」倒霉的莱格特结结巴巴地说，像跑断了气的人，一次只能迸出几个浑浊的音节。「小老儿亲自上去看过，老爷。卧室门——更衣室门——都从里面锁死了！」

===Original===
But by this time the noble master was in worse case than the man. His fine forehead was a tangle of livid cords; his baggy jowl filled out like a balloon. In another second he had abandoned his place as our host and fled the room; and in yet another we had forgotten ours as his guests and rushed headlong at his heels.

===Chinese===
可到了这个地步，这位高贵的宅主比管家还要狼狈。他漂亮的额头上青筋纠结成一团；松垂的下巴鼓得像个气球。下一秒钟，他已撇下东道主的身份夺门而出；再下一秒，我们也忘了客人的身份，争先恐后地追了出去。

===Original===
Raffles was as excited as any of us now: he outstripped us all. The cherubic little lawyer and I had a fine race for the last place but one, which I secured, while the panting butler and his satellites brought up a respectful rear. It was our unconventional author, however, who was the first to volunteer his assistance and advice.

===Chinese===
这会儿拉夫尔斯比我们谁都兴奋：他一马当先，把所有人甩在了后头。那位天使脸庞的小个子律师同我还为倒数第二名赛了一场好跑，名次归了我；喘着气的管家领着下手们恭恭敬敬地殿后。然而头一个自告奋勇献计献策的，却是我们那位不拘俗套的作家。

===Original===
“No use pushing, Thornaby!” cried he. “If it’s been done with a wedge and gimlet, you may smash the door, but you’ll never force it. Is there a ladder in the place?”

“There’s a rope-ladder somewhere, in case of fire, I believe,” said my lord vaguely, as he rolled a critical eye over our faces. “Where is it kept, Leggett?”

“William will fetch it, my lord.”

===Chinese===
「推是没用的，索恩比！」他喊道。「要是用了楔子和手锥，门可以砸烂，可别想撞开。这宅子里有梯子么？」

「我记得哪儿有一条绳梯，防火灾用的，」勋爵大人含含糊糊地说，一面用挑剔的眼光在我们脸上滚了一圈。「搁在哪儿，莱格特？」

「威廉去取，老爷。」

===Original===
And a pair of noble calves went flashing to the upper regions.

“What’s the good of bringing it down,” cried Parrington, who had thrown back to the wilds in his excitement. “Let him hang it out of the window above your own, and let me climb down and do the rest! I’ll undertake to have one or other of these doors open in two twos!”

===Chinese===
于是一双健美的小腿闪动着，直奔楼上去了。

「搬下来干什么，」帕林顿喊道，激动之下，他整个人退回了蛮荒状态。「叫他把绳梯挂在你楼上那扇窗子外头，让我爬下去，剩下的交给我！我保准转眼就打开这两扇门里的一扇！」

===Original===
The fastened doors were at right angles on the landing which we filled between us. Lord Thornaby smiled grimly on the rest of us, when he had nodded and dismissed the author like a hound from the leash.

“It’s a good thing we know something about our friend Parrington,” said my lord. “He takes more kindly to all this than I do, I can tell you.”

“It’s grist to his mill,” said Raffles charitably.

===Chinese===
两扇锁死的门在楼梯平台上互成直角，我们一伙人挤满了平台。索恩比勋爵点了头，把那位作家像猎犬离弦似的放了出去，然后朝我们余下的人冷冷一笑。

「咱们对这位帕林顿朋友知点底细，倒是件好事，」勋爵大人说。「跟你们说吧，他干这一套，比我在行得多。」

「这正好给他磨坊添谷子呢，」拉夫尔斯厚道地说。

===Original===
“Exactly! We shall have the whole thing in his next book.”

“I hope to have it at the Old Bailey first,” remarked Kingsmill, Q.C.

“Refreshing to find a man of letters such a man of action too!”

===Chinese===
「正是！这桩事下回要原原本本进他的新书了。」

「我指望它先上老贝利（Old Bailey）的法庭，」御用大律师金斯米尔说。

「文人而兼如此的行动家，真是令人耳目一新！」

===Original===
It was Raffles who said this, and the remark seemed rather trite for him, but in the tone there was a something that just caught my private ear. And for once I understood: the officious attitude of Parrington, without being seriously suspicious in itself, was admirably calculated to put a previously suspected person in a grateful shade. This literary adventurer had elbowed Raffles out of the limelight, and gratitude for the service was what I had detected in Raffles’s voice. No need to say how grateful I felt myself. But my gratitude was shot with flashes of unwonted insight. Parrington was one of those who suspected Raffles, or, at all events, one who was in the secret of those suspicions. What if he had traded on the suspect’s presence in the house? What if he were a deep villain himself, and *the* villain of this particular piece? I had made up my mind about him, and that in a tithe of the time I take to make it up as a rule, when we heard my man in the dressing-room. He greeted us with an impudent shout; in a few moments the door was open, and there stood Parrington, flushed and dishevelled, with a gimlet in one hand and a wedge in the other.

===Chinese===
说这话的正是拉夫尔斯。这话由他说出来未免太显平淡，可那语调里有一点什么，偏偏钻进了我这只私心的耳朵。而这一回我懂了：帕林顿那副过分殷勤的姿态，本身虽算不得如何可疑，却妙极地足以把先前受嫌疑的人送进一片令人感激的荫凉里。这位文坛冒险家把拉夫尔斯从众目所聚的光圈里挤了出去，我在拉夫尔斯嗓音里察觉到的，正是对这份效劳的感激。我自己多么感激，更不必说了。可我的感激里，夹着一道道异乎寻常的洞见的闪光。帕林顿是猜疑拉夫尔斯的人们中的一个，至少也是深知那些猜疑内情的一个。倘若他正是利用了那个嫌疑人在场的方便呢？倘若他自己便是个城府极深的大恶棍，而且正是这出戏里*正主儿*那一个呢？我心里已经给他定了案——平常我拿定一个主意所要的工夫，这回只用了十分之一——正在这时，我们听见我那位人物在更衣室里。他用一声放肆的大吼迎接我们；不多时门开了，帕林顿站在那儿，满脸通红，头发散乱，一手一把手锥，一手一块木楔。

===Original===
Within was a scene of eloquent disorder. Drawers had been pulled out, and now stood on end, their contents heaped upon the carpet. Wardrobe doors stood open; empty stud-cases strewed the floor; a clock, tied up in a towel, had been tossed into a chair at the last moment. But a long tin lid protruded from an open cupboard in one corner. And one had only to see Lord Thornaby’s wry face behind the lid to guess that it was bent over a somewhat empty tin trunk.

===Chinese===
屋里是一片无言之语的狼藉。抽屉全被拉了出来，直立于地上，内里的东西堆在地毯上。衣柜门大敞着；空了的衬衫扣盒撒了一地；一座用毛巾裹住的时钟，是最后关头随手抛进椅子的。但一只白铁皮箱的长盖子，从墙角敞开的柜橱里探了出来。只消看看索恩比勋爵在箱盖后那张扭曲的脸，便可猜出那盖子正俯在一口相当空了的铁皮箱上。

===Original===
“What a rum lot to steal!” said he, with a twitch of humor at the corners of his canine mouth. “My peer’s robes, with coronet complete!”

We rallied round him in a seemly silence. I thought our scribe would put in his word. But even he either feigned or felt a proper awe.

===Chinese===
「偷这么一堆怪家伙，可真怪！」他说，那张犬类的嘴角抽出一丝幽默。「我的贵族朝服，连同冠冕一整套！」

我们得体地沉默着，围拢在他身旁。我以为我们的文士总要插上一句。可就连他，也不知是装是真心，露出了几分应有的敬畏。

===Original===
“You may say it was a rum place to keep ’em,” continued Lord Thornaby. “But where would you gentlemen stable your white elephants? And these were elephants as white as snow; by Jove, I’ll job them for the future!”

===Chinese===
「你们尽可以笑话，搁那地方也真怪，」索恩比勋爵接着说。「可诸位先生，你们会把自家的白象安置在哪儿呢？何况这是白得像雪的白象；老天在上，往后我可要好好摆布它们了！」

===Original===
And he made merrier over his loss than any of us could have imagined the minute before; but the reason dawned on me a little later, when we all trooped downstairs, leaving the police in possession of the theatre of crime. Lord Thornaby linked arms with Raffles as he led the way. His step was lighter, his gayety no longer sardonic; his very looks had improved. And I divined the load that had been lifted from the hospitable heart of our host.

===Chinese===
他为自己这场损失打趣的兴致，比我们任何人一分钟前所能想象的都高；其中的缘故，我是稍晚一点才恍然的：那时我们一行人下楼，把犯罪现场留给了警察接管。索恩比勋爵挽起拉夫尔斯的手臂，走在头里。他的脚步轻快了，他的欢快不再是冷嘲式的；连他的容色都舒展开了。我猜到了从我们这位东道主好客的心头卸下的，是怎样一副重担。

===Original===
“I only wish,” said he, “that this brought us any nearer to the identity of the gentleman we were discussing at dinner, for, of course, we owe it to all our instincts to assume that it was he.”

“I wonder!” said old Raffles, with a foolhardy glance at me.

===Chinese===
「我只感到可惜，」他说，「这件事没能让我们离席间议论的那位先生的真身份更近一步；当然，凭我们的全部直觉，理应认定就是他干的。」

「那可难说！」老拉夫尔斯说，鲁莽地朝我瞟了一眼。

===Original===
“But I’m sure of it, my dear sir,” cried my lord. “The audacity is his and his alone. I look no further than the fact of his honoring me on the one night of the year when I endeavor to entertain my brother Criminologists. That’s no coincidence, sir, but a deliberate irony, which would have occurred to no other criminal mind in England.”

“You may be right,” Raffles had the sense to say this time, though I flattered myself it was my face that made him.

===Chinese===
「可我对这点确信不疑，亲爱的先生，」勋爵大人嚷道。「这份胆大妄为，非他莫属，只此一家。只看他偏偏拣我一年之中款待犯罪学家同仁的那一夜光临寒舍，此外我不必再看。那绝不是巧合，先生，那是存心的冷嘲，全英国的罪犯头脑里，再没有第二个想得出。」

「也许你说得对，」拉夫尔斯这回总算识相，这样说——尽管我私心自诩，是我的脸色促成的。

===Original===
“What is still more certain,” resumed our host, “is that no other criminal in the world would have crowned so delicious a conception with so perfect an achievement. I feel sure the inspector will agree with us.”

The policeman in command had knocked and been admitted to the library as Lord Thornaby spoke.

===Chinese===
「更可以断定的是，」我们的东道主接着说，「普天下再没有哪个罪犯，能把这样一个美妙的构想，完成得如此无懈可击。我想巡官一定会赞同我们。」

索恩比勋爵说话的当口，负责的警官已叩门进入书房。

===Original===
“I didn’t hear what you said, my lord.”

“Merely that the perpetrator of this amusing outrage can be no other than the swell mobsman who relieved Lady Melrose of her necklace and poor Danby of half his stock a year or two ago.”

===Chinese===
「老爷刚才的话，我没听清。」

「我只是说，干下这桩趣案的，必是那位衣冠窃贼（swell mobsman）无疑——一两年前，劫走梅尔罗斯夫人项链、搬空可怜的丹比半店铺存货的，正是此君。」

===Original===
“I believe your lordship has hit the nail on the head.”

“The man who took the Thimblely diamonds and returned them to Lord Thimblely, you know.”

===Chinese===
「小人相信，老爷正说中了要害。」

「就是取走欣布利（Thimblely）家钻石、又把它们还给欣布利勋爵的那个人，您知道。」

===Original===
“Perhaps he’ll treat your lordship the same.”

“Not he! I don’t mean to cry over *my* spilt milk. I only wish the fellow joy of all he had time to take. Anything fresh upstairs by the way?”

“Yes, my lord: the robbery took place between a quarter past eight and the half-hour.”

===Chinese===
「说不定他也会照样回敬老爷一回。」

「他才不呢！*我*洒了的这摊奶，我没打算哭。只愿那家伙拿走什么都是快活的。顺便问一句，楼上有新发现没有？」

「有，老爷：盗窃案发生在八点一刻到八点半之间。」

===Original===
“How on earth do you know?”

“The clock that was tied up in the towel had stopped at twenty past.”

===Chinese===
「这你是怎么知道的？」

「裹在毛巾里的那座钟，停在八点二十了。」

===Original===
“Have you interviewed my man?”

“I have, my lord. He was in your lordship’s room until close on the quarter, and all was as it should be when he left it.”

===Chinese===
「我的贴身仆人，你问过了么？」

「问过了，老爷。他在老爷房里一直待到将近八点一刻，离开时一切如常。」

===Original===
“Then do you suppose the burglar was in hiding in the house?”

“It’s impossible to say, my lord. He’s not in the house now, for he could only be in your lordship’s bedroom or dressing-room, and we have searched every inch of both.”

===Chinese===
「那么你认为窃贼是事先藏在这宅子里的？」

「这不好说，老爷。眼下他不在宅子里，因为他若还在，只可能在老爷的卧室或更衣室，而这两处我们已一寸一寸搜过了。」

===Original===
Lord Thornaby turned to us when the inspector had retreated, caressing his peaked cap.

“I told him to clear up these points first,” he explained, jerking his head toward the door. “I had reason to think my man had been neglecting his duties up there. I am glad to find myself mistaken.”

===Chinese===
巡官抚着自己的尖顶帽退下去后，索恩比勋爵转向我们。

「这几个问题，是我让他先查清楚的，」他解释道，朝门口一摆头。「我有理由疑心我那个人玩忽楼上的职守。很高兴是我弄错了。」

===Original===
I ought to have been no less glad to see my own mistake. My suspicions of our officious author were thus proved to have been as wild as himself. I owed the man no grudge, and yet in my human heart I felt vaguely disappointed. My theory had gained color from his behavior ever since he had admitted us to the dressing-room; it had changed all at once from the familiar to the morose; and only now was I just enough to remember that Lord Thornaby, having tolerated those familiarities as long as they were connected with useful service, had administered a relentless snub the moment that service had been well and truly performed.

===Chinese===
我本该同样高兴地看到自己的错误。我对我们那位热心过头的作家的猜疑，就此证明跟他本人一样荒唐。我对他并无芥蒂，可在凡人的心里，我却隐隐觉得失望。自从他把我们让进更衣室，我的理论一直从他的举止里汲取颜色；那举止先是熟不拘礼，忽而一变而为郁郁寡欢；直到此刻，我才公道地想起：索恩比勋爵先前容忍那些放肆，只因它们连着有用的效力；效力一旦十足兑现，他便毫不留情地赏了一记冷面。

===Original===
But if Parrington was exonerated in my mind, so also was Raffles reinstated in the regard of those who had entertained a far graver and more dangerous hypothesis. It was a miracle of good luck, a coincidence among coincidences, which had whitewashed him in their sight at the very moment when they were straining the expert eye to sift him through and through. But the miracle had been performed, and its effect was visible in every face and audible in every voice. I except Ernest, who could never have been in the secret; moreover, that gay Criminologist had been palpably shaken by his first little experience of crime. But the other three vied among themselves to do honor where they had done injustice. I heard Kingsmill, Q.C., telling Raffles the best time to catch him at chambers, and promising a seat in court for any trial he might ever like to hear. Parrington spoke of a presentation set of his books, and in doing homage to Raffles made his peace with our host. As for Lord Thornaby, I did overhear the name of the Athenaeum Club, a reference to his friends on the committee, and a whisper (as I thought) of Rule II.

===Chinese===
但如果说帕林顿在我心里洗清了嫌疑，那么拉夫尔斯也同样在另一些人眼里恢复了名誉——他们先前怀抱的，可是个严重得多、凶险得多的假设。那是一场好运的奇迹，是巧合中的巧合，恰恰在他们绷紧行家的眼睛、要把他从头到尾筛上一遍的当口，把他漂洗得雪白，呈到了他们眼前。奇迹既已铸成，其效力便写在每张脸上、响在每个声音里。我且把欧内斯特除外，他断无知情之理；何况这位乐天的犯罪学家，经此初次小小的罪案体验，分明惊魂未定。可其余三位却争相向他们错怪过的人赔情致敬。我听见御用大律师金斯米尔告诉拉夫尔斯，什么时辰到律师议事室最易堵到他，还许诺日后无论哪堂审判，只要他想听，都给他留一个庭上座位。帕林顿谈起要馈赠一套题签的著作，在向拉夫尔斯致敬的同时，也与我们的东道主言归于好。至于索恩比勋爵，我确曾隐约听到雅典娜俱乐部（Athenaeum Club）的名字，听到他提起委员会里的几位朋友，还有（我以为是）关于二号规则的一声低语。

===Original===
The police were still in possession when we went our several ways, and it was all that I could do to drag Raffles up to my rooms, though, as I have said, they were just round the corner. He consented at last as a lesser evil than talking of the burglary in the street; and in my rooms I told him of his late danger and my own dilemma, of the few words I had overheard in the beginning, of the thin ice on which he had cut fancy figures without a crack. It was all very well for him. He had never realized his peril. But let him think of me﻿—listening, watching, yet unable to lift a finger﻿—unable to say one warning word.

===Chinese===
我们各自散去时，警察仍未撤离；我好容易才把拉夫尔斯拖上楼到我的住处——虽说如前所述，我的住处就在拐角上。他总算应承了，权当两害相权取其轻，总强过在街上谈这桩窃案；到了我屋里，我把他方才的危险和我自己的两难一五一十告诉他：起初我偷听到的寥寥数语，他脚下那层薄冰——他在上面滑出了一连串花样，冰面竟无一声脆响。他倒是好心态。他压根儿没意识到自己的凶险。可他也该想想我——听着，望着，却抬不起一根指头——说不出一句示警的话。

===Original===
Raffles suffered me to finish, but a weary sigh followed the last symmetrical whiff of a Sullivan which he flung into my fire before he spoke.

“No, I won’t have another, thank you. I’m going to talk to you, Bunny. Do you really suppose I didn’t see through these wiseacres from the first?”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯听任我讲完，只是他开口之前，把一支沙利文的末一口匀整的烟吸尽，将烟卷掷进我的炉火，随之而来的是一声倦叹。

「不，我不再来一支了，谢谢。我要跟你谈谈，邦尼。你真以为，这些自作聪明的家伙我开头就没看穿？」

===Original===
I flatly refused to believe he had done so before that evening. Why had he never mentioned his idea to me? It had been quite the other way, as I indignantly reminded Raffles. Did he mean me to believe he was the man to thrust his head into the lion’s mouth for fun? And what point would there be in dragging me there to see the fun?

===Chinese===
我断然不信他在那天晚上之前就看穿了。那他为什么从没向我提过他的想法？恰恰相反——我愤愤地提醒拉夫尔斯。他难道要我相信，他是那种把脑袋伸进狮子嘴里取乐的人？再说，拖我到那儿去瞧这场热闹，又图的什么？

===Original===
“I might have wanted you, Bunny. I very nearly did.”

“For my face?”

“It has been my fortune before tonight, Bunny. It has also given me more confidence than you are likely to believe at this time of day. You stimulate me more than you think.”

===Chinese===
「我说不定用得上你，邦尼。差一点就当真用上了。」

「就为看我这张脸？」

「今夜之前，这张脸就是我的财神，邦尼。它给我的信心，也远超你如今所能相信的。你对我的激励，比你想的要大。」

===Original===
“Your gallery and your prompter’s box in one?”

“Capital, Bunny! But it was no joking matter with me either, my dear fellow; it was touch-and-go at the time. I might have called on you at any moment, and it was something to know I should not have called in vain.”

===Chinese===
「一座楼座，兼一只提词席？」

「妙极，邦尼！不过对我也绝非儿戏，亲爱的伙计；当时实在险到了毫巅。我随时都可能叫到你，而知道这一叫必不会落空，总是件叫人心里有底的事。」

===Original===
“But what to do, Raffles?”

“Fight our way out and bolt!” he answered, with a mouth that meant it, and a fine gay glitter of the eyes.

I shot out of my chair.

===Chinese===
「可叫我去干什么呢，拉夫尔斯？」

「杀出一条路，然后跑！」他答道，说这话的嘴是认真的，眼里闪着明快而欢快的光。

我从椅子里弹了起来。

===Original===
“You don’t mean to tell me you had a hand in the job?”

“I had the only hand in it, my dear Bunny.”

===Chinese===
「你该不是要说，这案子有你一份？」

「岂止一份，亲爱的邦尼，这只手是独一份。」

===Original===
“Nonsense! You were sitting at table at the time. No, but you may have taken some other fellow into the show. I always thought you would!”

“One’s quite enough, Bunny,” said Raffles dryly; he leaned back in his chair and took out another cigarette. And I accepted of yet another from his case; for it was no use losing one’s temper with Raffles; and his incredible statement was not, after all, to be ignored.

===Chinese===
「胡说！案发的时候你正坐在席上呢。不对——你准是另找了什么人入伙。我早就料到你会！」

「一个已经足够多了，邦尼，」拉夫尔斯干巴巴地说；他仰回椅背，又摸出一支烟。我也从他的烟盒里再取了一支；对拉夫尔斯发脾气是没有用的；何况他那番匪夷所思的话，终究也不能置之不理。

===Original===
“Of course,” I went on, “if you really had brought off this thing on your own, I should be the last to criticise your means of reaching such an end. You have not only scored off a far superior force, which had laid itself out to score off you, but you have put them in the wrong about you, and they’ll eat out of your hand for the rest of their days. But don’t ask me to believe that you’ve done all this alone! By George,” I cried, in a sudden wave of enthusiasm, “I don’t care how you’ve done it or who has helped you. It’s the biggest thing you ever did in your life!”

===Chinese===
「当然，」我接着说，「倘若你当真独力办成了这桩事，要挑你手段的毛病，我比谁都靠后。你不仅赢了一支远占上风、本已布好阵要赢你的人马，还叫他们明白从前错看了你——往后余生，他们都要在你手心里讨食了。可你别要我相信，这一切全是你一个人干的！老天在上，」我一阵热情涌起，喊道，「你怎么干成的、有谁帮衬，我全不计较。这是你此生干下的最大一票！」

===Original===
And certainly I had never seen Raffles look more radiant, or better pleased with the world and himself, or nearer that elation which he usually left to me.

“Then you shall hear all about it, Bunny, if you’ll do what I ask you.”

“Ask away, old chap, and the thing’s done.”

===Chinese===
诚然，我也从没见过拉夫尔斯如此容光焕发，对这世界和他自己如此心满意足，如此接近那种他平日总让给我的意气飞扬。

「那你就听我一五一十道来吧，邦尼——只要你依我一句话。」

「尽管吩咐，老伙计，赴汤蹈火。」

===Original===
“Switch off the electric lights.”

“All of them?”

===Chinese===
「把电灯关掉。」

「全关了？」

===Original===
“I think so.”

“There, then.”

===Chinese===
「我想是的。」

「好，关了。」

===Original===
“Now go to the back window and up with the blind.”

“Well?”

===Chinese===
「现在到后窗去，把百叶帘拉起来。」

「然后呢？」

===Original===
“I’m coming to you. Splendid! I never had a look so late as this. It’s the only window left alight in the house!”

His cheek against the pane, he was pointing slightly downward and very much aslant through a long lane of mews to a little square light like a yellow tile at the end. But I had opened the window and leaned out before I saw it for myself.

===Chinese===
「这就过来。妙极了！这么晚的时辰，我从没能瞧上一眼。整座宅子，就剩那一扇窗还亮着灯！」

他面颊贴着窗玻璃，微微朝下、又极斜地指着一条长长的马厩巷，巷子尽头有一点四四方方的小灯光，像一块黄瓦。不等他指点分明，我已推窗探身，亲眼望见了它。

===Original===
“You don’t mean to say that’s Thornaby House?”

I was not familiar with the view from my back windows.

===Chinese===
「你不会是说，那就是索恩比府？」

我对自家后窗望出去的景色并不熟悉。

===Original===
“Of course I do, you rabbit! Have a look through your own race-glass. It has been the most useful thing of all.”

===Chinese===
「正是，你这只兔子！用你那副观赛镜自己瞧瞧。它一直是顶顶有用的家伙。」

===Original===
But before I had the glass in focus more scales had fallen from my eyes; and now I knew why I had seen so much of Raffles these last few weeks, and why he had always come between seven and eight o’clock in the evening, and waited at this very window, with these very glasses at his eyes. I saw through them sharply now. The one lighted window pointed out by Raffles came tumbling into the dark circle of my vision. I could not see into the actual room, but the shadows of those within were quite distinct on the lowered blind. I even thought a black thread still dangled against the square of light. It was, it must be, the window to which the intrepid Parrington had descended from the one above.

===Chinese===
可望远镜还没对准焦点，我眼睛上又掉下层层鳞翳；于是我明白了，这几周何以总见得着拉夫尔斯，何以他总在晚上七八点之间来，总守在这扇窗前，把这一副镜片架在眼前。此刻透过镜片，我看得分明。拉夫尔斯指出的那扇独亮的窗，一下跌进我视野的暗圈里。屋内的实景我看不到，但放下的帘子上，里面人影绰绰，清清楚楚。我甚至觉得，那方光亮上还垂着一缕黑线。那正是——必是——无畏的帕林顿从上面一扇窗攀下来的那扇窗。

===Original===
“Exactly!” said Raffles in answer to my exclamation. “And that’s the window I have been watching these last few weeks. By daylight you can see the whole lot above the ground floor on this side of the house; and by good luck one of them is the room in which the master of the house arrays himself in all his nightly glory. It was easily spotted by watching at the right time. I saw him shaved one morning before you were up! In the evening his valet stays behind to put things straight; and that has been the very mischief. In the end I had to find out something about the man, and wire to him from his girl to meet her outside at eight o’clock. Of course he pretends he was at his post at the time: that I foresaw, and did the poor fellow’s work before my own. I folded and put away every garment before I permitted myself to rag the room.”

===Chinese===
「一点不差！」拉夫尔斯应着我的惊叹说。「这几周我守望的，正是那扇窗。白天里，这一面宅子底层以上的窗户尽收眼底；天可怜见，其中一扇正是宅主每夜锦袍加身、尽显威仪的屋子。挑对了时辰守望，一眼就能认出来。有天早晨你还没起床，我瞧见他刮脸来着！到了晚上，他的贴身仆人留下收拾归置；这才是最误事的一环。到头来我只得摸一摸这仆人的底，冒他的情人给他发电报，约他八点钟在外面会面。他自然一口咬定当时守在岗位上：这我早料到了，所以先替这可怜虫干完他该干的活，再干自己的。我先把每件衣裳叠好归了位，才容许自己去翻搅那间屋子。」

===Original===
“I wonder you had time!”

“It took me one more minute, and it put the clock on exactly fifteen. By the way, I did that literally, of course, in the case of the clock they found. It’s an old dodge, to stop a clock and alter the time; but you must admit that it looked as though one had wrapped it up all ready to cart away. There was thus any amount of prima facie evidence of the robbery having taken place when we were all at table. As a matter of fact, Lord Thornaby left his dressing-room one minute, his valet followed him the minute after, and I entered the minute after that.”

===Chinese===
「真亏你还有这工夫！」

「那不过多费我一分钟，却把时刻表恰好顶在了整一刻钟上。顺带说一句，对于他们找到的那座钟，我这话可是照字面办的。停掉钟、拨改时刻，本是老掉牙的花招；可你得承认，那模样正像有人把钟裹好、就待搬走。这样一来，一望而知全是现成的表面铁证，足证盗窃案发时我们全都安坐席上。事实的次序是：索恩比勋爵前脚离开更衣室，他的仆人隔一分钟跟出，我再隔一分钟进屋。」

===Original===
“Through the window?”

“To be sure. I was waiting below in the garden. You have to pay for your garden in town, in more ways than one. You know the wall, of course, and that jolly old postern? The lock was beneath contempt.”

===Chinese===
「从窗子进去的？」

「那还用说。我就在下面的花园里候着。在城里，一座花园你要付出多方面的代价。那堵墙你是知道的，还有那道妙极的古老后门？那把锁，简直不值一哂。」

===Original===
“But what about the window? It’s on the first floor, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「可窗子怎么办？它可在二层上呢，是不是？」

===Original===
Raffles took up the cane which he had laid down with his overcoat. It was a stout bamboo with a polished ferule. He unscrewed the ferule, and shook out of the cane a diminishing series of smaller canes, exactly like a child’s fishing-rod, which I afterward found to have been their former state. A double hook of steel was now produced and quickly attached to the tip of the top joint; then Raffles undid three buttons of his waistcoat; and lapped round and round his waist was the finest of Manila ropes, with the neatest of foot-loops at regular intervals.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯拿起他那根手杖——同大衣一起撂下的。是一根粗壮的竹杖，装着抛光的金属箍头。他旋下箍头，从杖身里抖出一节比一节细的成套小杖，活脱脱一支儿童钓竿——我后来才发现，它们先前正是儿童钓竿。跟着又取出一枚钢制的双头钩，飞快地装上顶节的尖端；随后拉夫尔斯解开马甲的三粒扣；他那腰间一圈圈缠着的，是上好的马尼拉麻绳（Manila ropes），绳上每隔一段，结着一只只利落的小脚环。

===Original===
“Is it necessary to go any further?” asked Raffles when he had unwound the rope. “This end is made fast to that end of the hook, the other half of the hook fits over anything that comes its way, and you leave your rod dangling while you swarm up your line. Of course, you must know what you’ve got to hook on to; but a man who has had a porcelain bath fixed in his dressing-room is the man for me. The pipes were all outside, and fixed to the wall in just the right place. You see I had made a reconnaissance by day in addition to many by night; it would hardly have been worth while constructing my ladder on chance.”

===Chinese===
「还有必要再往下讲么？」拉夫尔斯解完绳子，问道。「这一头拴牢在钩子的那一头上，钩子的另一半扣住够得着的任何东西，你让钓竿悬着，自己顺绳攀上去。当然，你得先知道有什么可钩；而一位在更衣室里装了瓷浴缸的先生，正合我的意。那些管子全在室外，恰好装在墙上正合适的位置。你瞧，我夜里踩勘过许多回，白天也踩勘过；单凭运气来架我这副梯子，未免太不值得。」

===Original===
“So you made it on purpose!”

“My dear Bunny,” said Raffles, as he wound the hemp girdle round his waist once more, “I never did care for ladder work, but I always said that if I ever used a ladder it should be the best of its kind yet invented. This one may come in useful again.”

===Chinese===
「这么说，这副家什是特意打造的！」

「亲爱的邦尼，」拉夫尔斯说，一边把那根麻绳腰带重新往腰上缠，「我一向不爱干爬梯子的活，可我也一直说，若有一日用梯，那必是迄今所发明同类中的极品。这一副，往后兴许还用得着。」

===Original===
“But how long did the whole thing take you?”

“From mother earth, to mother earth? About five minutes, tonight, and one of those was spent in doing another man’s work.”

===Chinese===
「可整套活计前后花了你多久？」

「从大地母亲怀里来，回大地母亲怀里去？今晚嘛，大约五分钟，其中一分钟还花在替别人干活上。」

===Original===
“What!” I cried. “You mean to tell me you climbed up and down, in and out, and broke into that cupboard and that big tin box, and wedged up the doors and cleared out with a peer’s robes and all the rest of it in five minutes?”

“Of course I don’t, and of course I didn’t.”

===Chinese===
「什么！」我叫道。「你是说，你爬上爬下、钻进钻出，撬开那口橱柜和那口大白铁箱，用楔子顶死了两扇门，还卷了一位贵族的朝服和其余家当脱身——前后只用了五分钟？」

「我当然不是这个意思，也当然没这么干。」

===Original===
“Then what do you mean, and what did you do?”

“Made two bites at the cherry, Bunny! I had a dress rehearsal in the dead of last night, and it was then I took the swag. Our noble friend was snoring next door all the time, but the effort may still stand high among my small exploits, for I not only took all I wanted, but left the whole place exactly as I found it, and shut things after me like a good little boy. All that took a good deal longer; tonight I had simply to rag the room a bit, sweep up some studs and links, and leave ample evidence of having boned those rotten robes *tonight*. That, if you come to think of it, was what you writing chaps would call the quintessential Q.E.F. I have not only shown these dear Criminologists that I couldn’t possibly have done this trick, but that there’s some other fellow who could and did, and whom they’ve been perfect asses to confuse with me.”

===Chinese===
「那你是什么意思？你到底干了什么？」

「樱桃分两口吃，邦尼！昨夜深更半夜，我搞了一场彩排，赃物就是在那时取的。咱们那位高贵的朋友整夜在隔壁打鼾，但这份功业在我小小业绩簿上仍可名列前茅：我不但取走了要取的一切，还让整间屋子原样如初，走时件件随手关好，乖乖得像个好孩子。那些功夫花得可长得多；今晚我只需把屋子稍加翻搅，扫拢些纽扣和袖扣，再留下充分证据，证明那身破朝服是*今晚*顺走的。细想想，这正是你们这班耍笔杆的所谓登峰造极的 Q.E.F.（quod erat faciendum，功毕于此）。我不仅向这些亲爱的犯罪学家们证明了此计断非我所能为，还证明了另有其人能为、且已为之——他们竟把此君同我混为一谈，实在是蠢到了家。」

===Original===
You may figure me as gazing on Raffles all this time in mute and rapt amazement. But I had long been past that pitch. If he had told me now that he had broken into the Bank of England, or the Tower, I should not have disbelieved him for a moment. I was prepared to go home with him to the Albany and find the regalia under his bed. And I took down my overcoat as he put on his. But Raffles would not hear of my accompanying him that night.

===Chinese===
你不妨想象，这段时间我始终目瞪口呆、如痴如醉地凝视着拉夫尔斯。可我早越过了那个阶段。此刻哪怕他说他闯进过英格兰银行，或是伦敦塔，我也不会有片刻怀疑。就算随他回奥尔巴尼公寓、在他床底下翻出一整套王权珍器，我也有了心理准备。于是他穿大衣，我也取下了我的。可那天夜里，拉夫尔斯说什么也不许我相陪。

===Original===
“No, my dear Bunny, I am short of sleep and fed up with excitement. You mayn’t believe it﻿—you may look upon me as a plaster devil﻿—but those five minutes you wot of were rather too crowded even for my taste. The dinner was nominally at a quarter to eight, and I don’t mind telling you now that I counted on twice as long as I had. But no one came until twelve minutes to, and so our host took his time. I didn’t want to be the last to arrive, and I was in the drawing-room five minutes before the hour. But it was a quicker thing than I care about, when all is said.”

===Chinese===
「不，亲爱的邦尼，我缺觉，刺激也受够了。你尽管不信——你尽可以把我看作一尊不知疲倦的石膏魔鬼——可你知道的那五分钟，挤得实在太满，连我都嫌太赶。晚宴名义上七点三刻开席，如今我不妨告诉你，我原本指望的时间，是实有的两倍。可到差十二分八点才来了头一位客人，我们的东道主也就慢慢腾腾。我不想最后一个到场，提前五分钟进了客厅。但说到底，这事比我乐意的还是仓促了些。」

===Original===
And his last word on the matter, as he nodded and went his way, may well be mine; for one need be no criminologist, much less a member of the Criminologists’ Club, to remember what Raffles did with the robes and coronet of the Right Hon. the Earl of Thornaby, K.G. He did with them exactly what he might have been expected to do by the gentlemen with whom he had foregathered; and he did it in a manner so characteristic of himself as surely to remove from their minds the last aura of the idea that he and himself were the same person. Carter Paterson was out of the question, and any labelling or addressing to be avoided on obvious grounds. But Raffles stabled the white elephants in the cloakroom at Charing Cross﻿—and sent Lord Thornaby the ticket.

===Chinese===
他点头作别、扬长而去，这桩事上他的最后一句话，也不妨借来作我的收梢；因为用不着是犯罪学家，更用不着是犯罪学家俱乐部的会员，人们也记得拉夫尔斯把嘉德勋位索恩比伯爵阁下的朝服与冠冕如何处置了。他处置它们的法子，恰是同他聚过餐的那些先生们所能预期的；而且干得如此本色，必定把他们心里「他与那人本是同一个」的最后一点余念，也驱散得干干净净。找卡特·帕特森（Carter Paterson）搬运公司是万万不能了，出于显而易见的理由，任何标签或寄件地址都须回避。于是拉夫尔斯把那对白象安顿进了查令十字（Charing Cross）车站的衣帽寄存处——又把寄存牌寄给了索恩比勋爵。

## The Field of Phillipi / 菲利比战场

===Original===
Nipper Nasmyth had been head of our school when Raffles was captain of cricket. I believe he owed his nickname entirely to the popular prejudice against a day-boy; and in view of the special reproach which the term carried in my time, as also of the fact that his father was one of the school trustees, partner in a banking firm of four resounding surnames, and manager of the local branch, there can be little doubt that the stigma was undeserved. But we did not think so then, for Nasmyth was unpopular with high and low, and appeared to glory in the fact. A swollen conscience caused him to see and hear even more than was warranted by his position, and his uncompromising nature compelled him to act on whatsoever he heard or saw: a savage custodian of public morals, he had in addition a perverse enthusiasm for lost causes, loved a minority for its own sake, and untenable tenets for theirs. Such, at all events, was my impression of Nipper Nasmyth, after my first term, which was also his last. I had never spoken to him, but I had heard him speak with extraordinary force and fervor in the school debates. I carried a clear picture of his unkempt hair, his unbrushed coat, his dominant spectacles, his dogmatic jaw. And it was I who knew the combination at a glance, after years and years, when the fateful whim seized Raffles to play once more in the Old Boys’ Match, and his will took me down with him to participate in the milder festivities of Founder’s Day.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯（Raffles）任板球队长的那阵子，尼珀·纳斯密斯（Nipper Nasmyth）正是我们学校的学生之首。我相信他这个绰号，全然得自众人对走读生的普遍偏见；念及这称呼在我那个年代所携带的特别羞辱，再念及他父亲身为本校校董之一、是那家挂着四个响亮姓氏的银行的合伙人、还兼着本地分行的经理，这污名多半是冤枉的，几乎没有疑问。可我们当年不这么想，因为纳斯密斯上上下下都不讨人喜欢，而他还仿佛引以为荣。一颗膨胀的良心，使他的所见所闻超出了他那个位置应有之限；而他那寸步不让的天性，又逼得他对所闻所见事事出手：身为公共道德的一名野蛮看守，他另有一股专爱败局的逆反热情——单为少数派而爱少数派，单为站不住脚而爱那些信条。不管怎么说，这就是我对尼珀·纳斯密斯的印象，得自我入学后的第一个学期，那也是他的最后一个学期。我从未同他说过话，却听过他在学校辩论会上出言惊人、慷慨激昂。他那一头乱发、那件从不刷的外套、那副咄咄逼人的眼镜、那副武断的下巴，在我脑中留下一幅清晰的画像。而多年多年之后，当那个命中注定的怪念头攫住拉夫尔斯，要他再打一场老校友赛（Old Boys’ Match），又是他的意志捎我同去凑创校人纪念日（Founder’s Day）上较为温和的热闹时，一眼认出这整套组合的，正是我。

===Original===
It was, however, no ordinary occasion. The bicentenary loomed but a year ahead, and a movement was on foot to mark the epoch with an adequate statue of our pious founder. A special meeting was to be held at the schoolhouse, and Raffles had been specially invited by the new head master, a man of his own standing, who had been in the eleven with him up at Cambridge. Raffles had not been near the old place for years; but I had never gone down since the day I left; and I will not dwell on the emotions which the once familiar journey awakened in my unworthy bosom. Paddington was alive with Old Boys of all ages﻿—but very few of ours﻿—if not as lively as we used to make it when we all landed back for the holidays. More of us had moustaches and cigarettes and “loud” ties. That was all. Yet of the throng, though two or three looked twice and thrice at Raffles, neither he nor I knew a soul until we had to change at the junction near our journey’s end, when, as I say, it was I who recognized Nipper Nasmyth at sight.
===Chinese===
然而，这一回却不是寻常日子。两百周年校庆在望，只隔一年，一场运动已在酝酿，要为我们那位虔诚的创校人立一座相称的雕像。校舍里将召开一次特别会议，拉夫尔斯受了新校长的专门邀请——新校长与他身份相当，当年在剑桥（Cambridge）还同他一个校队里打过球。拉夫尔斯多年不曾踏近这老地方；而我，自离校那天起就从没回去过；这一趟旧日熟悉的旅程，在我这不争气的胸中唤醒了怎样的情绪，我就不多流连了。帕丁顿（Paddington）活络着各年龄段的老校友——只是我们那拨的寥寥无几——虽不及当年大家放假一同杀回时闹得那样欢，也尽够热闹的了。我们这些人里，更多的是蓄了小胡子、抽上了香烟、系起了『招摇』领带的。如此而已。然而那一大群人里，虽说有两三位对拉夫尔斯多看了一两眼，他和我却一个熟人也认不出，直到在临近旅程终点的联轨站换车时——正如我所说，一眼认出尼珀·纳斯密斯的，又是我。

===Original===
The man was own son of the boy we both remembered. He had grown a ragged beard and a moustache that hung about his face like a neglected creeper. He was stout and bent and older than his years. But he spurned the platform with a stamping stride which even I remembered in an instant, and which was enough for Raffles before he saw the man’s face.
===Chinese===
这人活脱是我们俩都记得的那个男孩的嫡亲儿子。他蓄了一部乱蓬蓬的胡须，胡髭垂在脸畔，像一株无人打理的藤蔓。他发福了，佝背了，比他的年纪更显老。可他顿着双脚、大步踏过站台的架势，连我也顷刻认了出来；拉夫尔斯还没看清那人的脸，这架势就已经够了。

===Original===
“The Nipper it is!” he cried. “I could swear to that walk in a pantomime procession! See the independence in every step: that’s his heel on the neck of the oppressor: it’s the nonconformist conscience in baggy breeches. I must speak to him, Bunny. There was a lot of good in the old Nipper, though he and I did bar each other.”
===Chinese===
「正是尼珀这家伙！」他喊道。「这副走相，就是混进哑剧的游行队伍，我也能一口咬定是他！瞧那每一步里的桀骜：那是他的脚跟踩在暴君的脖子上：那是套在灯笼裤里的非国教徒良心。我一定得同他搭句话，邦尼（Bunny）。老尼珀身上好处多着呢，虽说当年他跟我互相看不顺眼。」

===Original===
And in a moment he had accosted the man by the boy’s nickname, obviously without thinking of an affront which few would have read in that hearty open face and hand.

“My name’s Nasmyth,” snapped the other, standing upright to glare.
===Chinese===
转眼间，他已凑上前去，用那男孩时代的绰号同那人招呼起来，显然没想过这有什么冒犯——在那张热诚开朗的脸和那只坦荡伸出的手里，也少有人能读出冒犯来。

「我叫纳斯密斯。」对方厉声说道，直挺挺地站定，怒目而视。

===Original===
“Forgive me,” said Raffles undeterred. “One remembers a nickname and forgets all it never used to mean. Shake hands, my dear fellow! I’m Raffles. It must be fifteen years since we met.”
===Chinese===
「恕我失礼。」拉夫尔斯毫不气馁地说。「人记得住一个绰号，却忘了它当年从来不曾含过的意思。握个手吧，亲爱的伙计！我是拉夫尔斯。咱们怕是有十五年没见了。」

===Original===
“At least,” replied Nasmyth coldly; but he could no longer refuse Raffles his hand. “So you are going down,” he sneered, “to this great gathering?” And I stood listening at my distance, as though still in the middle fourth.
===Chinese===
「至少。」纳斯密斯冷冷地答道；可他再也不能拒绝把手伸给拉夫尔斯了。「这么说，你们也要下去，」他冷笑道，「去赶那场盛大的聚会？」我站在自己那点距离外听着，仿佛还在中四班里当学生。

===Original===
“Rather!” cried Raffles. “I’m afraid I have let myself lose touch, but I mean to turn over a new leaf. I suppose that isn’t necessary in your case, Nasmyth?”

He spoke with an enthusiasm rare indeed in him: it had grown upon Raffles in the train; the spirit of his boyhood had come rushing back at fifty miles an hour. He might have been following some honorable calling in town; he might have snatched this brief respite from a distinguished but exacting career. I am convinced that it was I alone who remembered at that moment the life we were really leading at that time. With me there walked this skeleton through every waking hour that was to follow. I shall endeavor not to refer to it again. Yet it should not be forgotten that my skeleton was always there.
===Chinese===
「当然去！」拉夫尔斯喊道。「只怕我任由自己同母校断了音讯，不过我打算从此改过自新。我想这层在你就不必了，纳斯密斯？」

他说这话时带着一种他身上罕见的热情：这热情是拉夫尔斯在火车上一路滋长起来的；他少年时代的心气，以每小时五十英里的速度奔回来了。他满可以在城里操着某个体面的营生；他满可以从一项显赫却劳人的事业里，偷得这几日的喘息。我敢断定，那一刻唯有我一人还记得，我们当时真正过着的是怎样的日子。在我这里，这具骷髅陪我走过了此后每一个醒着的时辰。我将尽量不再提它。但不应忘记的是，我的这具骷髅始终都在。

===Original===
“It certainly is not necessary in my case,” replied Nasmyth, still as stiff as any poker. “I happen to be a trustee.”

“Of the school?”

“Like my father before me.”
===Chinese===
「在我这方面自然不必。」纳斯密斯答道，依旧僵直得赛过拨火棍。「我凑巧是位校董。」

「学校的？」

「子承父业，跟我父亲当年一样。」

===Original===
“I congratulate you, my dear fellow!” cried the hearty Raffles﻿—a younger Raffles than I had ever known in town.

“I don’t know that you need,” said Nasmyth sourly.
===Chinese===
「恭喜你呀，亲爱的伙计！」热诚的拉夫尔斯扬声喊道——一个比我在城里见识过的任何一个都年轻的拉夫尔斯。

「我看你用不着恭喜。」纳斯密斯酸溜溜地说。

===Original===
“But it must be a tremendous interest. And the proof is that you’re going down to this show, like all the rest of us.”

“No, I’m not. I live there, you see.”
===Chinese===
「可这必定是一桩莫大的兴味。证据便是：你这不是也要赶下去赴这场热闹，跟我们其余人一样。」

「不，我不是赶下去。你瞧，我就住在那儿。」

===Original===
And I think the Nipper recalled that name as he ground his heel upon an unresponsive flagstone.
===Chinese===
而我想，尼珀一边把脚跟碾在一块毫无反应的石板上，一边想起来了这个绰号的分量。

===Original===
“But you’re going to this meeting at the schoolhouse, surely?”

“I don’t know. If I do there may be squalls. I don’t know what you think about this precious scheme Raffles, but *I*﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「可校舍的会上，你总归是要去的吧？」

「难说。要是去，兴许要起风波。这个劳什子计划你怎么看，我不知道，拉夫尔斯，可*我*……」

===Original===
The ragged beard stuck out, set teeth showed through the wild moustache, and in a sudden outpouring we had his views. They were narrow and intemperate and perverse as any I had heard him advocate as the firebrand of the Debating Society in my first term. But they were stated with all the old vim and venom. The mind of Nasmyth had not broadened with the years, but neither had its natural force abated, nor that of his character either. He spoke with great vigor at the top of his voice; soon we had a little crowd about us; but the tall collars and the broad smiles of the younger Old Boys did not deter our dowdy demagogue. Why spend money on a man who had been dead two hundred years? What good could it do him or the school? Besides, he was only technically our founder. He had not founded a great public school. He had founded a little country grammar school which had pottered along for a century and a half. The great public school was the growth of the last fifty years, and no credit to the pillar of piety. Besides, he was only nominally pious. Nasmyth had made researches, and he knew. And why throw good money after a bad man?
===Chinese===
乱蓬蓬的胡须戟张起来，咬紧的牙关从野草般的胡髭后头露出；一阵突发的倾泻里，我们听全了他的主张。那些主张狭隘、激烈、偏执，不亚于我第一学期里听他以辩论社煽动家的身份鼓吹过的任何一套。可陈述起来，依旧是当年的那股锐气和毒劲。纳斯密斯的头脑不曾随岁月开阔，但其天生的膂力也未衰减，连他性格里的那股劲也分毫未减。他扯开嗓门、气势汹汹地大发宏论；不多时我们身边便围起一小圈人；可年轻老校友们的高领子和满脸的笑，也吓不住我们这位寒酸的煽动家。何苦在一个死了两百年的人身上花钱？这笔钱对他、对学校又有什么好处？再说，他也不过是名义上的创校人。他创立的并非一所了不起的公学；他创立的是一所乡下小文法学校，磨磨蹭蹭地混过一个半世纪。这所了不起的公学，是近五十年才长起来的，而那位虔敬的柱石半点功劳也摊不上。再说，他也不过是名义上虔敬。纳斯密斯做过考据，他心里有数。再说，何苦把好钱往一个坏人身后抛？

===Original===
“Are there many of your opinion?” inquired Raffles, when the agitator paused for breath. And Nasmyth beamed on us with flashing eyes.
===Chinese===
「和你持同一看法的多吗？」这位鼓动家停下来换气的当口，拉夫尔斯问道。纳斯密斯双眼放光，朝我们眉飞色舞。

===Original===
“Not one to my knowledge as yet,” said he. “But we shall see after tomorrow night. I hear it’s to be quite an exceptional gathering this year; let us hope it may contain a few sane men. There are none on the present staff, and I only know of one among the trustees!”
===Chinese===
「据我所知，眼下还一个没有。」他说。「不过明晚过后且看分晓。我听说今年的集会相当特别；但愿里头能有几个明白人。现任的教职员里一个没有，校董当中，我也就识得一位！」

===Original===
Raffles refrained from smiling as his dancing eye met mine.

“I can understand your view,” he said. “I am not sure that I don’t share it to some extent. But it seems to me a duty to support a general movement like this even if it doesn’t take the direction or the shape of our own dreams. I suppose you yourself will give something, Nasmyth?”
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯含笑闪动的眼睛同我的目光相遇，他把笑意忍住了。

「你的观点我能理解。」他说。「不敢说我在某种程度上不怀有同感。可依我看，支持这样一场众人齐动的运动是一桩义务，哪怕它走的方向、长成的形状，都不同于我们各自的梦想。我想你自己总也会捐一点吧，纳斯密斯？」

===Original===
“Give something? I? Not a brass farthing!” cried the implacable banker. “To do so would be to stultify my whole position. I cordially and conscientiously disapprove of the whole thing, and shall use all my influence against it. No, my good sir, I not only don’t subscribe myself, but I hope to be the means of nipping a good many subscriptions in the bud.”
===Chinese===
「捐一点？我？一个铜子儿也不捐！」这位毫不通融的银行家喊道。「捐了，就是亲手把我的整个立场变成笑柄。我发自肺腑、凭良心反对这整个玩意儿，还要动用我全部的影响力跟它作对。不，我的好先生，我不但自己一个子儿不认捐，还指望由我把许多笔认捐掐死在萌芽里。」

===Original===
I was probably the only one who saw the sudden and yet subtle change in Raffles﻿—the hard mouth, the harder eye. I, at least, might have foreseen the sequel then and there. But his quiet voice betrayed nothing, as he inquired whether Nasmyth was going to speak at next night’s meeting. Nasmyth said he might, and certainly warned us what to expect. He was still fulminating when our train came in.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯身上那突然而又微妙的转变，看见的恐怕只有我一个——那张严厉的嘴，那双更严厉的眼睛。至少，我本可以当场预见后文。可他平静的嗓音不曾泄露分毫，只问纳斯密斯明晚的会上是否打算发言。纳斯密斯说兴许会，还特意预先关照了我们，叫我们知道等着的是什么。我们的火车进站时，他还在雷鸣电闪。

===Original===
“Then we meet again at Philippi,” cried Raffles in gay adieu. “For you have been very frank with us all, Nasmyth, and I’ll be frank enough in my turn to tell you that I’ve every intention of speaking on the other side!”
===Chinese===
「那我们就在菲利比（Philippi）再见。」拉夫尔斯喜气洋洋地道别。「你既对我们大家这般坦率，纳斯密斯，我也坦率到底，告诉你：我可是打定了主意，要在会上替对立的一方发言！」

===Original===
It happened that Raffles had been asked to speak by his old college friend, the new head master. Yet it was not at the schoolhouse that he and I were to stay, but at the house that we had both been in as boys. It also had changed hands: a wing had been added, and the double tier of tiny studies made brilliant with electric light. But the quad and the fives-courts did not look a day older; the ivy was no thicker round the study windows; and in one boy’s castle we found the traditional print of Charing Cross Bridge which had knocked about our studies ever since a son of the contractor first sold it when he left. Nay, more, there was the bald remnant of a stuffed bird which had been my own daily care when it and I belonged to Raffles. And when we all filed in to prayers, through the green baize door which still separated the master’s part of the house from that of the boys, there was a small boy posted in the passage to give the sign of silence to the rest assembled in the hall, quite identically as in the dim old days; the picture was absolutely unchanged; it was only we who were out of it in body and soul.
===Chinese===
碰巧，拉夫尔斯的老大学同学——那位新校长——早就请他发言。不过他和我落脚的地方不是校舍，而是我们俩做男孩时都住过的那座宿舍。它也换了人家：添建了一翼厢房，那上下两层一格一格的小书房，装上了明晃晃的电灯。可四方院子和墙手球场（fives-courts）瞧着一天也不见老；书房窗畔的常春藤，也不见浓密一分；在一间男孩的城堡里，我们见到了那幅查令十字桥（Charing Cross Bridge）的传统版画——打从某位承包商的公子离校时头一回把它卖掉起，它便一直在我们的书房里辗转流传。不止如此，那里还剩着一只鸟标本的秃骸——当年它和我都归拉夫尔斯使唤时，天天照料它是我一人的差事。待到众人鱼贯入场去做祷告，穿过那道仍旧把宅子的教师一半与男孩一半隔开的绿呢门时，过道里照旧驻着一个小男孩，向聚在大厅里的其余人等打噤声的暗号——同昏暗的旧年月分毫不差；这幅画面全然未改；变的只是我们，无论身与心，都退出画外了。

===Original===
On our side of the baize door a fine hospitality and a finer flow of spirits were the order of the night. There was a sound representative assortment of quite young Old Boys, to whom ours was a prehistoric time, and in the trough of their modern chaff and chat we old stagers might well have been left far astern of the fun. Yet it was Raffles who was the life and soul of the party, and that not by meretricious virtue of his cricket. There happened not to be another cricketer among us, and it was on their own subjects that Raffles laughed with the lot in turn and in the lump. I never knew him in quite such form. I will not say he was a boy among them, but he was that rarer being, the man of the world who can enter absolutely into the fun and fervor of the salad age. My cares and my regrets had never been more acute, but Raffles seemed a man without either in his life.
===Chinese===
绿呢门我们这一侧，这一晚的主旋律，是丰盛的款待和更盛的兴致。到场的有一班有模有样、颇具代表性的十分年轻的老校友，对他们来说，我们那段岁月无异于史前；在他们时新打趣与闲谈的波谷里，我们这些老骨头本该被远远抛在乐子的后头。然而做这一夜的灵魂与中心的，偏偏是拉夫尔斯，而这并非沾了他板球身手的那层虚浮光彩。我们中间碰巧再没有第二个打板球的；拉夫尔斯是拿他们各自爱谈的话题，同他们逐个寒暄，也同他们合群哄笑。我从没见他这般状态过。我不愿说他混在他们中间像个男孩；可他是更罕见的一类人——一个能全然融入青涩年华的欢笑与热忱的入世老手。我的忧虑和悔恨从未这般尖锐，拉夫尔斯却像个生命里两样都无的人。

===Original===
He was not, however, the hero of the Old Boys’ Match, and that was expected of him by all the school. There was a hush when he went in, a groan when he came out. I had no reason to suppose he was not trying; these things happen to the cricketer who plays out of his class; but when the great Raffles went on to bowl, and was hit all over the field, I was not so sure. It certainly failed to affect his spirits; he was more brilliant than ever at our hospitable board; and after dinner came the meeting at which he and Nasmyth were to speak.
===Chinese===
然而他并没能当上老校友赛的英雄，而全校上下都眼巴巴指望着他。他进场时一片肃静，出局时一片哀叹。我没有理由断定他没有尽力；越了辈分出场的球员，这种事在所难免；可等到伟大的拉夫尔斯接着上场投球、被打得满场开花时，我就不敢肯定了。这确实未曾败坏他的兴致；在我们那张好客的餐桌上，他比先前更加神采焕发；晚饭过后，便是他和纳斯密斯都要发言的那场会议。

===Original===
It was a somewhat frigid gathering until Nasmyth rose. We had all dined with our respective hosts, and then repaired to this business in cold blood. Many were lukewarm about it in their hearts; there was a certain amount of mild prejudice, and a greater amount of animal indifference, to be overcome in the opening speech. It is not for me to say whether this was successfully accomplished. I only know how the temperature of that meeting rose with Nipper Nasmyth.
===Chinese===
在纳斯密斯起立之前，这场集会颇有些冷冰冰。我们都各自在主人家用过了晚饭，然后平心静气地转来办这桩正事。不少人心里对它不冷不热；开场演说要克服的，是些许温和的成见，和更大一股兽性的漠然。这任务完成得成与不成，轮不到我来说。我只知道，那场会议的温度，是随着尼珀·纳斯密斯升上来的。

===Original===
And I dare say, in all the circumstances of the case, his really was a rather vulgar speech. But it was certainly impassioned, and probably as purely instinctive as his denunciation of all the causes which appeal to the gullible many without imposing upon the cantankerous few. His arguments, it is true, were merely an elaboration of those with which he had favored some of us already; but they were pointed by a concise exposition of the several definite principles they represented, and barbed with a caustic rhetoric quite admirable in itself. In a word, the manner was worthy of the very foundation it sought to shake, or we had never swallowed such matter without a murmur. As it was, there was a demonstration in the wilderness when the voice ceased crying. But we sat in the deeper silence when Raffles rose to reply.
===Chinese===
我敢说，就事情的全般情形而论，他那确实是一场相当粗鄙的演说。可它无疑慷慨激昂，而且大概同他抨击一切事业时的做派一样，纯出天性——那一类事业，骗得了轻信的大多数，却压不住好斗的极少数。诚然，他的论证不过是先前已赏过我们中几位的那套话的加细铺陈；可经他对所代表的那几条明确原则一番简练的阐发，论证立了锋尖，又淬上一层本身着实可观的刻薄修辞。一言以蔽之：那做派配得上它企图撼动的那座根基本身，否则我们绝不会毫无怨言地咽下这等内容。即便如此，那旷野里的呼喊声一停，还是响起了一阵喧腾。可当拉夫尔斯起身作答时，我们坐进了更深的寂静。

===Original===
I leaned forward not to lose a word. I knew my Raffles so well that I felt almost capable of reporting his speech before I heard it. Never was I more mistaken, even in him! So far from a gibe for a gibe and a taunt for a taunt, there never was softer answer than that which A. J. Raffles returned to Nipper Nasmyth before the staring eyes and startled ears of all assembled. He courteously but firmly refused to believe a word his old friend Nasmyth had said﻿—about himself. He had known Nasmyth for twenty years, and never had he met a dog who barked so loud and bit so little. The fact was that he had far too kind a heart to bite at all. Nasmyth might get up and protest as loud as he liked: the speaker declared he knew him better than Nasmyth knew himself. He had the necessary defects of his great qualities. He was only too good a sportsman. He had a perfect passion for the weaker side. That alone led Nasmyth into such excesses of language as we had all heard from his lips that night. As for Raffles, he concluded his far too genial remarks by predicting that, whatever Nasmyth might say or think of the new fund, he would subscribe to it as handsomely as any of us, like “the generous good chap” that we all knew him to be.
===Chinese===
我前倾着身子，一个字也不肯漏。我太了解我的拉夫尔斯，几乎觉得不等开讲，就能替他拟出讲词。哪知连在他身上，我也从没错得这样厉害！哪里谈得上以讥还讥、以刺还刺，A. J. 拉夫尔斯当着全场圆睁的眼睛和竖起的耳朵，答复尼珀·纳斯密斯的，是再软和不过的答话。他客气而坚定地拒不相信他的老朋友纳斯密斯说过的一个字——关于他自己的那部分。他认识纳斯密斯二十年，从没见过一条狗叫得这般响、咬得这般少。实情是，他那副心肠太好，压根儿咬不下去口。纳斯密斯尽可以站起来，爱抗议多响抗议多响：演讲人宣称，他比纳斯密斯本人还了解纳斯密斯。他的伟大品质，带着必不可少的缺陷。他只是运动家风太足了。他对弱势的一方怀有一种十足的热忱。单凭这一条，纳斯密斯才说出了我们当晚人人听见的那些过头话。至于拉夫尔斯，他末了用好得过分的口吻预言：无论纳斯密斯对新基金说什么、想什么，他都会像我们中间随便哪一位一样大手笔认捐——因为他原就是我们都知道的那位『慷慨的好人』。

===Original===
Even so did Raffles disappoint the Old Boys in the evening as he had disappointed the school by day. We had looked to him for a noble raillery, a lofty and loyal disdain, and he had fobbed us off with friendly personalities not even in impeccable taste. Nevertheless, this light treatment of a grave offence went far to restore the natural amenities of the occasion. It was impossible even for Nasmyth to reply to it as he might to a more earnest onslaught. He could but smile sardonically, and audibly undertake to prove Raffles a false prophet; and though subsequent speakers were less merciful the note was struck, and there was no more bad blood in the debate. There was plenty, however, in the veins of Nasmyth, as I was to discover for myself before the night was out.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯晚间这样辜负了老校友们，正如他白天辜负了全校。我们本指望他来一场高贵的妙讽、一种高傲而忠诚的鄙夷，他却拿几句连品味都未必无懈可击的友好人身玩笑，把我们打发了。话虽如此，对一桩严重的冒犯这般轻描淡写，倒大大挽回了这场合本应有的温良。哪怕是纳斯密斯，也无法像回敬一场更认真的攻势那样回敬它。他只能讥诮地一笑，扬声许诺要证明拉夫尔斯是个假先知；虽说后来的发言人不这般留情，调子既已定下，辩论里便再没了火气。可纳斯密斯的血管里火气有的是——这一点，不出当夜我就要亲身领教。

===Original===
You might think that in the circumstances he would not have attended the head master’s ball with which the evening ended; but that would be sadly to misjudge so perverse a creature as the notorious Nipper. He was probably one of those who protest that there is “nothing personal” in their most personal attacks. Not that Nasmyth took this tone about Raffles when he and I found ourselves cheek by jowl against the ballroom wall; he could forgive his franker critics, but not the friendly enemy who had treated him so much more gently than he deserved.
===Chinese===
处此情形，你或许以为他总不会出席校长为这一夜收尾的舞会了；可若这么想，就大大看错了『尼珀』这么个出了名的拧种。他多半是那种人：口口声声自己最人身的人身攻击里『绝无个人意气』。倒不是说他同我肩挨着肩靠在舞厅墙边时，对拉夫尔斯用的就是这副腔调；他能原谅批评得直白的论敌，却原谅不了这个把他款待得远比他应得的更温存的友好之敌。

===Original===
“I seem to have seen you with this great man Raffles,” began Nasmyth, as he overhauled me with his fighting eye. “Do you know him well?”

“Intimately.”

“I remember now. You were with him when he forced himself upon me on the way down yesterday. He had to tell me who he was. Yet he talks as though we were old friends.”
===Chinese===
「我好像见过你同这位大人物拉夫尔斯在一起。」纳斯密斯开口了，一面用那只好斗的眼睛把我上下打量。「你同他很熟吗？」

「知根知底。」

「我想起来了。昨天下来的路上他硬凑上来同我攀谈时，你就在他身边。他还得自报家门，我才知道他是谁。可他说话的口气，倒好像我们是多年的老友。」

===Original===
“You were in the upper sixth together,” I rejoined, nettled by his tone.

“What does that matter? I am glad to say I had too much self-respect, and too little respect for Raffles, ever to be a friend of his then. I knew too many of the things he did,” said Nipper Nasmyth.
===Chinese===
「你们当年在高六班可是同班。」我给他这口气激恼了，回敬道。

「那算得了什么？我庆幸自己当年自尊太重，又太不把拉夫尔斯放在眼里，到底没同他做过朋友。他干的那些事，我知道得太多了。」尼珀·纳斯密斯说。

===Original===
His fluent insults had taken my breath. But in a lucky flash I saw my retort.

“You must have had special opportunities of observation, living in the town,” said I; and drew first blood between the long hair and the ragged beard; but that was all.
===Chinese===
他这一串流利的辱骂，打得我一时背气。可一记走运的灵光里，我看见了回击的路数。

「住在城里，你观察的机会想必格外多。」我说；这一枪从长发与乱须之间见了头血；可也就到此为止。

===Original===
“So he really did get out at nights?” remarked my adversary. “You certainly give your friend away. What’s he doing now?”
===Chinese===
「这么说，他还真在夜里出去过？」我的对手评说道。「你可真把你的朋友卖得不轻。他如今靠什么营生？」

===Original===
I let my eyes follow Raffles round the room before replying. He was waltzing with a master’s wife﻿—waltzing as he did everything else. Other couples seemed to melt before them. And the woman on his arm looked a radiant girl.
===Chinese===
我先放任目光跟着拉夫尔斯绕场，然后才作答。他正搂着某位教员的太太跳华尔兹——跳华尔兹也同他做其他一切事一样。别的舞侣仿佛都在他们面前消融。他臂弯里的那位妇人，看着像个光彩照人的姑娘。

===Original===
“I meant in town, or wherever he lives his mysterious life,” explained Nasmyth, when I told him that he could see for himself. But his clever tone did not trouble me; it was his epithet that caused me to prick my ears. And I found some difficulty in following Raffles right round the room.
===Chinese===
「我是说在伦敦城里，或者随便他过那种神秘日子的什么地方。」我告诉他眼见为实之后，纳斯密斯如此解释。可他那聪明的腔调并不叫我心烦；叫我竖起耳朵的，是他那一个字眼。于是我发觉，要跟着拉夫尔斯把整场绕下来，颇有些吃力。

===Original===
“I thought everybody knew what he was doing; he’s playing cricket most of his time,” was my measured reply; and if it bore an extra touch of insolence, I can honestly ascribe that to my nerves.

“And is that all he does for a living?” pursued my inquisitor keenly.
===Chinese===
「我以为人人都知道他在做什么；他大部分时间都在打板球。」我不紧不慢地答道；这话里若多带了一分无礼，我可以凭良心把它归给我的神经。

「他谋生就只干这一样？」我的审问官紧追不放。

===Original===
“You had better ask Raffles himself,” said I to that. “It’s a pity you didn’t ask him in public, at the meeting!”

But I was beginning to show temper in my embarrassment, and of course that made Nasmyth the more imperturbable.
===Chinese===
「那你最好去问拉夫尔斯本人。」我答了这一句。「可惜你没当众问——在会上！」

可我在窘迫里渐渐挂了火气，这自然让纳斯密斯愈发不动如山。

===Original===
“Really, he might be following some disgraceful calling, by the mystery you make of it!” he exclaimed. “And for that matter I call first-class cricket a disgraceful calling, when it’s followed by men who ought to be gentlemen, but are really professionals in gentlemanly clothing. The present craze for gladiatorial athleticism I regard as one of the great evils of the age; but the thinly veiled professionalism of the so-called amateur is the greatest evil of that craze. Men play for the gentlemen and are paid more than the players who walk out of another gate. In my time there was none of that. Amateurs were amateurs and sport was sport; there were no Raffleses in first-class cricket then. I had forgotten Raffles was a modern first-class cricketer: that explains him. Rather than see my son such another, do you know what I’d prefer to see him?”
===Chinese===
「说真的，瞧你们搞得这般神秘，他没准儿正干着什么不体面的营生呢！」他叫起来。「说到这个，我本就把甲级板球（first-class cricket）唤作不体面的营生——叫本该是绅士的人去干，实际却是穿了绅士衣裳吃这碗职业饭。当今这股角斗士式体格的狂热，我看是时代的一大恶；而所谓业余选手身上那层薄薄遮着的职业气，更是这狂热里最大的恶。男人们替绅士那一边出赛，拿的却比从另一道门进场的职业球员还多。我那时候没有这些名堂。业余的就是业余的，运动就是运动；那时候的甲级板球场上，可没有拉夫尔斯们。我倒忘了拉夫尔斯如今是当代的甲级板球球员了：这就把他说明白了。与其看我儿子变成那样一个人，你知道我情愿看他变成什么？」

===Original===
I neither knew nor cared: yet a wretched premonitory fascination held me breathless till I was told.
===Chinese===
我既不知道，也不关心：可一种不祥的、要命的魔力攥得我大气不出，直到他说出口。

===Original===
“I’d prefer to see him a thief!” said Nasmyth savagely; and when his eyes were done with me, he turned upon his heel. So that ended that stage of my discomfiture.
===Chinese===
「我情愿看他做个贼！」纳斯密斯恶狠狠地说；等他那双眼睛把我钉够了，他一旋身走了。我这场狼狈的第一幕，就此收场。

===Original===
It was only to give place to a worse. Was all this accident or fell design? Conscience had made a coward of me, and yet what reason had I to disbelieve the worst? We were pirouetting on the edge of an abyss; sooner or later the false step must come and the pit swallow us. I began to wish myself back in London, and I did get back to my room in our old house. My dancing days were already over; there I had taken the one resolution to which I remained as true as better men to better vows; there the painful association was no mere sense of personal unworthiness. I fell to thinking in my room of other dances﻿ ﻿… and was still smoking the cigarette which Raffles had taught me to appreciate when I looked up to find him regarding me from the door. He had opened it as noiselessly as only Raffles could open doors, and now he closed it in the same professional fashion.
===Chinese===
接替它的，只是更糟的一幕。这一切究竟是碰巧，还是狠毒的设计？良心早把我变成了懦夫，可我又有什么理由不信最坏的那种？我们正踮着脚尖，回旋在深渊的边上；错的那一步迟早要来，深坑迟早要把我们吞了。我开始盼着自己回到伦敦去，而我也的确回到了我们那座老宅里我自己的房间。我的跳舞岁月早已完结；在那里，我曾立下此生唯一一桩我信守到底的决心，其坚贞不下于更好的人信守更好的誓言；在那里，这番痛苦的联想，已不止是一己的不配之感。我在房里渐渐想起别的舞会……手里那支烟还没抽完——那烟还是拉夫尔斯教会我品味的——我一抬头，发现他正倚在门口望着我。他开门开得悄没声儿，天底下只有拉夫尔斯开得出这样的门；此刻他又用同一副行家的手法，把它掩上了。

===Original===
“I missed Achilles hours ago,” said he. “And still he’s sulking in his tent!”
===Chinese===
「几个钟头之前，我就没瞧见阿喀琉斯（Achilles）了。」他说。「而他至今还在帐篷里怄气呢！」

===Original===
“I have been,” I answered, laughing as he could always make me, “but I’ll chuck it if you’ll stop and smoke. Our host doesn’t mind; there’s an ashtray provided for the purpose. I ought to be sulking between the sheets, but I’m ready to sit up with you till morning.”
===Chinese===
「怄过的。」我答道，像他向来那样被他逗笑了，「不过你要是肯留下抽支烟，我就不怄了。咱们的主人不在乎；这屋里还专门备了烟灰缸。本该怄气的，是躺在被窝里的我，可我乐意陪你坐到天亮。」

===Original===
“We might do worse; but, on the other hand, we might do still better,” rejoined Raffles, and for once he resisted the seductive Sullivan. “As a matter of fact, it’s morning now; in another hour it will be dawn; and where could day dawn better than in Warfield Woods, or along the Stockley road, or even on the Upper or the Middle? I don’t want to turn in, any more than you do. I may as well confess that the whole show down here has exalted me more than anything for years. But if we can’t sleep, Bunny, let’s have some fresh air instead.”
===Chinese===
「比这更糟的咱们又不是没干过；不过话说回来，更好的咱们也干得成。」拉夫尔斯应道，而且破天荒头一遭，抵挡住了沙利文（Sullivan）的诱惑。「说实话，此刻已是清晨；再过一个钟头，天就要亮了；论看天亮，还有什么地方比得上沃菲尔德树林（Warfield Woods），比得上斯托克利路（Stockley road）沿线，甚至比得上上游或中游的河边呢？我不想睡，同你一样。我不妨招认：下乡这一整套热闹，比多少年来的任何事都更叫我心气高涨。可既然睡不着，邦尼，那就换点新鲜空气吧。」

===Original===
“Has everybody gone to bed?” I asked.

“Long ago. I was the last in. Why?”
===Chinese===
「都上床睡了？」我问。

「早就睡了。我是最后进屋的。怎么啦？」

===Original===
“Only it might sound a little odd, our turning out again, if they were to hear us.”
===Chinese===
「没什么，只是咱们要是再出去，万一叫他们听见，未免有点古怪。」

===Original===
Raffles stood over me with a smile made of mischief and cunning; but it was the purest mischief imaginable, the most innocent and comic cunning.

“They shan’t hear us at all, Bunny,” said he. “I mean to get out as I did in the good old nights. I’ve been spoiling for the chance ever since I came down. There’s not the smallest harm in it now; and if you’ll come with me I’ll show you how it used to be done.”
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯俯身立在我跟前，脸上挂着一个由顽皮与狡黠配成的笑；可那是想得到的顶纯粹的顽皮、顶无辜顶逗乐的狡黠。

「他们根本听不见咱们，邦尼。」他说。「我打算像从前那些黄金夜晚那样出去。打从下乡那天起，我就手痒着等这个机会。如今这么干没有半点害处；你肯同去，我就表演给你看，当年是怎么干的。」

===Original===
“But I know,” said I. “Who used to haul up the rope after you, and let it down again to the minute?”
===Chinese===
「这我还不清楚？」我说。「从前是谁替你把绳子收上去、又掐着分秒再放下来的？」

===Original===
Raffles looked down on me from lowered lids, over a smile too humorous to offend.

“My dear good Bunny! And do you suppose that even then I had only one way of doing a thing? I’ve had a spare loophole all my life, and when you’re ready I’ll show you what it was when I was here. Take off those boots, and carry your tennis-shoes; slip on another coat; put out your light; and I’ll meet you on the landing in two minutes.”
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯垂着眼帘俯看我，唇边挂一个笑——那笑里有太多谐趣，冒犯不到人。

「我亲爱的好邦尼！难道你以为，即便在当年，我做一件事也只有一条路子？我这辈子，备用的暗门从来都留着一扇；你一收拾停当，我就领你看我在这儿那会儿走的是哪一扇。脱下那双靴子，把网球鞋拎上；再换一件外衣；把灯灭了；两分钟后楼梯平台上见。」

===Original===
He met me with uplifted finger, and not a syllable; and downstairs he led me, stocking soles close against the skirting, two feet to each particular step. It must have seemed child’s play to Raffles; the old precautions were obviously assumed for my entertainment; but I confess that to me it was all refreshingly exciting﻿—for once without a risk of durance if we came to grief! With scarcely a creak we reached the hall, and could have walked out of the street door without danger or difficulty. But that would not do for Raffles. He must needs lead me into the boys’ part, through the green baize door. It took a deal of opening and shutting, but Raffles seemed to enjoy nothing better than these mock obstacles, and in a few minutes we were resting with sharp ears in the boys’ hall.
===Chinese===
他竖着一根手指迎我，一个音节也没有；下楼时他领着我，穿袜的脚掌紧贴着踢脚板，每一级台阶都分作两脚踩。这在拉夫尔斯必是孩童游戏；那些旧日的防备，分明是特为给我逗乐才摆的；可我承认，这一切于我是何等提神的新鲜——这一回纵使砸了锅，也无吃官司蹲班房之险！我们几乎没弄出一声吱呀就到了大厅，本可以毫发无险、轻而易举地从临街的门走出去。可拉夫尔斯偏不肯。他非得领我进男孩们那半边，穿过那道绿呢门。那门开关起来颇费了一番手脚，可拉夫尔斯仿佛最享受这些假想的障碍；不出几分钟，我们已经支棱着耳朵，在男孩们的大厅里歇下了脚。

===Original===
“Through these windows?” I whispered, when the clock over the piano had had matters its own way long enough to make our minds quite easy.

“How else?” whispered Raffles, as he opened the one on whose ledge our letters used to await us of a morning.
===Chinese===
「从这些窗子出去？」我悄声问——钢琴上方那座钟独自主宰了好一阵，足叫我们安下心来。

「不然还怎么出去？」拉夫尔斯悄声说，一面推开那扇窗——从前每天早晨，我们的信就在那窗台上候着我们。

===Original===
“And then through the quad﻿—”

“And over the gates at the end. No talking, Bunny; there’s a dormitory just overhead; but ours was in front, you remember, and if they had ever seen me I should have nipped back this way while they were watching the other.”
===Chinese===
「出去再穿过方院——」

「再翻过尽头那几道门。别说话，邦尼；头顶正上方就是一间宿舍；不过咱们那间在前楼，你记得吧——他们要是当真瞧见了我，我就趁他们盯住那头的工夫，从这边一缩身退回来。」

===Original===
His finger was on his lips as we got out softly into the starlight. I remember how the gravel hurt as we left the smooth flagged margin of the house for the open quad; but the nearer of two long green seats (whereon you prepared your construe for the second-school in the summer term) was mercifully handy; and once in our rubber soles we had no difficulty in scaling the gates beyond the fives-courts. Moreover, we dropped into a very desert of a country road, nor saw a soul when we doubled back beneath the outer study windows, nor heard a footfall in the main street of the slumbering town. Our own fell like the night-dews and the petals of the poet; but Raffles ran his arm through mine, and would chatter in whispers as we went.
===Chinese===
我们轻轻跨出窗外、踏进星光里时，他的手指仍抵在唇上。我还记得，一离开房子平整的石板边沿、踏上开阔方院的碎石，脚底硌得有多疼；幸而两条长长的绿座椅中近处的那一条（夏日学期里，你就在那上头预备第二堂课的翻译功课）仁慈地近在手边；一换上胶底鞋，翻过墙手球场外那几道门便不费吹灰之力。再说，我们一落步便进了一条荒漠也似的乡路；折回来打外面那些书房窗下经过时，一个人影也没瞧见；沉睡小镇的大街上，也听不见半点脚步。我们自己的脚步，落得像夜露、像诗人笔下的花瓣；拉夫尔斯却把胳膊穿进我的臂弯，一路走，还要一路咬着耳朵闲话。

===Original===
“So you and Nipper had a word﻿—or was it words? I saw you out of the tail of my eye when I was dancing, and I heard you out of the tail of my ear. It sounded like words, Bunny, and I thought I caught my name. He’s the most consistent man I know, and the least altered from a boy. But he’ll subscribe all right, you’ll see, and be very glad I made him.”
===Chinese===
「这么说，你同尼珀交锋了一句——还是交了好几锋？我跳舞那会儿，拿眼角瞧见了你，又拿耳角听见了你。听着可不止一个字呢，邦尼，我还当自己逮住了我的名字。他是我认得的人里最始终如一的一个，也是打男孩时代起变得最少的一个。可他认捐是一定的，你等着瞧，到时候他还得谢我逼他这一把。」

===Original===
I whispered back that I did not believe it for a moment. Raffles had not heard all Nasmyth had said of him. And neither would he listen to the little I meant to repeat to him; he would but reiterate a conviction so chimerical to my mind that I interrupted in my turn to ask him what ground he had for it.
===Chinese===
我悄悄回话说，这我半点也不信。纳斯密斯背后说他些什么，拉夫尔斯并没听全。而我想转述给他听的那一星半点，他也一个字听不进；他只顾翻来复去重申一个在我看来纯属空中楼阁的信念，倒叫我反过来打断他，问他这信念有何根据。

===Original===
“I’ve told you already,” said Raffles. “I mean to make him.”

“But how?” I asked. “And when, and where?”
===Chinese===
「我早把缘故告诉过你了。」拉夫尔斯说。「我打算亲手促成他。」

「可怎么个促成法？」我问。「什么时候？在哪儿？」

===Original===
“At Philippi, Bunny, where I said I’d see him. What a rabbit you are at a quotation!
===Chinese===
「在菲利比呀，邦尼，我说过要在那儿见他的。一句引文就吓破了胆，你可真是只兔子！」

===Original===
“ ‘And I think that the field of Philippi
					

					Was where Caesar came to an end;
					

					But who gave old Brutus the tip, I
					

					Can’t comprehend!’
===Chinese===
「依我看菲利比那片战场，
正是凯撒断气之地；
可谁给老布鲁图斯报的信，我
实在猜不出底细！」

===Original===
“You may have forgotten your Shakespeare, Bunny, but you ought to remember that.”
===Chinese===
「你的莎士比亚兴许忘了，邦尼，可这一首你总该记得。」

===Original===
And I did, vaguely, but had no idea what it or Raffles meant, as I plainly told him.
===Chinese===
我确实记得，影影绰绰地记得；可它什么意思、拉夫尔斯又是什么意思，我一概不知——我便照实对他说了。

===Original===
“The theatre of war,” he answered﻿—“and here we are at the stage door!”

Raffles had stopped suddenly in his walk. It was the last dark hour of the summer night, but the light from a neighboring lamppost showed me the look on his face as he turned.
===Chinese===
「战争的舞台，」他答道——「这不，咱们已经到了舞台门口！」

拉夫尔斯走着走着，突然收住了脚。这是夏夜最后的一个黑暗时辰，可邻近一盏路灯的光，照见了他转过脸来时脸上的神情。

===Original===
“I think you also inquired when,” he continued. “Well, then, this minute﻿—if you will give me a leg up!”
===Chinese===
「我想你还问了『何时』。」他接着说。「喏，就是此刻——只要你肯托我一把！」

===Original===
And behind him, scarcely higher than his head, and not even barred, was a wide window with a wire blind, and the name of Nasmyth among others lettered in gold upon the wire.
===Chinese===
他身后，高不过他的头顶、连栅栏都不设的，是一扇宽阔的大窗，挂着一幅金属网帘；纳斯密斯的名字同另外几个名字一道，用金字写在网上。

===Original===
“You’re never going to break in?”

“This instant, if you’ll, help me; in five or ten minutes, if you won’t.”
===Chinese===
「你该不会是要撬进去吧？」

「你肯搭手，这就进去；你不肯，五分钟十分钟后也进去。」

===Original===
“Surely you didn’t bring the﻿—the tools?”

He jingled them gently in his pocket.
===Chinese===
「你总不至于把——把家伙什也带来了吧？」

他把它们在口袋里轻轻晃出一串叮当。

===Original===
“Not the whole outfit, Bunny. But you never know when you mayn’t want one or two. I’m only thankful I didn’t leave the lot behind this time. I very nearly did.”
===Chinese===
「没带全副行头，邦尼。可谁说得准哪天就用得上一两件。我只想谢天谢地，这一回没把它们全撂在家里。我差一点儿就全撂下了。」

===Original===
“I must say I thought you would, coming down here,” I said reproachfully.

“But you ought to be glad I didn’t,” he rejoined with a smile. “It’s going to mean old Nasmyth’s subscription to the Founder’s Fund, and that’s to be a big one, I promise you! The lucky thing is that I went so far as to bring my bunch of safe keys. Now, are you going to help me use them, or are you not? If so, now’s your minute; if not, clear out and be﻿—”
===Chinese===
「说句实话，我原想着你下乡这趟总该撂下的。」我带着责备说。

「可你该庆幸我没撂下才对。」他含笑应道。「这就意味着老纳斯密斯要给创校基金（Founder’s Fund）认捐了，而且我打包票，还是一大笔！万幸的是，我竟连那串保险柜钥匙也随身带来了。怎么样，你到底帮不帮我用它们？帮，就趁此刻；不帮，就滚开去——」

===Original===
“Not so fast, Raffles,” said I testily. “You must have planned this before you came down, or you would never have brought all those things with you.”
===Chinese===
「先别急着赶我走，拉夫尔斯。」我烦躁地说。「你下乡之前必定就盘算好了这一出，不然才不会把这些东西一股脑儿都带上。」

===Original===
“My dear Bunny, they’re a part of my kit! I take them wherever I take my evening-clothes. As to this potty bank, I never even thought of it, much less that it would become a public duty to draw a hundred or so without signing for it. That’s all I shall touch, Bunny﻿—I’m not on the make tonight. There’s no risk in it either. If I am caught I shall simply sham champagne and stand the racket; it would be an obvious frolic after what happened at that meeting. And they will catch me, if I stand talking here: you run away back to bed﻿—unless you’re quite determined to ‘give old Brutus the tip!’ ”
===Chinese===
「亲爱的邦尼，它们是我随身行头的一部分！晚礼服带到哪儿，它们就跟到哪儿。至于这家巴掌大的银行，我压根儿没想到过它，更没想到从里头不签字提个百把镑，竟会成一桩公众义务。我只动这么些，邦尼——今晚我不图财。这也毫无风险。真叫人逮住了，我就只装作香槟上了头，甘当处分；有了会上那一出，这明摆着是一场胡闹。倒是你让我在这儿站着说下去，他们才真要来逮我了：你跑回床上去吧——除非你铁了心，非要『给老布鲁图斯报信』不可！」

===Original===
Now we had barely been a minute whispering where we stood, and the whole street was still as silent as the tomb. To me there seemed least danger in discussing the matter quietly on the spot. But even as he gave me my dismissal Raffles turned and caught the sill above him, first with one hand and then with the other.
