His legs swung like a pendulum as he drew himself up with one arm, then shifted the position of the other hand, and very gradually worked himself waist-high with the sill. But the sill was too narrow for him; that was as far as he could get unaided; and it was as much as I could bear to see of a feat which in itself might have hardened my conscience and softened my heart. But I had identified his doggerel verse at last. I am ashamed to say that it was part of a set of my very own writing in the school magazine of my time. So Raffles knew the stuff better than I did myself, and yet scorned to press his flattery to win me over! He had won me: in a second my rounded shoulders were a pedestal for those dangling feet. And before many more I heard the old metallic snap, followed by the raising of a sash so slowly and gently as to be almost inaudible to me listening just below.
===Chinese===
其实我们站在原地咬耳朵，统共还不满一分钟，整条街静得像坟墓。依我看，最稳妥的莫过于就地从从容容把事情商量个明白。可拉夫尔斯打发我走的话音未落，人已转过身去，双手先后扣住了头顶的窗台。他单臂引体向上，双腿像钟摆般荡着；接着换过另一只手的位置，一点一点把自己蹭到齐窗台高。可窗台太窄，容不下他；无人搭手，他至多到这一步；而看这一幕，我已忍无可忍——这手绝活本身，原可以磨硬我的良心、又软了我的心肠。可那首打油诗，我总算对上了号。说来惭愧，那正是我当年在校刊上发表、亲手写下的一组诗里的一首。可见这玩意儿，拉夫尔斯比我本人还熟，却又不屑拿这记马屁来收买我！他早把我收买了：转瞬之间，我弓起的肩背，已成了那双悬空之脚的垫座。没等再数几下，我便听见那声熟悉的金属脆响，接着是一扇窗框被抬起——抬得那么慢、那么轻，连就在下方侧耳听着的我，都几乎听不见。

===Original===
Raffles went through hands first, disappeared for an instant, then leaned out, lowering his hands for me.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯双手先行钻了进去，身形一晃不见，随即又探出上半身，垂下双手来接我。

===Original===
“Come on, Bunny! You’re safer in than out. Hang on to the sill and let me get you under the arms. Now all together﻿—quietly does it﻿—and over you come!”
===Chinese===
「来，邦尼！进来比待在外头安全。扒住窗台，让我托你腋下。好，一齐使劲——慢着点儿——这就把你翻过来！」

===Original===
No need to dwell on our proceedings in the bank. I myself had small part in the scene, being posted rather in the wings, at the foot of the stairs leading to the private premises in which the manager had his domestic being. But I made my mind easy about him, for in the silence of my watch I soon detected a nasal note overhead, and it was resonant and aggressive as the man himself. Of Raffles, on the contrary, I heard nothing, for he had shut the door between us, and I was to warn him if a single sound came through. I need scarcely add that no warning was necessary during the twenty minutes we remained in the bank. Raffles afterward assured me that nineteen of them had been spent in filing one key; but one of his latest inventions was a little thick velvet bag in which he carried the keys; and this bag had two elastic mouths, which closed so tightly about either wrist that he could file away, inside, and scarcely hear it himself. As for these keys, they were clever counterfeits of typical patterns by two great safe-making firms. And Raffles had come by them in a manner all his own, which the criminal world may discover for itself.
===Chinese===
我们在银行里的那番作为，不必细表。这场戏里我的戏份寥寥，被派在边幕的位置，守在通往私宅的楼梯脚下——经理的居家日月，就在那上头度过。可我为他放了心：放哨的寂静里，我很快听出头顶上一副鼻息，洪亮而咄咄逼人，同他本人的做派如出一辙。拉夫尔斯那边，我反倒什么也听不见，因为他把我们之间的门关了，讲定但凡有一丝声响透过来，便由我示警。几乎不用补充：我们留在银行里的那二十分钟，一次警也没有示过。拉夫尔斯事后向我担保，那二十分钟里有十九分钟，都花在锉一把钥匙上；好在他新近的发明里，有一只厚丝绒小袋，专门装钥匙；袋口有两道松紧箍，套在两只手腕上箍得严严实实，人在袋里锉，连他自己也几乎听不见响。至于那些钥匙，它们仿着两家保险柜大行号的典型样式，做得以假乱真。而拉夫尔斯弄到它们的路子，是他独有的路子——黑道上的诸位，大可自己去参详。

===Original===
When he opened the door and beckoned to me, I knew by his face that he had succeeded to his satisfaction, and by experience better than to question him on the point. Indeed, the first thing was to get out of the bank; for the stars were drowning in a sky of ink and water, and it was a comfort to feel that we could fly straight to our beds. I said so in whispers as Raffles cautiously opened our window and peeped out. In an instant his head was in, and for another I feared the worst.
===Chinese===
等他开了门向我招手，我一看他的脸色，便知道他已尽如所愿地得手；而凭经验，我识趣，不问。的确，头一件急务是出银行；星星正在一幅水墨掺和的天幕里溺水，而一想到我们能径直飞回各自的床上，就是一桩安慰。拉夫尔斯小心推开我们的窗、探头外望时，我凑着耳朵这样说。刹那间他缩回了头，有那么一瞬，我以为最坏的事临头了。

===Original===
“What was that, Bunny? No, you don’t, my son! There’s not a soul in sight that I can see, but you never know, and we may as well lay a scent while we’re about it. Ready? Then follow me, and never mind the window.”
===Chinese===
「那是什么，邦尼？不，你甭想，小子！我眼里倒是一个人影也看不见，可谁说得准，顺手正好布它一道疑踪。预备好了？那就跟上我，窗子不用管了。」

===Original===
With that he dropped softly into the street, and I after him, turning to the right instead of the left, and that at a brisk trot instead of the innocent walk which had brought us to the bank. Like mice we scampered past the great schoolroom, with its gable snipping a paler sky than ever, and the shadows melting even in the colonnade underneath. Masters’ houses flitted by on the left, lesser landmarks on either side, and presently we were running our heads into the dawn, one under either hedge of the Stockley road.
===Chinese===
话音未落，他已轻轻落进街心，我跟着落下；我们向右拐而不是向左，而且把来银行时那副清白的步子，换成了轻快的小跑。我们像两只耗子，窜过那座大讲堂——它的山墙正把愈发灰白的天空剪出口子，连底下柱廊里的暗影也在消融。教师宅邸在左侧掠过，小界标在两侧闪过；不多时，我们已一头扎进黎明，一人在斯托克利路这道树篱下，一人在那道树篱下。

===Original===
“Did you see that light in Nab’s just now?” cried Raffles as he led.

“No; why?” I panted, nearly spent.
===Chinese===
「刚才纳布（Nab）家那盏灯，你瞧见没有？」领跑的拉夫尔斯喊道。

「没瞧见；怎么？」我气喘吁吁，力气几乎见底。

===Original===
“It was in Nab’s dressing-room.”

“Yes?”
===Chinese===
「灯就亮在纳布的更衣室里。」

「后来呢？」

===Original===
“I’ve seen it there before,” continued Raffles. “He never was a good sleeper, and his ears reach to the street. I wouldn’t like to say how often I was chased by him in the small hours! I believe he knew who it was toward the end, but Nab was not the man to accuse you of what he couldn’t prove.”
===Chinese===
「那盏灯，我从前往那儿也见过的。」拉夫尔斯接着说。「他从来睡不沉，一双耳朵一直灵到街面上。后半夜叫他追过多少回，我都不敢细说！我相信到末了，他已经知道追的是谁了；可纳布不是那种人：拿不出凭据的事，他不往你头上栽。」

===Original===
I had no breath for comment. And on sped Raffles like a yacht before the wind, and on I blundered like a wherry at sea, making heavy weather all the way, and nearer foundering at every stride. Suddenly, to my deep relief, Raffles halted, but only to tell me to stop my pipes while he listened.
===Chinese===
我连品评一句的气力都没有了。拉夫尔斯像顺风里的快艇一路飞驰；我像海上的平底驳船一路跌撞，全程顶风抗浪，每跨一步都更接近沉没。突然，拉夫尔斯收住了脚——我大大松了口气——却只为吩咐我收声闭嘴，他要听。

===Original===
“It’s all right, Bunny,” he resumed, showing me a glowing face in the dawn. “History’s on its own tracks once more, and I’ll bet you it’s dear old Nab on ours! Come on, Bunny; run to the last gasp, and leave the rest to me.”
===Chinese===
「没事了，邦尼。」他又开口，在曙光里向我亮出一张红光满面的脸。「历史又走上了自己的老轨道；我敢同你打赌，走上咱们这条道的，正是亲爱的老纳布！来吧，邦尼；跑到咽气为止，余下的交给我。」

===Original===
I was past arguing, and away he went. There was no help for it but to follow as best I could. Yet I had vastly preferred to collapse on the spot, and trust to Raffles’s resource, as before very long I must. I had never enjoyed long wind and the hours that we kept in town may well have aggravated the deficiency. Raffles, however, was in first-class training from first-class cricket, and he had no mercy on Nab or me. But the master himself was an old Oxford miler, who could still bear it better than I; nay, as I flagged and stumbled, I heard him pounding steadily behind.
===Chinese===
争辩于我已成过去，他拔腿就走。没法子，只好尽力跟上。可我巴不得当场瘫倒，把一切托付给拉夫尔斯的急智——反正不出多久，我也必定如此。我生来不是长气，在城里熬的那些时辰，只怕更把这点短处亏深了。拉夫尔斯却有甲级板球练出的上好底子，他对纳布和我，一概不留情。可这位老师本人是牛津的老一英里跑手，至今还扛得住，比我扛得住；可不，正当我气衰步乱，我听见他一路沉稳地踏在我身后。

===Original===
“Come on, come on, or he’ll do us!” cried Raffles shrilly over his shoulder; and a gruff sardonic laugh came back over mine. It was pearly morning now, but we had run into a shallow mist that took me by the throat and stabbed me to the lungs. I coughed and coughed, and stumbled in my stride, until down I went, less by accident than to get it over, and so lay headlong in my tracks. And old Nab dealt me a verbal kick as he passed.
===Chinese===
「快跑，快跑，不然他要拿下咱们了！」拉夫尔斯回头尖声喊；一声粗嘎的冷笑也从我肩头传了回来。此刻已是珍珠色的清晨，可我们跑进了一片浅雾，它掐住我的咽喉，直扎进我的肺。我咳了又咳，一踉跄接一踉跄，最后索性倒了——与其说失足，不如说索性倒个干净——就势一头扑倒在自己的脚印上。老纳布打我身边跑过，赏了我一句嘴上的飞脚。

===Original===
“You beast!” he growled, as I have known him growl it in form.

But Raffles himself had abandoned the flight on hearing my downfall, and I was on hands and knees just in time to see the meeting between him and old Nab. And there stood Raffles in the silvery mist, laughing with his whole light heart, leaning back to get the full flavor of his mirth; and, nearer me, sturdy old Nab, dour and grim, with beads of dew on the hoary beard that had been lamp-black in our time.
===Chinese===
「你这畜生！」他低吼道，一如我从前在课堂上听他吼过的那样。

倒是拉夫尔斯一听见我倒下，自己先弃了逃奔；我手脚并用地爬起，恰好赶上看见他与老纳布会面。只见拉夫尔斯立在银灰的雾里，整颗轻快的心都在笑，还向后仰着身子，好把这份欢畅尝个透；离我近些的，是壮实的老纳布，又倔又冷峻，花白胡须上凝着露珠——我们那时候，那部胡子还黑得像灯烟。

===Original===
“So I’ve caught you at last!” said he. “After more years than I mean to count!”

“Then you’re luckier than we are, sir,” answered Raffles, “for I fear our man has given us the slip.”
===Chinese===
「这么说，我总算逮住你了！」他说。「隔的年头多得我都不屑数了！」

「那您可比我们走运，先生。」拉夫尔斯答道，「因为我们那位，恐怕已经甩脱了我们。」

===Original===
“Your man!” echoed Nab. His bushy eyebrows had shot up: it was as much as I could do to keep my own in their place.
===Chinese===
「你们的那位！」纳布应声道。他的浓眉直竖了起来：我费了好大的劲，才管住我自己的眉毛没飞起来。

===Original===
“We were indulging in the chase ourselves,” explained Raffles, “and one of us has suffered for his zeal, as you can see. It is even possible that we, too, have been chasing a perfectly innocent man.”

“Not to say a reformed character,” said our pursuer dryly. “I suppose you don’t mean a member of the school?” he added, pinking his man suddenly as of yore, with all the old barbed acumen. But Raffles was now his match.
===Chinese===
「我们自己正追猎得起劲呢。」拉夫尔斯解释道，「其中一位还为这份热忱吃了苦头，您都瞧见了。甚至有可能，我们追的也是一位全然无辜的人。」

「岂止，没准还是位改过自新的角色。」我们的追捕者干巴巴地说。「我猜你们指的，总不会是本校的人吧？」他补了一句，像当年一样冷不防朝他的对手刺出一剑，还是那股带倒钩的老辣。可如今的拉夫尔斯，足堪同他匹敌。

===Original===
“That would be carrying reformation rather far, sir. No, as I say, I may have been mistaken in the first instance; but I had put out my light and was looking out of the window when I saw a fellow behaving quite suspiciously. He was carrying his boots and creeping along in his socks﻿—which must be why you never heard him, sir. They make less noise than rubber soles even﻿—that is, they must, you know! Well, Bunny had just left me, so I hauled him out and we both crept down to play detective. No sign of the fellow! We had a look in the colonnade﻿—I thought I heard him﻿—and that gave us no end of a hunt for nothing. But just as we were leaving he came padding past under our noses, and that’s where we took up the chase. Where he’d been in the meantime I have no idea; very likely he’d done no harm; but it seemed worth while finding out. He had too good a start, though, and poor Bunny had too bad a wind.”
===Chinese===
「那这『改过自新』可就改得太远了，先生。不，正如我说的，起初我兴许看错了；可当时我灭了灯，正朝窗外张望，就看见一个家伙行迹十分可疑。他手里提着靴子，只穿袜子蹑手蹑脚地走——您一路没听见动静，毛病必定出在这儿，先生。袜子比胶底还轻——呃，想必总归轻些，您知道的！正巧邦尼刚离开我，我就把他拽了出来，我们俩蹑手蹑脚下楼，扮了回侦探。那家伙毫无踪影！我们到柱廊里查看了一回——我仿佛听见了他——结果害我们空追了好一大场。可我们正要走，他却踏着袜子底，从我们鼻子底下溜了过去——我们的追逐就是打那儿接上的。这中间他上哪儿去了，我毫无头绪；多半也没干什么坏事；可弄个明白总还值得。只是他起跑的先手太大，可怜的邦尼这口气又太短。」

===Original===
“You should have gone on and let me rip,” said I, climbing to my feet at last.
===Chinese===
「你本该只管追下去，别管我。」我说着，终于爬起身来。

===Original===
“As it is, however, we will all let the other fellow do so,” said old Nab in a genial growl. “And you two had better turn into my house and have something to keep the morning cold out.”
===Chinese===
「不过事到如今，那位的『追』，就让他自己去追个够吧。」老纳布宽厚地低吼道。「你们俩最好到我屋里去转一转，喝点什么，把清晨的寒气挡在外面。」

===Original===
You may imagine with what alacrity we complied; and yet I am bound to confess that I had never liked Nab at school. I still remember my term in his form. He had a caustic tongue and fine assortment of damaging epithets, most of which were levelled at my devoted skull during those three months. I now discovered that he also kept a particularly mellow Scotch whiskey, an excellent cigar, and a fund of anecdote of which a mordant wit was the worthy bursar. Enough to add that he kept us laughing in his study until the chapel bells rang him out.
===Chinese===
我们何等爽快地领命，可想而知；可我必须招认，上学那会儿，我从来不曾喜欢过纳布。我在他班上的那一学期，至今记忆犹新。他有一条刻薄的舌头，和一整套装人的字眼，那三个月里，那些字眼大半都倾倒在我这颗忠心的脑袋上。如今我方才发现，他还藏着一瓶格外醇和的苏格兰威士忌、一手极好的雪茄，和一肚子掌故——替这批掌故司账的，是一位当之无愧的刻薄妙手。只消再补一句：他让我们在他书房里一直笑到礼拜堂的钟声把他请出门去。

===Original===
As for Raffles, he appeared to me to feel far more compunction for the fable which he had been compelled to foist upon one of the old masters than for the immeasurably graver offence against society and another Old Boy. This, indeed, did not worry him at all; and the story was received next day with absolute credulity on all sides. Nasmyth himself was the first to thank us both for our spirited effort on his behalf; and the incident had the ironic effect of establishing an immediate entente cordiale between Raffles and his very latest victim. I must confess, however, that for my own part I was thoroughly uneasy during the Old Boys’ second innings, when Raffles made a selfish score, instead of standing by me to tell his own story in his own way. There was never any knowing with what new detail he was about to embellish it: and I have still to receive full credit for the tact that it required to follow his erratic lead convincingly. Seldom have I been more thankful than when our train started next morning, and the poor, unsuspecting Nasmyth himself waved us a last farewell from the platform.
===Chinese===
至于拉夫尔斯，据我看来，他被迫哄给一位老师听的那套瞎话，远比那桩对社会、对另一位老校友犯下的、重得不可计量的罪过，更叫他过意不去。后者，说实在的，竟丝毫没搅扰过他；到了第二天，那故事四面八方都信得实实的。纳斯密斯本人头一个感谢我们二人代他出力的那番豪举；这件事还落下一个讽刺的结果：拉夫尔斯同他这位最新的受害者之间，随即结成了亲善协约。只是我必须招认，老校友赛第二局轮到拉夫尔斯打出他那份自私的比分、而不肯站在我身边照他自己的路数亲口讲述这故事时，我心里着实七上八下。谁也说得准他下一刻又要往这故事里装点什么新的枝节；而要我令人信服地跟上他那天马行空的引路，这中间需要的急智，我到今天还没领足功劳。第二天早晨火车开动、可怜的、毫无察觉的纳斯密斯本人在站台上向我们挥手送别时，我很少那样庆幸过。

===Original===
“Lucky we weren’t staying at Nab’s,” said Raffles, as he lit a Sullivan and opened his *Daily Mail* at its report of the robbery. “There was one thing Nab would have spotted like the downy old bird he always was and will be.”
===Chinese===
「幸亏咱们没住在纳布家。」拉夫尔斯说着，点起一支沙利文，把《每日邮报》（*Daily Mail*）翻到那桩抢劫案的报道上。「有一处破绽，纳布准会看出来——他这只老练的鸟，一向如此，往后也还是如此。」

===Original===
“What was that?”

“The front door must have been found duly barred and bolted in the morning, and yet we let them assume that we came out that way. Nab would have pounced on the point, and by this time we might have been nabbed ourselves.”
===Chinese===
「哪一处？」

「清晨人们发现前门时，它必定好好地上着闩、插着销，可咱们却由他们认定，咱们是从那儿出去的。这一点纳布准会扑上来咬住不放；到这会儿，被逮住的可能就是咱们自己了。」

===Original===
It was but a little over a hundred sovereigns that Raffles had taken, and, of course, he had resolutely eschewed any and every form of paper money. He posted his own first contribution of twenty-five pounds to the Founder’s Fund immediately on our return to town, before rushing off to more first-class cricket, and I gathered that the rest would follow piecemeal as he deemed it safe. By an odd coincidence, however, a mysterious but magnificent donation of a hundred guineas was almost simultaneously received in notes by the treasurer of the Founder’s Fund, from one who simply signed himself “Old Boy.” The treasurer happened to be our late host, the new man at our old house, and he wrote to congratulate Raffles on what he was pleased to consider a direct result of the latter’s speech. I did not see the letter that Raffles wrote in reply, but in due course I heard the name of the mysterious contributor. He was said to be no other than Nipper Nasmyth himself. I asked Raffles if it was true. He replied that he would ask old Nipper point-blank if he came up as usual to the Varsity match, and if they had the luck to meet. And not only did this happen, but I had the greater luck to be walking round the ground with Raffles when we encountered our shabby friend in front of the pavilion.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯拿走的，不过一百零几枚金镑；而且自然而然，纸币的这样那样，他一概坚决不沾。一回到城里，他立即把自己头一笔二十五镑的认捐汇给了创校基金，随后便赶去打更多的甲级板球；我私下忖度，余下的款项，会随他觉得稳妥，零敲碎打地跟上去。然而说来也怪，几乎同一时间，创校基金的司库收到一笔一百几尼的神秘而豪阔的捐款，以钞票付讫，署名单单一个「老校友」。这位司库，恰好便是我们新近的那位主人、我们老宅里的新当家；他写信向拉夫尔斯道贺，庆他欣然视作后者那篇演说的直接成果。拉夫尔斯的回信我没有见着，不过时候一到，那位神秘捐客的名姓，还是传到了我耳朵里。据说不是别人，正是尼珀·纳斯密斯本人。我问拉夫尔斯这是不是真的。他答道，老尼珀若照例上来打大学对抗赛（Varsity match），他们又有幸相逢，他便直截了当去问他。这话不但应了验，我还走了更大的运：正同拉夫尔斯绕场漫步，就在球员亭前，迎面撞上了我们这位寒酸的朋友。

===Original===
“My dear fellow,” cried Raffles, “I hear it was you who gave that hundred guineas by stealth to the very movement you denounced. Don’t deny it, and don’t blush to find it fame. Listen to me. There was a great lot in what you said; but it’s the kind of thing we ought all to back, whether we strictly approve of it in our hearts or not.”
===Chinese===
「亲爱的伙计，」拉夫尔斯喊道，「我听说，那笔一百几尼的款子，正是你偷偷捐给你亲口痛斥的那场运动的。别抵赖，也别因发觉自己出了名而脸红。听我说。你那番话里，好处多得很；可这一类事，正该我们人人都支持，不管我们心底里是不是当真赞成。」

===Original===
“Exactly, Raffles, but the fact is﻿—”

“I know what you’re going to say. Don’t say it. There’s not one in a thousand who would do as you’ve done, and not one in a million who would do it anonymously.”
===Chinese===
「正是这话，拉夫尔斯，可实情是——」

「你要说什么我知道。别说。你做的事，一千个人里没有一个做得出；不具名地做，一百万人里也没有一个。」

===Original===
“But what makes you think I did it, Raffles?”

“Everybody is saying so. You will find it all over the place when you get back. You will find yourself the most popular man down there, Nasmyth!”
===Chinese===
「可你凭什么认定是我做的，拉夫尔斯？」

「人人都这么讲。你回去便会发现，这事儿传遍了各处。你还会发现，你成了那底下最得人缘的人，纳斯密斯！」

===Original===
I never saw a nobler embarrassment than that of this awkward, ungainly, cantankerous man: all his angles seemed to have been smoothed away: there was something quite human in the flushed, undecided, wistful face.
===Chinese===
我从未见过比这更堂皇的一场窘——落在这个别扭、粗笨、好斗的人身上：他周身的棱角仿佛都磨平了：那张涨红的、举棋不定的、怅惘的脸上，有一种十足人性的东西。

===Original===
“I never was popular in my life,” he said. “I don’t want to buy my popularity now. To be perfectly candid with you, Raffles﻿—”

“Don’t! I can’t stop to hear. They’re ringing the bell. But you shouldn’t have been angry with me for saying you were a generous good chap, Nasmyth, when you were one all the time. Goodbye, old fellow!”
===Chinese===
「我这辈子没得人缘过。」他说。「我也不想如今来买人缘。跟你完全交底，拉夫尔斯——」

「别说！我停不得身听。他们摇铃了。可我说你是位慷慨的好人，你原本不该生我的气，纳斯密斯——你自始至终都是这么个人。再会，老伙计！」

===Original===
But Nasmyth detained us a second more. His hesitation was at an end. There was a sudden new light in his face.

“Was I?” he cried. “Then I’ll make it *two* hundred, and damn the odds!”
===Chinese===
可纳斯密斯又把我们多留了一秒。他的踌躇到头了。他脸上忽然亮起一道新的光。

「我是这么个人？」他喊道。「那我就凑成*两*百，去他的赔率！」

===Original===
Raffles was a thoughtful man as we went to our seats. He saw nobody, would acknowledge no remark. Neither did he attend to the cricket for the first half-hour after lunch; instead, he eventually invited me to come for a stroll on the practice ground, where, however, we found two chairs aloof from the fascinating throng.
===Chinese===
走回座位的路上，拉夫尔斯满腹心事。他谁也不瞧，谁的搭话也不应。午饭后头半个钟头，他连球赛也没看；末了，他邀我上练习场去遛一遛；到了那儿，我们却寻了两把椅子，远远避开那迷人的众人。

===Original===
“I am not often sorry, Bunny, as you know,” he began. “But I have been sorry since the interval. I’ve been sorry for poor old Nipper Nasmyth. Did you see the idea of being popular dawn upon him for the first time in his life?”
===Chinese===
「你知道，邦尼，我不是个常怀歉疚的人。」他开了腔。「可打中场休息起，我一直怀着歉疚。我为可怜的老尼珀·纳斯密斯歉疚。『得人缘』这个念头，在他这辈子头一遭破晓——你瞧见了么？」

===Original===
“I did; but you had nothing to do with that, my dear man.”

Raffles shook his head over me as our eyes met. “I had everything to do with it. I tried to make him tell the meanest lie. I made sure he would, and for that matter he nearly did. Then, at the last moment, he saw how to hedge things with his conscience. And his second hundred will be a real gift.”
===Chinese===
「瞧见了；可这跟你不相干呀，亲爱的。」

拉夫尔斯迎着我的目光，朝我摇了摇头。「相干得很。我存心逼他说一桩最下作的谎。我拿得准他会说；说真的，他也险些说了。可到了最后一刻，他看出了法子，同他的良心两讫对冲。而他那第二个一百，将是一份真心实意的馈赠。」

===Original===
“You mean under his own name﻿—”

“And with his own free will. My good Bunny, is it possible you don’t know what I did with the hundred we drew from that bank!”
===Chinese===
「你是说，用他本人的名字——」

「而且是出于他本人的自由意志。我的好邦尼，咱们从那家银行提出的那一百镑，我拿它派了什么用场，你竟会不知道！」

===Original===
“I knew what you were going to do with it,” said I. “I didn’t know you had actually got further than the twenty-five you told me you were sending as your own contribution.”

Raffles rose abruptly from his chair.
===Chinese===
「你打算拿它派什么用场，我知道。」我说。「只是你说要汇二十五镑算你自己认捐的那笔，我没料到你当真又往前走出了这么远。」

拉夫尔斯猛地从椅子里立起身。

===Original===
“And you actually thought that came out of his money?”

“Naturally.”
===Chinese===
「而你还当真以为，那笔钱是从他的钱里出的？」

「这不是自然而然么。」

===Original===
“In my name?”

“I thought so.”
===Chinese===
「用我的名义？」

「我原以为是。」

===Original===
Raffles stared at me inscrutably for some moments, and for some more at the great white numbers over the grandstand.
===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯莫测高深地盯了我片刻，又盯了片刻大看台顶上那几个巨大的白色数字。

===Original===
“We may as well have another look at the cricket,” said he. “It’s difficult to see the board from here, but I believe there’s another man out.”
===Chinese===
「咱们不妨再去看看球。」他说。「从这儿瞧记分牌吃力，不过我想，又出局一人了。」

## A Bad Night / 糟糕的一夜

===Original===
There was to be a certain little wedding in which Raffles and I took a surreptitious interest. The bride-elect was living in some retirement, with a recently widowed mother and an asthmatical brother, in a mellow hermitage on the banks of the Mole. The bridegroom was a prosperous son of the same suburban soil which had nourished both families for generations. The wedding presents were so numerous as to fill several rooms at the pretty retreat upon the Mole, and of an intrinsic value calling for a special transaction with the Burglary Insurance Company in Cheapside. I cannot say how Raffles obtained all this information. I only know that it proved correct in each particular. I was not indeed deeply interested before the event, since Raffles assured me that it was “a one-man job,” and naturally intended to be the one man himself. It was only at the eleventh hour that our positions were inverted by the wholly unexpected selection of Raffles for the English team in the Second Test Match.

===Chinese===
有一桩不起眼的小小婚事，拉夫尔斯（Raffles）和我正对它怀着一份鬼鬼祟祟的兴趣。待嫁的新娘，随一位新寡的母亲和一个患哮喘的弟弟，深居简出，住在摩尔河（Mole）畔一座古雅的隐庐里。新郎则是同一片伦敦近郊水土养出的富家子——那片水土滋育这两个家族，已历数代。婚礼贺礼多得摆满了摩尔河畔那座秀美僻静宅邸的好几个房间，其内在价值之可观，还须同奇普赛德（Cheapside）的盗窃保险公司（Burglary Insurance Company）专办一桩保险。这些情报拉夫尔斯是从哪儿弄来的，我说不上。我只知道，事后证明它桩桩属实。事前我本不甚上心，因为拉夫尔斯向我担保，这是「一个人的活儿」，而且顺理成章，打算自己来做那一个人。直到最后关头，局面才陡然倒转：拉夫尔斯出乎万般意料，入选了英格兰队，出战第二场对抗赛（Test Match）。

===Original===
In a flash I saw the chance of my criminal career. It was some years since Raffles had served his country in these encounters; he had never thought to be called upon again, and his gratification was only less than his embarrassment. The match was at Old Trafford, on the third Thursday, Friday, and Saturday in July; the other affair had been all arranged for the Thursday night, the night of the wedding at East Molesey. It was for Raffles to choose between the two excitements, and for once I helped him to make up his mind. I duly pointed out to him that in Surrey, at all events, I was quite capable of taking his place. Nay, more, I insisted at once on my prescriptive right and on his patriotic obligation in the matter. In the country’s name and in my own, I implored him to give it and me a chance; and for once, as I say, my arguments prevailed. Raffles sent his telegram﻿—it was the day before the match. We then rushed down to Esher, and over every inch of the ground by that characteristically circuitous route which he enjoined on me for the next night. And at six in the evening I was receiving the last of my many instructions through a window of the restaurant car.

===Chinese===
一瞬间，我望见了我犯罪生涯的机会。拉夫尔斯上一次在这样的交锋里为国效力，已是好几年前的事；他从没想过还会被征召，他的得意，只比他的窘迫略逊一筹。比赛在老特拉福德（Old Trafford），七月第三个星期四、五、六；另一桩事早已全盘定在星期四夜里——东莫尔西（East Molesey）行婚礼的那一夜。两番惊险，去就之间，本该由拉夫尔斯来挑，而这回，是我帮他拿定了主意。我及时向他指出：无论如何，在萨里郡（Surrey），我完全顶得了他的缺。非但如此，我还立刻援引起我的惯例权利，和他爱国的义务。我以国家的名义、也以我个人的名义，恳求他给这件事、也给我一个机会；而这一回，如我所说，我的说辞奏了效。拉夫尔斯发出了电报——那是在比赛前一天。随后我们赶到伊舍（Esher），循着他替我定下的、次日夜间要走的那条十足迂回的路线，把那片地方的每一寸都踏勘了一遍。到傍晚六点，我已经在餐车窗口，领受他那没完没了的叮嘱中的最后几条了。

===Original===
“Only promise me not to take a revolver,” said Raffles in a whisper. “Here are my keys; there’s an old life-preserver somewhere in the bureau; take that, if you like﻿—though what you take I rather fear you are the chap to use!”

“Then the rope be round my own neck!” I whispered back. “Whatever else I may do, Raffles, I shan’t give *you* away; and you’ll find I do better than you think, and am worth trusting with a little more to do, or I’ll know the reason why!”

===Chinese===
「只求你答应我别带左轮，」拉夫尔斯悄声道。「钥匙在这儿；写字台里什么地方有一根老护身短棒（life-preserver）；你愿意就带上它——不过我怕，不管你带上什么，你都是那种当真会用上一用的家伙！」

「那就让绞索套上我自己的脖子好了！」我也悄声回敬。「不论我还会闯出什么祸，拉夫尔斯，我决不出卖你；你会发现我干得比你以为的好，也配得上再托付些事情做——不然我非得问出个究竟不可！」

===Original===
And I meant to know it, as he was borne out of Euston with raised eyebrows, and I turned grimly on my heel. I saw his fears for me; and nothing could have made me more fearless for myself. Raffles had been wrong about me all these years; now was my chance to set him right. It was galling to feel that he had no confidence in my coolness or my nerve, when neither had ever failed him at a pinch. I had been loyal to him through rough and smooth. In many an ugly corner I had stood as firm as Raffles himself. I was his right hand, and yet he never hesitated to make me his catspaw. This time, at all events, I should be neither one nor the other; this time I was the understudy playing lead at last; and I wish I could think that Raffles ever realized with what gusto I threw myself into his part.

===Chinese===
我也当真打算问出个明白——眼看他扬着眉，被火车载出尤斯顿（Euston），我咬着牙转身而去。我看出了他对我的忧惧；而再没有什么比这更叫我无所畏惧的了。这些年，拉夫尔斯始终看错了我；如今正是我让他认清的机会。一想到他竟不信我的沉着与胆气，便觉气闷难当——危急关头，我这两样何曾误过他的事。顺境逆境，我都对他忠心耿耿。多少凶险的角落里，我站得同拉夫尔斯本人一样稳。我是他的右臂，他却从不迟疑拿我当火中取栗的猫爪。这一回，无论如何，我两者都不再做了；这一回，我是替补的演员，终于登台主演；我只巴望拉夫尔斯多少能体会到，我揣进他那个角色里去，是怀着怎样的兴头。

===Original===
Thus I was first out of a crowded theatre train at Esher next night, and first down the stairs into the open air. The night was close and cloudy; and the road to Hampton Court, even now that the suburban builder has marked much of it for his own, is one of the darkest I know. The first mile is still a narrow avenue, a mere tunnel of leaves at midsummer; but at that time there was not a lighted pane or cranny by the way. Naturally, it was in this blind reach that I fancied I was being followed. I stopped in my stride; so did the steps I made sure I had heard not far behind; and when I went on, they followed suit. I dried my forehead as I walked, but soon brought myself to repeat the experiment when an exact repetition of the result went to convince me that it had been my own echo all the time. And since I lost it on getting quit of the avenue, and coming out upon the straight and open road, I was not long in recovering from my scare. But now I could see my way, and found the rest of it without mishap, though not without another semblance of adventure. Over the bridge across the Mole, when about to turn to the left, I marched straight upon a policeman in rubber soles. I had to call him “officer” as I passed, and to pass my turning by a couple of hundred yards, before venturing back another way.

===Chinese===
于是第二天夜里，在伊舍，我头一个挤出满载的剧场专列，头一个下台阶走进露天的夜色。夜气闷热，浓云低垂；通往汉普顿宫（Hampton Court）的那条路，即便如今近郊的营造商已把它大半圈作己有，仍是我所知最黑的路之一。头一英里至今仍是一条窄窄的林荫道，仲夏时节简直是一条树叶的隧道；而在那时，沿途没有一扇亮着的窗，没有一线透光的缝。自然，偏偏就在这段盲肠似的暗路上，我疑心被人跟上了。我脚下一定；我吃准听见的不远处那阵脚步声也定了；我再走，它应声又起。我一面走一面抹额头上的汗；待到情形分毫不差地重演一遍，我便再试了一回，这才信了：那从头到尾不过是自己的回声。既然一出林荫道、踏上笔直敞亮的大路，那声音便丢了，我的惊惶也就没过多久平复了下去。不过这一来我已认得路，余下的一段走来并无闪失——只是又添了一场貌似惊险的遭遇。在跨过摩尔河的桥上，我正待左转，却一头撞上一位穿胶底鞋的警察。我只得在走过他身边时唤他一声「长官」，还只得直走过我的路口两百来码，才敢绕另一条路折回来。

===Original===
At last I had crept through a garden gate, and round by black windows to a black lawn drenched with dew. It had been a heating walk, and I was glad to blunder on a garden seat, most considerately placed under a cedar which added its own darkness to that of the night. Here I rested a few minutes, putting up my feet to keep them dry, untying my shoes to save time, and generally facing the task before me with a coolness which I strove to make worthy of my absent chief. But mine was a self-conscious quality, as far removed from the original as any other deliberate imitation of genius. I actually struck a match on my trousers, and lit one of the shorter Sullivans. Raffles himself would not have done such a thing at such a moment. But I wished to tell him that I had done it; and in truth I was not more than pleasurably afraid; I had rather that impersonal curiosity as to the issue which has been the saving of me in still more precarious situations. I even grew impatient for the fray, and could not after all sit still as long as I had intended. So it happened that I was finishing my cigarette on the edge of the wet lawn, and about to slip off my shoes before stepping across the gravel to the conservatory door, when a most singular sound arrested me in the act. It was a muffled gasping somewhere overhead. I stood like stone; and my listening attitude must have been visible against the milky sheen of the lawn, for a labored voice hailed me sternly from a window.

===Chinese===
终于，我钻过了一道园门，贴着一扇扇黑黢黢的窗，绕到一片让露水浸透的黑草坪。这一路走得人身上发热，幸而叫我撞见一张花园座椅，安放得极知趣，在一株雪松底下，雪松又给夜色添上它自带的一份黑。我在这里歇了几分钟，把脚架起来免得沾湿，解开鞋带好省些工夫，总而言之，拿一副冷静来面对眼前的活计——这副冷静，我竭力想让它配得上我不在场的主子。怎奈我这份冷静是自觉自致的货色，同原件相去之远，不差于任何对天才的刻意仿造。我竟当真在裤腿上划了根火柴，点起一支短一号的沙利文（Sullivan）。换了拉夫尔斯本人，在这样的节骨眼上决计不来这一套。可我正巴不得告诉他，我来过了这一套；说实在的，我的害怕也不过是种快意的害怕；对于结局，我倒怀着一种置身事外的好奇——在后来凶险得多的处境里救我于水火的，正是这份好奇。我甚至等得心焦，终究没能按原先打算的那样久坐。于是便赶巧了：我正在湿草坪边上抽完那支烟，正要脱下鞋、跨过砾石地走向花房的门，一个无比古怪的声响使我猛然顿住。那是头顶上什么地方一阵闷住的喘息。我石像般钉在原地；草坪泛着乳白的光泽，我那侧耳倾听的姿态必定历历可见，因为一副喘得艰难的嗓音，从一扇窗口厉声喝问起我来。

===Original===
“Who on earth are you?” it wheezed.

“A detective officer,” I replied, “sent down by the Burglary Insurance Company.”

===Chinese===
「你到底是谁？」那嗓音喘着问。

「侦探警官，」我答道，「盗窃保险公司派下来的。」

===Original===
Not a moment had I paused for my precious fable. It had all been prepared for me by Raffles, in case of need. I was merely repeating a lesson in which I had been closely schooled. But at the window there was pause enough, filled only by the uncanny wheezing of the man I could not see.

“I don’t see why they should have sent you down,” he said at length. “We are being quite well looked after by the local police; they’re giving us a special call every hour.”

“I know that, Mr. Medlicott,” I rejoined on my own account. “I met one of them at the corner just now, and we passed the time of night.”

===Chinese===
我那套金贵的谎话，半拍也不曾迟疑。这本是拉夫尔斯替我备好的，以防万一。我不过是在背诵一课受过他严格调教的功课。倒是窗口那边踌躇了好一阵，只有那个我看不见的人一阵阵瘆人的喘鸣，填补着这段沉寂。

「我不明白他们干吗还派你下来，」他终于开口。「本地警察把我们照看得好好儿的；他们每个钟头都专门来巡一次。」

「这个我知道，梅德利科特（Medlicott）先生，」我自行应道。「方才我在拐角上还碰见他们一位，报了报夜里的时辰呢。」

===Original===
My heart was knocking me to bits. I had started for myself at last.

“Did you get my name from him?” pursued my questioner, in a suspicious wheeze.

“No; they gave me that before I started,” I replied. “But I’m sorry you saw me, sir; it’s a mere matter of routine, and not intended to annoy anybody. I propose to keep a watch on the place all night, but I own it wasn’t necessary to trespass as I’ve done. I’ll take myself off the actual premises, if you prefer it.”

===Chinese===
我的心擂得我七零八落。我终于头一回，独自开场了。

「我的名字是听他说的么？」盘问我的人追问，喘声里透着狐疑。

「不是；动身之前他们就告诉我了，」我答道。「不过，先生，很抱歉叫您瞧见了；这纯属例行公事，并不想搅扰谁。我打算整夜看守这宅子，可我也认了，本不必像这样擅入。您若愿意，我就退到宅子的地界外头去。」

===Original===
This again was all my own; and it met with a success that might have given me confidence.

“Not a bit of it,” replied young Medlicott, with a grim geniality. “I’ve just woke up with the devil of an attack of asthma, and may have to sit up in my chair till morning. You’d better come up and see me through, and kill two birds while you’re about it. Stay where you are, and I’ll come down and let you in.”

===Chinese===
这几句又全是我自己的即兴；而收到的成效，本也够给我添些信心了。

「哪儿的话，」小梅德利科特答道，透着一股冷面热心。「我这刚醒，一场哮喘闹得邪火，怕是得在椅子里坐到天亮了。你不如上楼来，陪我熬过这一宿，顺手还一举两得。站在原地别动，我这就下来给你开门。」

===Original===
Here was a dilemma which Raffles himself had not foreseen! Outside, in the dark, my audacious part was not hard to play; but to carry the improvisation indoors was to double at once the difficulty and the risk. It was true that I had purposely come down in a true detective’s overcoat and bowler; but my personal appearance was hardly of the detective type. On the other hand as the soi-disant guardian of the gifts one might only excite suspicion by refusing to enter the house where they were. Nor could I forget that it was my purpose to effect such entry first or last. That was the casting consideration. I decided to take my dilemma by the horns.

===Chinese===
这一下可好，两难临头，连拉夫尔斯本人也不曾料到！在外头的黑地里，我那胆大包天的角儿并不难唱；可要把即兴戏搬进屋去，难度和风险登时就得翻上一番。不错，我是特意穿一件地道的侦探式大衣、戴一顶圆顶硬礼帽下来的；可我这张脸，实在不是当侦探的那一型。可话说回来，既以礼物守护人自居，偏又不肯进那存放礼物的宅门，只会更惹人疑心。何况我忘不了，我的本意原是迟早也要进这宅子。这是定乾坤的一条。我决意揪住这对犄角，迎头而上。

===Original===
There had been a scraping of matches in the room over the conservatory; the open window had shown for a moment, like an empty picture-frame, a gigantic shadow wavering on the ceiling; and in the next half-minute I remembered to tie my shoes. But the light was slow to reappear through the leaded glasses of an outer door farther along the path. And when the door opened, it was a figure of woe that stood within and held an unsteady candle between our faces.

I have seen old men look half their age, and young men look double theirs; but never before or since have I seen a beardless boy bent into a man of eighty, gasping for every breath, shaken by every gasp, swaying, tottering, and choking, as if about to die upon his feet. Yet with it all, young Medlicott overhauled me shrewdly, and it was several moments before he would let me take the candle from him.

===Chinese===
方才花房顶上那间屋里响过一阵划火柴的窸窣；敞开的窗子一度像一只空画框，框着一个在天花板上摇曳的巨大黑影；跟着那半分钟里，我想起来该把鞋带系上。可是顺着小径更远处一扇外门的铅条玻璃，灯光迟迟才重新透出。门开了，立在门里的却是一副愁苦的身形，在我们两张脸中间擎着一支摇曳的蜡烛。

我见过老人看着只有半辈子年纪，也见过年轻人看着翻了一倍；可从前不曾、往后也不曾再见一个无须的少年，佝偻成一个八十岁的老翁，每一口气都喘得艰难，每一喘又震得他浑身乱颤，摇摇晃晃，踉踉跄跄，气哽喉塞，仿佛就要立着断气。然而饶是如此，小梅德利科特仍旧把我精明地打量了个透，过了好几拍，才肯让我从他手里接过蜡烛。

===Original===
“I shouldn’t have come down﻿—made me worse,” he began whispering in spurts. “Worse still going up again. You must give me an arm. You will come up? That’s right! Not as bad as I look, you know. Got some good whiskey, too. Presents are all right; but if they aren’t you’ll hear of it indoors sooner than out. Now I’m ready﻿—thanks! Mustn’t make more noise than we can help﻿—wake my mother.”

===Chinese===
「我本不该下来的——下这一趟更糟，」他一阵一阵地悄声说。「回头再上去，更是雪上加霜。你得扶我一把。你肯上来的，对吧？这就对了！我没有看上去那么不济，你放心。家里还有上好的威士忌。礼物都好好的；万一不好好的，你在屋里头也比我在这外头早听见动静。好了，我这就齐了——多谢！能不弄响就不弄响——别吵醒我母亲。」

===Original===
It must have taken us minutes to climb that single flight of stairs. There was just room for me to keep his arm in mine; with the other he hauled on the banisters; and so we mounted, step by step, a panting pause on each, and a pitched battle for breath on the half-landing. In the end we gained a cozy library, with an open door leading to a bedroom beyond. But the effort had deprived my poor companion of all power of speech; his laboring lungs shrieked like the wind; he could just point to the door by which we had entered, and which I shut in obedience to his gestures, and then to the decanter and its accessories on the table where he had left them overnight. I gave him nearly half a glassful, and his paroxysm subsided a little as he sat hunched up in a chair.

“I was a fool﻿ ﻿… to turn in,” he blurted in more whispers between longer pauses. “Lying down is the devil﻿ ﻿… when you’re in for a real bad night. You might get me the brown cigarettes﻿ ﻿… on the table in there. That’s right﻿ ﻿… thanks awfully﻿ ﻿… and now a match!”

===Chinese===
单是爬那一段楼梯，我们就准花了有好几分钟。我刚好容他一条胳膊架在我臂弯里；另一只手他拽着扶栏；我们就这样一步一歇往上挪，每登一级都要喘息一阵，到了半层平台，更是打了一场争取呼吸的硬仗。末了我们总算进了间暖融融的书房，一扇敞门通向里头的卧室。可这一番挣扎，夺尽了我那位可怜伙伴说话的气力；他那两片吃力的肺，嘶叫得像刮风；他只能指一指我们进来的那扇门——我依着他的手势把门关上——又指一指桌上那套他隔夜留在原处的细颈瓶和一应物件。我给他斟上小半杯，他蜷在椅子里，那阵抽搐总算平息了些许。

「我是个傻瓜……不该上床睡的，」他喘息的间隔更长了，断断续续地低语。「犯了真正难熬的夜，躺着顶要命……你替我把棕色的烟拿来……里屋桌上。这就对了……多谢多谢……再来一根火柴！」

===Original===
The asthmatic had bitten off either end of the stramonium cigarette, and was soon choking himself with the crude fumes, which he inhaled in desperate gulps, to exhale in furious fits of coughing. Never was more heroic remedy; it seemed a form of lingering suicide; but by degrees some slight improvement became apparent, and at length the sufferer was able to sit upright, and to drain his glass with a sigh of rare relief. I sighed also, for I had witnessed a struggle for dear life by a man in the flower of his youth, whose looks I liked, whose smile came like the sun through the first break in his torments, and whose first words were to thank me for the little I had done in bare humanity.

That made me feel the thing I was. But the feeling put me on my guard. And I was not unready for the remark which followed a more exhaustive scrutiny than I had hitherto sustained.

===Chinese===
那患气喘的已把曼陀罗（stramonium）香烟的两头咬掉，转眼便拿那股呛人的生烟自戕起来：他绝望地大口吞咽，又在一阵阵狂咳里狂喷。天底下再没有比这更悲壮的疗法；简直像一种慢性的自尽；可渐渐地，几分起色显出来了，受难的人末了竟能坐直身子，带着一声难得的宽慰，叹着把杯中酒干了。我也叹了一声，因为我目睹的是一场保命的搏斗，搏斗的人正当青春盛年，他的相貌我看着喜欢，他的笑容像照进苦难第一道裂缝的阳光，而他的第一句话，是谢我那点纯属人性本色的举手之劳。

这叫我掂出了自己是个什么东西。可这感觉也令我生了戒心。随后他把我从头到脚又一番彻底的打量，比先前任何一次都更不留情面；打量之后的那句话，我也并非毫无防备。

===Original===
“Do you know,” said young Medlicott, “that you aren’t a bit like the detective of my dreams?”

“Only to proud to hear it,” I replied. “There would be no point in my being in plain clothes if I looked exactly what I was.”

My companion reassured me with a wheezy laugh.

===Chinese===
「你可知道，」小梅德利科特说，「你一点也不像我梦里的那位侦探？」

「听这话我只觉得与有荣焉，」我答道。「我若看着就同我的行当一模一样，穿便衣还有什么用处。」

同伴喘着嗓音笑了笑，叫我安心。

===Original===
“There’s something in that,” said he, “although I do congratulate the insurance people on getting a man of your class to do their dirty work. And I congratulate myself,” he was quick enough to add, “on having you to see me through as bad a night as I’ve had for a long time. You’re like flowers in the depths of winter. Got a drink? That’s right! I suppose you didn’t happen to bring down an evening paper?”

I said I had brought one, but had unfortunately left it in the train.

===Chinese===
「这话在理，」他说，「虽说我真得恭喜保险公司，请动你这等人物来替他们干脏活。我也恭喜我自己，」他忙不迭添上一句，「这么些日子以来最难熬的一夜，有你看着我熬。你像数九寒冬里的花。来一杯？这就对了！你总不至于碰巧还带下来一份晚报吧？」

我说带是带了一份，可惜落在了火车上。

===Original===
“What about the Test Match?” cried my asthmatic, shooting forward in his chair.

“I can tell you that,” said I. “We went in first﻿—”

“Oh, I know all about that,” he interrupted. “I’ve seen the miserable score up to lunch. How many did we scrape altogether?”

===Chinese===
「对抗赛打得怎么样了？」我的气喘病人喊起来，整个人从椅子里猛探向前。

「这我倒能奉告，」我说。「咱们先上场击球——」

「哦，这段我知道，」他打断我。「午饭前那份可怜的比分我看了。咱们磕磕绊绊，一共拿了多少？」

===Original===
“We’re scraping them still.”

“No! How many?”

“Over two hundred for seven wickets.”

===Chinese===
「这会儿还在磕绊呢。」

「不会吧！多少了？」

「两百挂零，失了七个三柱门。」

===Original===
“Who made the stand?”

“Raffles, for one. He was 62 not out at close of play!”

===Chinese===
「谁稳住的阵脚？」

「拉夫尔斯算一个。当日收局，他六十二分不出局！」

===Original===
And the note of admiration rang in my voice, though I tried in my self-consciousness to keep it out. But young Medlicott’s enthusiasm proved an ample cloak for mine; it was he who might have been the personal friend of Raffles; and in his delight he chuckled till he puffed and blew again.

“Good old Raffles!” he panted in every pause. “After being chosen last, and as a bowler-man! That’s the cricketer for me, sir; by Jove, we must have another drink in his honor! Funny thing, asthma; your liquor affects your head no more than it does a man with a snakebite; but it eases everything else, and sees you through. Doctors will tell you so, but you’ve got to ask ’em first; they’re no good for asthma! I’ve only known one who could stop an attack, and he knocked me sideways with nitrite of amyl. Funny complaint in other ways; raises your spirits, if anything. You can’t look beyond the next breath. Nothing else worries you. Well, well, here’s luck to A. J. Raffles, and may he get his century in the morning!”

===Chinese===
惊叹的调子在我嗓音里铮铮作响，尽管我自觉得很，一心想压住它。不过小梅德利科特的那股热乎劲儿，正好给我的做了件宽绰的斗篷；倒像他本人才是拉夫尔斯的至交；他一乐，直笑得又呼哧带喘起来。

「好个老拉夫尔斯！」他每喘一口，就在间歇里喊一声。「压轴入选，还是以投手上阵！这才是我心目中的板球家，先生；天哪，咱们非得再干一杯敬他不可！哮喘这东西，玄得很；酒上你的头，不比上了蛇毒的人喝酒更上头；可它把旁的处处都松泛了，保你熬得过去。大夫们也会这么告诉你，可你得先去问他们；治哮喘他们一无所长！我就认得一位治得住发作的，一支亚硝酸戊酯（nitrite of amyl）把我治得服服帖帖。这病别处也玄；要说么，倒提人的精神。你顾不上想下一口气以外的事。别的什么也搅扰不着你。好了好了，祝 A. J. 拉夫尔斯走运，愿他明儿早上拿他的一百分！」

===Original===
And he struggled to his feet for the toast; but I drank it sitting down. I felt unreasonably wroth with Raffles, for coming into the conversation as he had done﻿—for taking centuries in Test Matches as he was doing, without bothering his head about me. A failure would have been in better taste; it would have shown at least some imagination, some anxiety on one’s account I did not reflect that even Raffles could scarcely be expected to picture me in my cups with the son of the house that I had come to rob; chatting with him, ministering to him; admiring his cheery courage, and honestly attempting to lighten his load! Truly it was an infernal position: how could I rob him or his after this? And yet I had thrust myself into it; and Raffles would never, never understand!

===Chinese===
他挣起身来祝酒；这杯酒我却是坐着喝的。我对拉夫尔斯无端地动了肝火，气他就这样闯进我们的谈话——气他此刻正在对抗赛里一场一场地拿百分，心思里全没有我。他若失手一场，倒还更有分寸；那至少还显得出几分想象，几分替人悬的心。我没有细想：即便拉夫尔斯，也断乎想不到我此刻正同我要行劫的那户人家的儿子杯盏相酬；同他闲谈，伺候他；钦佩他的达观胆气，还真心实意想替他分忧！这实在是个要命的位置：这之后教我怎么偷他、偷他家里的人？可这位置是我自己一头扎进去的；而拉夫尔斯永远、永远不会懂得！

===Original===
Even that was not the worst. I was not quite sure that young Medlicott was sure of me. I had feared this from the beginning, and now (over the second glass that could not possibly affect a man in his condition) he practically admitted as much to me. Asthma was such a funny thing (he insisted) that it would not worry him a bit to discover that I had come to take the presents instead of to take care of them! I showed a sufficiently faint appreciation of the jest. And it was presently punished as it deserved, by the most violent paroxysm that had seized the sufferer yet: the fight for breath became faster and more furious, and the former weapons of no more avail. I prepared a cigarette, but the poor brute was too breathless to inhale. I poured out yet more whiskey, but he put it from him with a gesture.

“Amyl﻿—get me amyl!” he gasped. “The tin on the table by my bed.”

===Chinese===
这还不算最糟。我拿不准的是，小梅德利科特是不是拿得准我。这一点我从一起头就存着怕；而此刻（第二杯酒下肚——这点酒，按理连他那样状态的人都动不了分毫），他几乎是向我招认了。哮喘就是这么桩妙事（他一口咬定），哪怕查明我来是取礼物的、不是护礼物的，他也半点不往心上去！我对这句玩笑，报以恰到好处的一笑，笑得足够淡。它随即得了应得的报应：病人起了一场比先前哪一场都凶的抽搐：夺气的搏斗愈来愈急、愈来愈狠，先前那些家伙什一概不管用了。我备好一支烟，可怜人却气都接不上，吸不进去了。我再斟威士忌，他抬手就推开了。

「亚硝酸戊酯——给我拿亚硝酸戊酯！」他喘着说。「我床头桌上那只小铁罐。」

===Original===
I rushed into his room, and returned with a little tin of tiny cylinders done up like miniature crackers in scraps of calico; the spent youth broke one in his handkerchief, in which he immediately buried his face. I watched him closely as a subtle odor reached my nostrils; and it was like the miracle of oil upon the billows. His shoulders rested from long travail; the stertorous gasping died away to a quick but natural respiration; and in the sudden cessation of the cruel contest, an uncanny stillness fell upon the scene. Meanwhile the hidden face had flushed to the ears, and, when at length it was raised to mine, its crimson calm was as incongruous as an optical illusion.

“It takes the blood from the heart,” he murmured, “and clears the whole show for the moment. If it only lasted! But you can’t take two without a doctor; one’s quite enough to make you smell the brimstone﻿ ﻿… I say, what’s up? You’re listening to something! If it’s the policeman we’ll have a word with him.”

===Chinese===
我冲进他的卧室，取回来一小铁罐细管，一支支像袖珍爆竹似的，用零头卡其布裹着；这耗尽了的青年折断一支，包进手帕，随即把整张脸埋了进去。一股奇异的气味钻上我的鼻端，我目不转睛地盯着他；那景象，好比平息怒涛的圣油显灵。他的双肩卸下了久拖的苦役；那嘶响的喘鸣渐次消歇，化作急促却自然的呼吸；这场残酷较量戛然而止，一片异样的寂静罩上全场。与此同时，那张埋着不露的脸直红到耳根，待它终于抬起来对着我的脸，那红得安详的容色，竟像一桩视错觉般的不相称。

「它把血从心上引开，」他喃喃道，「一时间满眼都清亮了。只可惜药力不长久！可没有医生跟着，第二支是用不得的；一支就尽够教你闻见硫磺味了……我说，怎么啦？你在听什么动响！要真是那警察，咱们倒得同他说句话。」

===Original===
It was not the policeman; it was no outdoor sound that I had caught in the sudden cessation of the bout for breath. It was a noise, a footstep, in the room below us. I went to the window and leaned out: right underneath, in the conservatory, was the faintest glimmer of a light in the adjoining room.

“One of the rooms where the presents are!” whispered Medlicott at my elbow. And as we withdrew together, I looked him in the face as I had not done all night.

===Chinese===
那不是警察；喘息骤停的当口我捕到的，不是任何户外的声息。那是一声响动，一个脚步声，就在我们楼下那间屋里。我走到窗前，探身出去：正下方，花房里，紧邻的那间房中透出一线最微弱的灯光。

「那是放礼物的房间之一！」梅德利科特在我肘边悄声道。我们一齐退开时，我直视起他的脸来——这一整夜，我还从不曾这样看过他。

===Original===
I looked him in the face like an honest man, for a miracle was to make me one once more. My knot was cut﻿—my course inevitable. Mine, after all, to prevent the very thing that I had come to do! My gorge had long since risen at the deed; the unforeseen circumstances had rendered it impossible from the first; but now I could afford to recognize the impossibility, and to think of Raffles and the asthmatic alike without a qualm. I could play the game by them both, for it was one and the same game. I could preserve thieves’ honor, and yet regain some shred of that which I had forfeited as a man!

===Chinese===
我像个诚实人那样直视他的脸，因为一场奇迹，就要让我重新做回一个诚实人。我的死结解开了——我的去路已无可回避。我此来要做的事，到头来竟归我来阻挡！这桩勾当，我早已从心底作呕；那始料未及的种种变故，本就教它从头起便行不通；可如今，我尽可以坦然认下这份行不通，想到拉夫尔斯、想到这气喘病人，两下都不必再揪心了。他们两个，我都守得住游戏的规矩，因为本就是同一场游戏。窃贼的信义，我保得住；为人所失的那点信义，我还能捞回一丝！

===Original===
So I thought as we stood face to face, our ears straining for the least movement below, our eyes locked in a common anxiety. Another muffled footfall﻿—felt rather than heard﻿—and we exchanged grim nods of simultaneous excitement. But by this time Medlicott was as helpless as he had been before; the flush had faded from his face, and his breathing alone would have spoiled everything. In dumb show I had to order him to stay where he was, to leave my man to me. And then it was that in a gusty whisper, with the same shrewd look that had disconcerted me more than once during our vigil, young Medlicott froze and fired my blood by turns.

“I’ve been unjust to you,” he said, with his right hand in his dressing-gown pocket. “I thought for a bit﻿—never mind what I thought﻿—I soon saw I was wrong. But﻿—I’ve had this thing in my pocket all the time!”

===Chinese===
我们面对面站着，侧耳捕捉楼下最轻的一丝动静，四目交锁，同怀忧急——我心里正是这样想着。又是一声闷住的脚步——与其说听见，不如说觉出——我们彼此点了点头，交换了一个同样绷紧的冷脸。可到了这时，梅德利科特又同先前一般使不上劲了；血色早从他脸上退尽，单是他一个人的呼吸，就足以坏事。我只好打着手势，命令他原地莫动，把那个人留给我。也正是在这时，小梅德利科特用一阵漏风的耳语，配上守夜当中不止一回搅得我心神不定的那种精明眼神，教我的血一阵冷、一阵热。

「我方才错怪了你，」他说，右手插在晨衣口袋里。「有一阵我曾想——想过什么，不必提了——很快我就看明白，是我错。可是——这东西，我一直就揣在口袋里！」

===Original===
And he would have thrust his revolver upon me as a peace-offering, but I would not even take his hand, as I tapped the life-preserver in my pocket, and crept out to earn his honest grip or to fall in the attempt. On the landing I drew Raffles’s little weapon, slipped my right wrist through the leathern loop, and held it in readiness over my right shoulder. Then, downstairs I stole, as Raffles himself had taught me, close to the wall, where the planks are nailed. Nor had I made a sound, to my knowledge; for a door was open, and a light was burning, and the light did not flicker as I approached the door. I clenched my teeth and pushed it open; and here was the veriest villain waiting for me, his little lantern held aloft.

===Chinese===
他原要把他的左轮塞给我，权当赔罪的礼，我却连他的手也不肯握，只敲了敲自己口袋里的护身短棒，蹑足出门，去挣他诚实的一握，不然就栽在半路。到了楼梯平台，我抽出拉夫尔斯那件小小的兵器，右手腕套进皮环，把它擎过右肩，戒备着。随后我往楼下潜行，用的是拉夫尔斯亲授的功夫：贴墙根走，踩在钉死的板条上。据我所知，我没有弄出一点声息；因为有一扇门开着，一盏灯点着，而我逼近门口时，那灯火纹丝不曾摇动。我咬紧牙关，推门而入；等在里头的，是一个十足的恶棍，小灯高高擎在手上。

===Original===
“You blackguard!” I cried, and with a single thwack I felled the ruffian to the floor.

There was no question of a foul blow. He had been just as ready to pounce on me; it was simply my luck to have got the first blow home. Yet a fellow-feeling touched me with remorse, as I stood over the senseless body, sprawling prone, and perceived that I had struck an unarmed man. The lantern only had fallen from his hands; it lay on one side, smoking horribly; and a something in the reek caused me to set it up in haste and turn the body over with both hands.

===Chinese===
「你这恶棍！」我大喊一声，一短棒把那歹徒抽翻在地。

这谈不上什么下手不光明。他本来也正要扑我；不过是我运气，先一招得了手。然而一种同病相怜之情，触得我悔意顿生——我站在那不省人事的身躯旁边，见它扑地摊作一团，这才看清，我打的是个手无寸铁的人。只有灯笼从他手里跌落；横在一边，冒着骇人的烟；烟气里有种什么，催得我慌忙把它扶起，又用双手把那身子翻转过来。

===Original===
Shall I ever forget the incredulous horror of that moment?

It was Raffles himself!

How it was possible, I did not pause to ask myself; if one man on earth could annihilate space and time, it was the man lying senseless at my feet; and that was Raffles, without an instant’s doubt. He was in villainous guise, which I knew of old, now that I knew the unhappy wearer. His face was grimy, and dexterously plastered with a growth of reddish hair; his clothes were those in which he had followed cabs from the London termini; his boots were muffled in thick socks; and I had laid him low with a bloody scalp that filled my cup of horror. I groaned aloud as I knelt over him and felt his heart. And I was answered by a bronchial whistle from the door.

===Chinese===
我此生可忘得了那一刻难以置信的恐怖么？

那是拉夫尔斯本人！

这怎么可能，我无暇自问；普天之下若有一人能把空间与时间一并抹去，那便是躺在我脚边不省人事的这一个；而这一个，分毫不差，正是拉夫尔斯。他一身恶棍的行头，我本就认得——如今认出了这倒霉的穿衣人，更认得亲切。他满脸油垢，一副泛红的须发巧手黏贴而成；那身衣裳，正是他从前在伦敦各大车站跟踪马车时穿的；靴子上裹着厚袜，消了声响；而我这一棒打得他头皮绽开，血流满面，给我这一杯恐怖直斟到齐口。我跪在他身旁探他的心跳，不由得放声呻吟起来。回答我的，是门口一声气管里吹出来的哨音。

===Original===
“Jolly well done!” cheered my asthmatical friend. “I heard the whole thing﻿—only hope my mother didn’t. We must keep it from her if we can.”

I could have cursed the creature’s mother from my full heart; yet even with my hand on that of Raffles, as I felt his feeble pulse, I told myself that this served him right. Even had I brained him, the fault had been his, not mine. And it was a characteristic, an inveterate fault, that galled me for all my anguish: to trust and yet distrust me to the end, to race through England in the night, to spy upon me at his work﻿—to do it himself after all!

===Chinese===
「打得漂亮！」我那位气喘的朋友喝彩。「我全听见了——但愿我母亲没听见。能瞒住她，咱们总得瞒。」

那家伙的母亲，我是打心底里诅咒得出声的；可即便我的手按着拉夫尔斯的手、摸着他细弱的脉息，我仍告诉自己：这是他活该。哪怕我一棒砸开了他的瓢，错也在他，不在我。何况这还是他一桩根深蒂固的老毛病，痛中偏又添堵：到了这步田地，对我还是又信又不信，连夜横穿英格兰，到我当值时来窥伺我——到头来，索性自己动手！

===Original===
“Is he dead?” wheezed the asthmatic coolly.

“Not he,” I answered, with an indignation that I dared not show.

“You must have hit him pretty hard,” pursued young Medlicott, “but I suppose it was a case of getting first knock. And a good job you got it, if this was his,” he added, picking up the murderous little life-preserver which poor Raffles had provided for his own destruction.

===Chinese===
「他死了么？」气喘病人冷冷地问。

「他才不呢，」我答道，一腔愠怒不敢形于色。

「你下手想必够重的，」小梅德利科特追问，「不过我料想，这一场总得有人先动手。好在你抢了先，这玩意儿若是他的话，」他补上一句，一面拾起那件凶横的小小护身短棒——可怜的拉夫尔斯亲手备下它，倒险些备成了自己送命的由头。

===Original===
“Look here,” I answered, sitting back on my heels. “He isn’t dead, Mr. Medlicott, and I don’t know how long he’ll be as much as stunned. He’s a powerful brute, and you’re not fit to lend a hand. But that policeman of yours can’t be far away. Do you think you could struggle out and look for him?”

“I suppose I am a bit better than I was,” he replied doubtfully. “The excitement seems to have done me good. If you like to leave me on guard with my revolver, I’ll undertake that he doesn’t escape me.”

I shook my head with an impatient smile.

===Chinese===
「你听着，」我答道，一屁股坐回脚跟上。「他没死，梅德利科特先生，可我说不好，他这昏沉还得昏多久。这家伙力气大得像头牲口，你又搭不上手。不过你那位警察走不远。你看，你能不能挣扎着出去寻他一寻？」

「我想我是比先前好些了，」他将信将疑地答。「这场惊动，倒似乎于我有益。你若肯留下我持枪看守，我保他逃不出我的手心。」

我摇了摇头，带一点不耐烦的笑。

===Original===
“I should never hear the last of it,” said I. “No, in that case all I can do is to handcuff the fellow and wait till morning if he won’t go quietly; and he’ll be a fool if he does, while there’s a fighting chance.”

Young Medlicott glanced upstairs from his post on the threshold. I refrained from watching him too keenly, but I knew what was in his mind.

“I’ll go,” he said hurriedly. “I’ll go as I am, before my mother is disturbed and frightened out of her life. I owe you something, too, not only for what you’ve done for me, but for what I was fool enough to think about you at the first blush. It’s entirely through you that I feel as fit as I do for the moment. So I’ll take your tip, and go just as I am, before my poor old pipes strike up another tune.”

===Chinese===
「那这桩事我这辈子就别想听到底了，」我说。「不；到那一步，我唯一能做的，就是把这家伙铐起来，他若不肯乖乖地走，就干等到天亮；只要还有一线动手的机会，乖乖走才真是傻瓜。」

小梅德利科特在他把守的门槛上，朝楼上望了一眼。我克制着，不把他盯得太紧，可他心里打的什么谱，我肚里明白。

「我去，」他急忙说。「我这就这么一身去，趁我母亲还没被吵醒、吓得魂不附体。我也欠你一桩情——不为你替我做下的，单为我起头糊涂、竟那样想过你。我这会儿撑得住，全是托你的福。所以就依你的话，我这就去，趁我这把可怜的老风箱还没奏起下一支曲子。」

===Original===
I scarcely looked up until the good fellow had turned his back upon the final tableau of watchful officer and prostrate prisoner and gone out wheezing into the night. But I was at the door to hear the last of him down the path and round the corner of the house. And when I rushed back into the room, there was Raffles sitting cross-legged on the floor, and slowly shaking his broken head as he stanched the blood.

===Chinese===
这位好人背过身去，丢下「警觉的官差」与「趴伏的囚犯」这最后一幕布景，喘着走进夜色里去了，在那之前我几乎不曾抬头。可他下小径、绕过屋角的最后一点声息，我一直守在门口听尽。等我奔回屋里，拉夫尔斯已经盘腿坐在地板上，一边按着伤口止血，一边缓缓摇着他那颗打破了的头。

===Original===
“Et tu, Bunny!” he groaned. “Mine own familiar friend!”

“Then you weren’t even stunned!” I exclaimed. “Thank God for that!”

===Chinese===
「你也下得去手，邦尼！」他呻吟道。「我这素日知心的朋友！」

「那敢情你压根儿就没昏！」我失声道。「谢天谢地！」

===Original===
“Of course I was stunned,” he murmured, “and no thanks to you that I wasn’t brained. Not to know me in the kit you’ve seen scores of times! You never looked at me, Bunny; you didn’t give me time to open my mouth. I was going to let you run me in so prettily! We’d have walked off arm-in-arm; now it’s as tight a place as ever we were in, though you did get rid of old blowpipes rather nicely. But we shall have the devil’s own run for our money!”

===Chinese===
「我当然昏了，」他喃喃道，「脑袋没开花，可不是你的功劳。那身行头你见过几十回，竟认不出我来！你压根儿没看我一眼，邦尼；你没给我张嘴的工夫。我本来还要让你把我漂漂亮亮地捉了去！咱们本可以手挽手扬长而去；如今这地方，同咱们从前陷过的任何地方一样险——不过你把老风箱打发开的那一手，倒真够漂亮。可这一回，咱们是拼了命才赚得着了！」

===Original===
Raffles had picked himself up between his mutterings, and I had followed him to the door into the garden, where he stood busy with the key in the dark, having blown out his lantern and handed it to me. But though I followed Raffles, as my nature must, I was far too embittered to answer him again. And so it was for some minutes that might furnish forth a thrilling page, but not a novel one to those who know their Raffles and put up with me. Suffice it that we left a locked door behind us, and the key on the garden wall, which was the first of half a dozen that we scaled before dropping into a lane that led to a footbridge higher up the backwater. And when we paused upon the footbridge, the houses along the bank were still in peace and darkness.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯一面咕哝，一面已经爬起身来；我跟着他走到通花园的门口，他吹熄灯笼递到我手里，正摸着黑忙那把钥匙。我虽天性使然，跟着拉夫尔斯走，心里的怨愤却深得答不上他的话。就这样过了好几分钟——那几分钟能敷演出一页惊险文字，只是对识得拉夫尔斯其人、又肯容忍我的读者，并非什么新篇章。只需交代一句：我们身后留下一道上锁的门，钥匙搁在花园墙上——那堵墙，是随后翻越的六堵里的头一堵——末了我们落进一条小巷，巷子通到回水湾上游的一座便桥。我们在便桥上驻足时，沿岸的人家仍旧一派安睡的漆黑。

===Original===
Knowing *my* Raffles as I did, I was not surprised when he dived under one end of this bridge, and came up with his Inverness cape and opera hat, which he had hidden there on his way to the house. The thick socks were peeled from his patent-leathers, the ragged trousers stripped from an evening pair, bloodstains and Newgate fringe removed at the water’s edge, and the whole sepulchre whited in less time than the thing takes to tell. Nor was that enough for Raffles, but he must alter me as well, by wearing my overcoat under his cape, and putting his Zingari scarf about my neck.

===Chinese===
我深知我的拉夫尔斯，所以见他一头钻进这桥一头的桥洞，再冒出来时手里多了他的因弗内斯斗篷（Inverness cape）和歌剧礼帽，我丝毫不奇——那是他上宅子去的路上预先藏下的。厚袜从漆皮鞋上剥下，破烂长裤剥去，露出底下一条晚装裤，血渍和那副纽盖特式刘海（Newgate fringe），都在水边洗净抹去，整座坟墓的粉刷功夫，比讲述一遍还快便告完成。而这还不够，拉夫尔斯偏要把我一并改装：把我那件大衣穿在他斗篷底下，再把他的辛加里围巾（Zingari）围上我的脖子。

===Original===
“And now,” said he, “you may be glad to hear there’s a 3:12 from Surbiton, which we could catch on all fours. If you like we’ll go separately, but I don’t think there’s the slightest danger now, and I begin to wonder what’s happening to old blowpipes.”

===Chinese===
「如今，」他说，「有件消息你听了准高兴：瑟比顿（Surbiton）有一班 3:12 的车，咱们爬着也赶得上。你若愿意，咱们分头走；不过我看这会儿没有一丝一毫的危险了，我倒开始好奇，老风箱他怎么样了。」

===Original===
So, indeed, did I, and with no small concern, until I read of his adventures (and our own) in the newspapers. It seemed that he had made a gallant spurt into the road, and there paid the penalty of his rashness by a sudden incapacity to move another inch. It had eventually taken him twenty minutes to creep back to locked doors, and another ten to ring up the inmates. His description of my personal appearance, as reported in the papers, is the only thing that reconciles me to the thought of his sufferings during that half-hour.

But at the time I had other thoughts, and they lay too deep for idle words, for to me also it was a bitter hour. I had not only failed in my self-sought task; I had nearly killed my comrade into the bargain. I had meant well by friend and foe in turn, and I had ended in doing execrably by both. It was not all my fault, but I knew how much my weakness had contributed to the sum. And I must walk with the man whose fault it was, who had travelled two hundred miles to obtain this last proof of my weakness, to bring it home to me, and to make our intimacy intolerable from that hour. I must walk with him to Surbiton, but I need not talk; all through Thames Ditton I had ignored his sallies; nor yet when he ran his arm through mine, on the river front, when we were nearly there, would I break the seal my pride had set upon my lips.

===Chinese===
我确实也好奇，且挂心得不轻，直到后来在报纸上读到他的历险（连同我们的一桩）。看来他好一场英雄气概的冲刺，冲上了大路，却就在那里为孟浪付出了代价——突然之间，一寸也挪不动了。他足足花了二十分钟才爬回那几扇上锁的门前，又花十分钟才把屋里的人唤起。他对我的相貌的一番描述，据报上所载，是那半个钟头他的苦楚留给我的唯一慰藉。

可在当时，我另有一番心事，藏得太深，不是闲话打得到的，因为那一时，于我也是苦时。我不但在自己寻来的差事上砸了锅；还几乎搭上了搭挡的性命，算作添头。我对朋友、对仇家，先后都存着好心，到头来对两边都办下了最糟的事。这不能全怪我，可我的软弱在这笔账里占了几成，我心里有数。我还得同那个一手造成这局面的人并肩走——他连夜赶了两百英里路，就为给我的软弱讨来这最后一纸凭证，当面交到我手上，教我们的交情从那一刻起再也难以忍受。我必须同他一路走到瑟比顿，却不必同他说话；穿过泰晤士迪顿（Thames Ditton）一路，他的连番打趣我概不理会；及至到了河畔、望得见车站了，他伸过胳膊来挽我，我仍不肯撕开我的自尊贴在我唇上的封条。

===Original===
“Come, Bunny,” he said at last, “I have been the one to suffer most, when all’s said and done, and I’ll be the first to say that I deserved it. You’ve broken my head; my hair’s all glued up in my gore; and what yarn I’m to put up at Manchester, or how I shall take the field at all, I really don’t know. Yet I don’t blame you, Bunny, and I do blame myself. Isn’t it rather hard luck if I am to go unforgiven into the bargain? I admit that I made a mistake; but, my dear fellow, I made it entirely for your sake.”

“For my sake!” I echoed bitterly.

Raffles was more generous; he ignored my tone.

===Chinese===
「好了，邦尼，」他终于开口，「归根到底，吃苦最多的是我一个，头一个说这话的也是我：这是我活该。你打破了我的头；我的头发全叫血黏作一团；到了曼彻斯特（Manchester），我拿什么话去搪塞，或者怎么个上场法，我实在说不上。可我并不怪你，邦尼，我只怪我自己。倘若到头来连个饶恕也落不着，这运气岂不是苦得过了头？我承认我是失了一着；可是，亲爱的伙计，我失这一着，完完全全是为了你。」

「为了我！」我苦涩地应道。

拉夫尔斯倒宽宏些；他不理会我的语气。

===Original===
“I was miserable about you﻿—frankly﻿—miserable!” he went on. “I couldn’t get it out of my head that somehow you would be laid by the heels. It was not your pluck that I distrusted, my dear fellow, but it was your very pluck that made me tremble for you. I couldn’t get you out of my head. I went in when runs were wanted, but I give you my word that I was more anxious about you; and no doubt that’s why I helped to put on some runs. Didn’t you see it in the paper, Bunny? It’s the innings of my life, so far.”

===Chinese===
「我为你心里煎熬——坦白说——煎熬！」他接着讲。「我心里怎么也撵不走一个念头：不管怎么着，你总要栽进网里。我信不过的，亲爱的伙计，不是你的胆量；恰恰是你那股胆量，叫我为你直打颤。你在我脑子里就是撵不走。该拿分的时候我上了场，可我对你说句实话，我更挂心的是你；不用说，正为这个，我才顺手多添了几分。报你没看见么，邦尼？这是我这辈子最好的一局。」

===Original===
“Yes,” I said, “I saw that you were in at close of play. But I don’t believe it was you﻿—I believe you have a double who plays your cricket for you!”

And at the moment that seemed less incredible than the fact.

===Chinese===
「看见了，」我说，「我看见你收局时还没出局。可我不信那是你——我相信你有个替身，替你打你的板球！」

而在那一刻，这话竟比事实还少了几分不可思议。

===Original===
“I’m afraid you didn’t read your paper very carefully,” said Raffles, with the first trace of pique in his tone. “It was rain that closed play before five o’clock. I hear it was a sultry day in town, but at Manchester we got the storm, and the ground was under water in ten minutes. I never saw such a thing in my life. There was absolutely not the ghost of a chance of another ball being bowled. But I had changed before I thought of doing what I did. It was only when I was on my way back to the hotel, by myself, because I couldn’t talk to a soul for thinking of you, that on the spur of the moment I made the man take me to the station instead, and was under way in the restaurant car before I had time to think twice about it. I am not sure that of all the mad deeds I have ever done, this was not the maddest of the lot!”

===Chinese===
「恐怕你报读得不仔细，」拉夫尔斯说，语气里头一回带出一丝嗔怪。「是场雨，五点不到就把比赛收了局。听说城里那天闷热，可在曼彻斯特，暴雨全叫我们赶上了，不出十分钟，场地便泡在了水里。我生平没见过这样的场面。再投一球，连鬼影一样的指望都没有。不过，早在想起我后来干的事之前，我就换好了衣裳。只是往旅馆走回去的路上——我一个人走，因为满脑子是你，跟谁也说不成话——才临时起意，叫车夫改道送我去车站；等我想清楚第二遍，人已经坐在餐车里上了路。我要说，在我干过的所有疯事里，这一件，只怕疯得最厉害！」

===Original===
“It was the finest,” I said in a low voice; for now I marvelled more at the impulse which had prompted his feat, and at the circumstances surrounding it, than even at the feat itself.

“Heaven knows,” he went on, “what they are saying and doing in Manchester! But what can they say? What business is it of theirs? I was there when play stopped, and I shall be there when it starts again. We shall be at Waterloo just after half-past three, and that’s going to give me an hour at the Albany on my way to Euston, and another hour at Old Trafford before play begins. What’s the matter with that? I don’t suppose I shall notch any more, but all the better if I don’t; if we have a hot sun after the storm, the sooner they get in the better; and may I have a bowl at them while the ground bites!”

===Chinese===
「也是干得最漂亮的一件，」我低声说；因为此刻，比起事迹本身，那促成这事迹的一股冲动、连同环绕着它的种种情形，更教我惊叹。

「天知道他们此刻在曼彻斯特说些什么、忙些什么！」他接着说。「可他们又能说什么？关他们什么事？停赛时我在场，再开赛时我还会在场。咱们三点半刚过便到滑铁卢（Waterloo），我来得及在去尤斯顿之前，先回奥尔巴尼公寓（Albany）一个钟头，再赶老特拉福德，开赛前还有一个钟头。这有什么不妥？我不指望还能添分了，添不了倒更好；暴雨过后若来毒日头，他们越早上场击球越好；再让我趁场地咬球的时候，投他们几球！」

===Original===
“I’ll come up with you,” I said, “and see you at it.”

“My dear fellow,” replied Raffles, “that was my whole feeling about you. I wanted to ‘see you at it’﻿—that was absolutely all. I wanted to be near enough to lend a hand if you got tied up, as the best of us will at times. I knew the ground better than you, and I simply couldn’t keep away from it. But I didn’t mean you to know that I was there; if everything had gone as I hoped it might, I should have sneaked back to town without ever letting you know I had been up. You should never have dreamt that I had been at your elbow; you would have believed in yourself, and in my belief in you, and the rest would have been silence till the grave. So I dodged you at Waterloo, and I tried not to let you know that I was following you from Esher station. But you suspected somebody was; you stopped to listen more than once; after the second time I dropped behind, but gained on you by taking the shortcut by Imber Court and over the footbridge where I left my coat and hat. I was actually in the garden before you were. I saw you smoke your Sullivan, and I was rather proud of you for it, though you must never do that sort of thing again. I heard almost every word between you and the poor devil upstairs. And up to a certain point, Bunny, I really thought you played the scene to perfection.”

===Chinese===
「我随你北上，」我说，「去看你露一手。」

「亲爱的伙计，」拉夫尔斯答道，「我对你的整套心事，也正在此。我想『看你露一手』——全然只此一桩。我只想离得近一些，万一你叫什么事缠住了，好搭上一把手——咱们再好的人，也难免有个缠住的时候。那片场地我比你熟，我实在一步也挪不开。可我并不要你知道我在那儿；倘若事事都照我指望的那么顺，我原打算悄悄溜回城里，永远不叫你知道我来过。你做梦也想不到我就在你肘边；你会信你自己，也会信我对你的信心，其余的一切，直到进坟，都是沉默。所以我在滑铁卢躲开了你；从伊舍站一路，我也尽量不叫你察觉我在后面跟着。可你疑心有人跟着；你不止一回停步细听；你第二回停步之后，我便坠到后头，却抄因伯苑（Imber Court）的近道赶回了前头，过便桥时把大衣和帽子留在那里。进花园的时候，我竟比你还早。我看着你抽你的沙利文，我还替你得意呢——虽然这种事，你往后万万来不得第二回。你同楼上那位可怜人之间的对答，我几乎字字入耳。而且直到某一个节骨眼为止，邦尼，我真觉得，那一场戏你演得十全十美。」

===Original===
The station lights were twinkling ahead of us in the fading velvet of the summer’s night. I let them increase and multiply before I spoke.

“And where,” I asked, “did you think I first went wrong?”

“In going indoors at all,” said Raffles. “If I had done that, I should have done exactly what you did from that point on. You couldn’t help yourself, with that poor brute in that state. And I admired you immensely, Bunny, if that’s any comfort to you now.”

===Chinese===
车站的灯火，在前方夏夜渐褪的天鹅绒里闪烁。我由着它们越闪越近、越闪越密，这才开口。

「那么，」我问，「依你看，我打头一桩错在哪儿？」

「错在压根儿进了屋，」拉夫尔斯说。「换了是我进去了，从那一步起，你随后做的每一件事，我都会原样做一遍。那可怜人落在那副田地，你实在由不得自己。我是打心底佩服你，邦尼——这话若能在此时此地安慰你一二。」

===Original===
Comfort! It was wine in every vein, for I knew that Raffles meant what he said, and with his eyes I soon saw myself in braver colors. I ceased to blush for the vacillations of the night, since he condoned them. I could even see that I had behaved with a measure of decency, in a truly trying situation, now that Raffles seemed to think so. He had changed my whole view of his proceedings and my own, in every incident of the night but one. There was one thing, however, which he might forgive me, but which I felt that I could forgive neither Raffles nor myself. And that was the contused scalp wound over which I shuddered in the train.

“And to think that I did that,” I groaned, “and that you laid yourself open to it, and that we have neither of us got another thing to show for our night’s work! That poor chap said it was as bad a night as he had ever had in his life; but I call it the very worst that you and I ever had in ours.”

===Chinese===
安慰！这是流遍每一条血管的酒，因为我知道拉夫尔斯句句由衷；透过他的眼睛，我很快看见了一个更见光彩的自己。这一夜里的摇摆反复，我不再为之脸红，既然他一概宽宥了。拉夫尔斯既这样看，我甚至看得出来，在那一个真正难堪的处境里，我的行事原也有几分分寸。那一夜他的行事、我的行事，桩桩件件，他都给我换了一副眼光，只除一件。只除那一件事——他或者肯饶我，我却觉得，拉夫尔斯同我自己，我一个也饶不了。那就是那一处头皮的挫伤，火车上我一念及便浑身战栗。

「想想吧，那一棒竟是我下的手，」我呻吟道，「你偏偏还把自己送到棒下；咱们俩忙了一夜，除它之外，竟谁也没挣出第二件东西好交代！那可怜的小伙子说，那是他此生最难熬的一夜；可我说，那是你我这辈子最最糟的一夜。」

===Original===
Raffles was smiling under the double lamps of the first-class compartment that we had to ourselves.

“I wouldn’t say that, Bunny. We have done worse.”

“Do you mean to tell me that you did anything at all?”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯在头等车厢成对的车灯底下微笑，这节车厢只我们两个。

「我不这么说，邦尼。咱们干过更糟的。」

「你是想告诉我，你到底还是干了些什么？」

===Original===
“My dear Bunny,” replied Raffles, “you should remember how long I had been maturing felonious little plan, what a blow it was to me to have to turn it over to you, and how far I had travelled to see that you did it and yourself as well as might be. You know what I did see, and how well I understood. I tell you again that I should have done the same thing myself, in your place. But I was not in your place, Bunny. My hands were not tied like yours. Unfortunately, most of the jewels have gone on the honeymoon with the happy pair; but these emerald links are all right, and I don’t know what the bride was doing to leave this diamond comb behind. Here, too, is the old silver skewer I’ve been wanting for years﻿—they make the most charming paper-knives in the world﻿—and this gold cigarette-case will just do for your smaller Sullivans.”

Nor were these the only pretty things that Raffles set out in twinkling array upon the opposite cushions. But I do not pretend that this was one of our heavy hauls, or deny that its chief interest still resides in the score of the Second Test Match of that Australian tour.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的邦尼，」拉夫尔斯答道，「你该记得，那桩小小的犯法计划，我孕育了有多久；不得不把它转手交给你，于我是怎样一记闷棍；我又赶了多远的路，就为看着你把它——也把你这个人——办得周全。我看见了什么，又明白到什么田地，如今你都知道了。我再说一遍：换了我坐在你的位子上，我也会一步不差地这么做。可我并不在你的位子上，邦尼。我的双手，不像你那样叫人捆着。可惜的是，大半的珠宝都随那对幸福人儿度蜜月去了；可这几对翡翠袖扣完好无损；这位新娘又是什么缘故，把这支钻石发梳落在了后头。这儿还有那支老银簪，我惦记了好些年——世上最惹人爱的裁纸刀，非它们莫属——这只金烟盒嘛，正好装你的小号沙利文。」

拉夫尔斯在对面坐垫上亮晶晶摆开一列的漂亮东西，也不止这几件。可我不冒充这一票是我们什么大宗的进项，也不否认：这桩买卖最要紧的兴味，至今仍寄在那次澳大利亚巡回赛（Australian tour）第二场对抗赛的比分里。

## A Trap to Catch a Cracksman / 诱捕撬锁贼的陷阱

===Original===
I was just putting out my light when the telephone rang a furious tocsin in the next room. I flounced out of bed more asleep than awake; in another minute I should have been past ringing up. It was one o’clock in the morning, and I had been dining with Swigger Morrison at his club.

“Hulloa!”

“That you, Bunny?”

===Chinese===
我刚要熄灯，隔壁房里的电话铃便疯了似的敲响警钟。我从床上翻身跳起，睡意多过清醒；再迟一分钟，只怕连打都打不通了。时间是凌晨一点钟，我刚刚在斯威格·莫里森（Swigger Morrison）的俱乐部里同他吃过晚饭。

「喂！」

「是你吗，邦尼？」

===Original===
“Yes﻿—are you Raffles?”

“What’s left of me! Bunny, I want you﻿—quick.”

===Chinese===
「是——你是拉夫尔斯吗？」

「还剩半条命的我！邦尼，我需要你——快来。」

===Original===
And even over the wire his voice was faint with anxiety and apprehension.

“What on earth has happened?”

===Chinese===
即使隔着电话线，他的声音也透着焦虑与惶恐，微弱不堪。

「到底出什么事了？」

===Original===
“Don’t ask! You never know﻿—”

“I’ll come at once. Are you there, Raffles?”

“What’s that?”

“Are you there, man?”

===Chinese===
「别问！这种事你永远说不准——」

「我马上就来。你还在吗，拉夫尔斯？」

「你说什么？」

「你还在听吗，老兄？」

===Original===
“Ye‑e‑es.”

“At the Albany?”

“No, no; at Maguire’s.”

“You never said so. And where’s Maguire?”

===Chinese===
「是———的。」

「在奥尔巴尼公寓？」

「不，不；在马圭尔（Maguire）家里。」

「这话你可从来没说过。马圭尔人又住在哪儿？」

===Original===
“In Half-moon Street.”

“I know that. Is he there now?”

“No﻿—not come in yet﻿—and I’m caught.”

“Caught!”

===Chinese===
「在半月街（Half-moon Street）。」

「这我知道。他这会儿在家吗？」

「不——还没回来——而我被逮住了。」

「被逮住了！」

===Original===
“In that trap he bragged about. It serves me right. I didn’t believe in it. But I’m caught at last﻿ ﻿… caught﻿ ﻿… at last!”

“When he told us he set it every night! Oh, Raffles, what sort of a trap is it? What shall I do? What shall I bring?”

===Chinese===
「就在他吹嘘过的那个陷阱里。我活该。我本来是不信它的。可我到底还是被逮住了……被逮住了……到底还是！」

「他还告诉过我们，他每天夜里都上着它呢！噢，拉夫尔斯，那到底是个什么样的陷阱？我该怎么办？我该带些什么去？」

===Original===
But his voice had grown fainter and wearier with every answer, and now there was no answer at all. Again and again I asked Raffles if he was there; the only sound to reach me in reply was the low metallic hum of the live wire between his ear and mine. And then, as I sat gazing distractedly at my four safe walls, with the receiver still pressed to my head, there came a single groan, followed by the dull and dreadful crash of a human body falling in a heap.

===Chinese===
可他每答一句，声音就愈发微弱、愈发疲惫，到这会儿已全无回应。我一遍又一遍地问拉夫尔斯还在不在；传回我耳中的唯一声息，是他与我之间那根通电的导线发出的低低金属嗡鸣。随后，正当我坐在那儿，六神无主地瞪着我那四面太平的四壁，听筒还压在头上，忽听得一声呻吟，接着是一具人体软软瘫倒的那一种沉闷而可怕的轰响。

===Original===
In utter panic I rushed back into my bedroom, and flung myself into the crumpled shirt and evening clothes that lay where I had cast them off. But I knew no more what I was doing than what to do next I afterward found that I had taken out a fresh tie, and tied it rather better than usual; but I can remember thinking of nothing but Raffles in some diabolical mantrap, and of a grinning monster stealing in to strike him senseless with one murderous blow. I must have looked in the glass to array myself as I did; but the mind’s eye was the seeing eye, and it was filled with this frightful vision of the notorious pugilist known to fame and infamy as Barney Maguire.

===Chinese===
我惊慌失措地冲回卧室，一头扎进那堆扔在原处的皱衬衫和晚礼服里。可我那时在干什么，同接下来该干什么一样，心里全然没数——事后我才发现，自己竟取出了一条干净领带，打的结还比往常齐整些；但我记得，满脑子只剩一个念头：拉夫尔斯正落在什么恶魔似的捕人机关里，一个龇牙咧嘴的怪物正溜进去，要一记凶残的重拳把他打得不省人事。我必定是对着镜子装扮过自己，不然怎会穿戴成那副样子；可真正在看的是心里的那只眼，而它满满装着的，正是那位以赫赫声名兼昭昭臭名著称的拳师——巴尼·马圭尔（Barney Maguire）——的可怖形象。

===Original===
It was only the week before that Raffles and I had been introduced to him at the Imperial Boxing Club. Heavyweight champion of the United States, the fellow was still drunk with his sanguinary triumphs on that side, and clamoring for fresh conquests on ours. But his reputation had crossed the Atlantic before Maguire himself; the grandiose hotels had closed their doors to him; and he had already taken and sumptuously furnished the house in Half-moon Street which does not re-let to this day. Raffles had made friends with the magnificent brute, while I took timid stock of his diamond studs, his jewelled watch-chain, his eighteen-carat bangle, and his six-inch lower jaw. I had shuddered to see Raffles admiring the gewgaws in his turn, in his own brazen fashion, with that air of the cool connoisseur which had its double meaning for me. I for my part would as lief have looked a tiger in the teeth. And when we finally went home with Maguire to see his other trophies, it seemed to me like entering the tiger’s lair. But an astounding lair it proved, fitted throughout by one eminent firm, and ringing to the rafters with the last word on fantastic furniture.

===Chinese===
就在一个星期之前，拉夫尔斯和我才在帝国拳击俱乐部（Imperial Boxing Club）经人引见与他相识。这位美利坚合众国的重量级拳王，还陶醉在大洋那一边的一连串血腥胜利里，嚷嚷着要上我们这一边来开辟新的征服。可他的名声比他本人先一步横渡了大西洋；那些金碧辉煌的大酒店早已对他闭门不纳；于是他索性住进了半月街上那座宅子，还装修得穷奢极侈——那房子至今也没能再租出去。拉夫尔斯同这头仪表堂堂的畜生交上了朋友，我则怯生生地打量他的钻石饰扣、嵌宝表链、十八开金的镯子，还有他那六英寸长的大下巴。看到拉夫尔斯也轮番鉴赏起这些花哨玩意儿来，用的还是他那种不知脸红的做派，摆出一副冷静行家的气度——那气度在我心里自有双重意味——我不由得直打寒颤。要依我说，倒宁可去瞪着一只老虎的牙口。后来我们终于随马圭尔回家去看他其余的战利品，我感觉就像是跨进虎穴。谁知那却是座叫人瞠目结舌的巢穴，从头到尾由一家鼎鼎大名的商行装配布置，连椽木都嗡嗡回响着奇幻家具的最新名词。

===Original===
The trophies were a still greater surprise. They opened my eyes to the rosier aspect of the noble art, as presently practised on the right side of the Atlantic. Among other offerings, we were permitted to handle the jewelled belt presented to the pugilist by the State of Nevada, a gold brick from the citizens of Sacramento, and a model of himself in solid silver from the Fisticuff Club in New York. I still remember waiting with bated breath for Raffles to ask Maguire if he were not afraid of burglars, and Maguire replying that he had a trap to catch the cleverest cracksman alive, but flatly refusing to tell us what it was. I could not at the moment conceive a more terrible trap than the heavyweight himself behind a curtain. Yet it was easy to see that Raffles had accepted the braggart’s boast as a challenge. Nor did he deny it later when I taxed him with his mad resolve; he merely refused to allow me to implicate myself in its execution. Well, there was a spice of savage satisfaction in the thought that Raffles had been obliged to turn to me in the end. And, but for the dreadful thud which I had heard over the telephone, I might have extracted some genuine comfort from the unerring sagacity with which he had chosen his night.

===Chinese===
那些战利品更叫人惊奇。它们让我大开眼界，见识了这项高尚艺术眼下在大西洋正确的一边盛行时较为光鲜的一面。在诸多献礼之中，我们还获准亲手把玩内华达州（Nevada）颁赠给这位拳师的嵌宝腰带、萨克拉门托（Sacramento）市民奉献的金砖，以及纽约拳斗俱乐部（Fisticuff Club）所赠的一座他本人的纯银塑像。我至今记得，自己屏住呼吸，只等拉夫尔斯开口问马圭尔怕不怕窃贼；而马圭尔回答说，他有一副能逮住在世最聪明的撬锁贼的陷阱，却断然不肯告诉我们那是什么。我在那一刻实在想不出，天底下还有什么陷阱，能比帘幕后面站着这位重量级拳王本人更可怕的。然而不难看出，拉夫尔斯已经把这牛皮大王的牛皮当作挑战，一口应承了下来。事后我就他那个疯狂的决心向他诘问，他也不否认；只是不许我把自己也牵连到这桩勾当的执行里去。唉，一想到拉夫尔斯到头来不得不来找我，我心里自有一丝狠巴巴的快意。而且，若不是电话里传来那声可怕的闷响，我本可以从他挑选时机那份百无一失的老谋深算里，榨出一点货真价实的安慰来。

===Original===
Within the last twenty-four hours Barney Maguire had fought his first great battle on British soil. Obviously, he would no longer be the man that he had been in the strict training before the fight; never, as I gathered, was such a ruffian more off his guard, or less capable of protecting himself and his possessions, than in these first hours of relaxation and inevitable debauchery for which Raffles had waited with characteristic foresight. Nor was the terrible Barney likely to be more abstemious for signal punishment sustained in a far from bloodless victory. Then what could be the meaning of that sickening and most suggestive thud? Could it be the champion himself who had received the coup de grâce in his cups? Raffles was the very man to administer it﻿—but he had not talked like that man through the telephone.

===Chinese===
就在刚过去的二十四小时之内，巴尼·马圭尔在不列颠的土地上打完了他的第一场大战。显而易见，他已不再是赛前严格训练时候的那个人了；据我判断，天底下再没有哪个恶棍，比在这放松身心、难免放纵的头几个钟头里更疏于防范、更无力保全自身和家财的了——而拉夫尔斯正以他特有的先见之明，守候着这几个钟头。何况那位可怕的巴尼，在一场远谈不上不流血的胜利里吃了结结实实的苦头，就更不见得会节制杯中之物了。那么，那声叫人作呕、又引人遐想的闷响，究竟作何解？莫非在酒酣耳热之际挨上致命一击的，竟是拳王本人？下这一击的，正是拉夫尔斯这样的好手——可他在电话里的口气，又不像这个人了。

===Original===
And yet﻿—and yet﻿—what else could have happened? I must have asked myself the question between each and all of the above reflections, made partly as I dressed and partly in the hansom on the way to Half-moon Street. It was as yet the only question in my mind. You must know what your emergency is before you can decide how to cope with it; and to this day I sometimes tremble to think of the rashly direct method by which I set about obtaining the requisite information. I drove every yard of the way to the pugilist’s very door. You will remember that I had been dining with Swigger Morrison at his club.

===Chinese===
可是——可是——还能出什么事呢？上述这许多思量，一部分是穿衣时想的，一部分是在去半月街的双轮马车上想的；而在这一连串思量的间隙与末尾，我必定把这个问题问了自己一遍又一遍。它此刻还是我脑子里唯一的问题。你总得先弄清你的急难是什么，才谈得上决定如何应付；而直到今天，我一想起自己获取必要情报时那套鲁莽直露的办法，有时还不免发抖。那一趟，我是一码路也没省，径直坐车坐到这位拳师的家门口。你该记得，我刚同斯威格·莫里森在他的俱乐部里吃过晚饭。

===Original===
Yet at the last I had a rough idea of what I meant to say when the door was opened. It seemed almost probable that the tragic end of our talk over the telephone had been caused by the sudden arrival and as sudden violence of Barney Maguire. In that case I was resolved to tell him that Raffles and I had made a bet about his burglar trap, and that I had come to see who had won. I might or might not confess that Raffles had rung me out of bed to this end. If, however, I was wrong about Maguire, and he had not come home at all, then my action would depend upon the menial who answered my reckless ring. But it should result in the rescue of Raffles by hook or crook.

===Chinese===
不过到了最后，门一打开我该说什么，心里总算有了个粗略的主意。我们电话里那场谈话的悲惨收场，多半是巴尼·马圭尔骤然到家、又骤然动手所致，这似乎八九不离十。若是那样，我打定主意告诉他：拉夫尔斯和我拿他的防盗陷阱打过赌，我是来看谁输谁赢的。至于拉夫尔斯正是为此把我从床上叫起来的，这一层可以说，也可以不说。可倘若我把马圭尔料错了，他根本没回家，那么我的行止，就得看应我那记莽撞门铃的仆役是何等角色了。但无论如何，都得把拉夫尔斯救出来，不择手段。

===Original===
I had the more time to come to some decision, since I rang and rang in vain. The hall, indeed, was in darkness; but when I peeped through the letter-box I could see a faint beam of light from the back room. That was the room in which Maguire kept his trophies and set his trap. All was quiet in the house: could they have haled the intruder to Vine Street in the short twenty minutes which it had taken me to dress and to drive to the spot? That was an awful thought; but even as I hoped against hope, and rang once more, speculation and suspense were cut short in the last fashion to be foreseen.

===Chinese===
我倒因此有了更充裕的时间拿定主意，因为我按了又按门铃，始终无人应门。门厅里果然一片漆黑；可我从信箱口往里窥探时，能看见后屋透出一缕微光。那正是马圭尔存放战利品、布设陷阱的屋子。宅子里静悄悄的：会不会就在我穿衣赶路的那短短二十分钟里，他们已经把那不速之客扭送到瓦因街（Vine Street）去了？那是个可怕的念头；可正当我怀着万一之望、再按一次门铃时，猜测与悬念以一种最料想不到的方式戛然而止。

===Original===
A brougham was coming sedately down the street from Piccadilly; to my horror, it stopped behind me as I peered once more through the letter-box, and out tumbled the dishevelled prizefighter and two companions. I was nicely caught in my turn. There was a lamppost right opposite the door, and I can still see the three of them regarding me in its light. The pugilist had been at least a fine figure of a bully and a braggart when I saw him before his fight; now he had a black eye and a bloated lip, hat on the back of his head, and made-up tie under one ear. His companions were his sallow little Yankee secretary, whose name I really forget, but whom I met with Maguire at the Boxing Club, and a very grand person in a second skin of shimmering sequins.

===Chinese===
一辆布鲁厄姆马车（brougham）正从皮卡迪利方向沿着街从容驶来；我正在信箱口再度窥探，它竟在我背后停了下来，吓得我魂飞魄散——摔摔打打跳下车的是衣冠不整的拳王和两位同伴。这回轮到我被逮个正着。大门正对面立着一根路灯柱，此刻我仍能看见他们三个在灯光下打量我的模样。赛前我见到这位拳师时，他好歹还算得上一表人才的一个恶霸加牛皮大王；这会儿他一只乌青眼、一片肿嘴唇，帽子扣在后脑勺上，领结歪到一只耳朵底下。他的同伴，一个是面色灰黄的美国小个子秘书——名字我实在想不起来了，只记得是在拳击俱乐部随马圭尔一起见过——另一位，则是位十分显贵的人物，一身亮闪闪的亮片紧裹着，好比第二层皮肤。

===Original===
I can neither forget nor report the terms in which Barney Maguire asked me who I was and what I was doing there. Thanks, however, to Swigger Morrison’s hospitality, I readily reminded him of our former meeting, and of more that I only recalled as the words were in my mouth.

===Chinese===
巴尼·马圭尔问我是谁、在那里干什么时用的那些字眼，我既忘不掉，也没法照实复述。不过，多亏斯威格·莫里森的殷勤款待，我不费吹灰之力便让他想起了我们上回的会面，还想起了更多的事——那些话到了嘴边，我才记起来。

===Original===
“You’ll remember Raffles,” said I, “if you don’t remember me. You showed us your trophies the other night, and asked us both to look you up at any hour of the day or night after the fight.”

I was going on to add that I had expected to find Raffles there before me, to settle a wager that we had made about the mantrap. But the indiscretion was interrupted by Maguire himself, whose dreadful fist became a hand that gripped mine with brute fervor, while with the other he clouted me on the back.

===Chinese===
「你要是记不起我，总记得拉夫尔斯吧，」我说。「前几天晚上你还给我们看了你的战利品，还请我们两个赛后来找你，白天黑夜什么时候都行。」
